- **D305 - Sela's turn back is a reload at the turn (November 14, chapter 10; D286 built).** When Sela's ship turns back with Taren, the zone reloads at its spawn `Turn`: the party comes back as the pair the story needs whoever the player was flying, the convoy's west flank is Idra's from then on, and the reload is also the Back section's checkpoint, so a defeat after the turn costs at most that section. The cut to the epilogue is the same kind of hand-over: the party held, the plate SEVEN WEEKS ON (`TimePlate`, drawn by the screen fade), then Neighbourhood Afloat's `Arrival` (`CutAway`).
- **D306 - The anchor's release is the chamber's own, and Meret yields standing (November 14, chapter 10).** The last collar opens only if it is held through one whole ease (`LastCollar`); then `AnchorRelease` plays the moment the game has walked toward: the four clamps let go one by one and then together, the chain runs out, the chamber's cold light turns warm, the road below begins to flow, the cue plays and barks are hushed. Meret's line on seeing it open (`MeretSeesItOpen`) is played by the release itself and not as an after-talk, so the camera stays drawn back on the anchor through it. She yields upright (`YieldsStanding`): she is taken into custody, not struck down. The lift between the gantry and the well refuses until her frame is down and stands still while a conversation holds the controls (D275). The checkpoint `Gantry` is on the gangway, nearer her than the zone's `Arrival` (D302, wiring 6.5). Coordinates and the walk-through: `docs/maps/StillwaterChamber.md`.

- **D308 - Chapter 10 is played from its berths as built, and its fights' words are read off the screen (chapter 10's routes, November 14).** `tools/mooring_routes.py` writes chapter-62 to chapter-69, from Idra's plan at Many Hands' chart table (chapter9.complete) to the town after the credits (`credits.rolled`, `game.complete`). Places come from the builders' own constants (Sorrel's `Air` and `Shelf`, Last Mooring's chain runs and islands, the passage's `On` and `Half`, the Continuance's ways) and every prompt a route presses from the built scene; `MooringRouteTests` loads each zone in the state the routes walk it and judges every leg, press, dock and launch with the party docked on the berth by its own pad, and every way out of a zone (chapter 9's test had never judged one). The driver gained six hands, each a person reading the screen (`ContinuousReview.Mooring.cs`, `MooringReplayHandsTests`, each shown failing first): a dock or a launch is gone to the berth's pad or launch where it stands now (Mother of Doors' fourth shelf swings); the markings of a boss nobody strikes (Mother of Doors, the Tollkeeper, in their rooms) are rolled out of as they land, since nobody can lock them and the older rule reads only the locked boss's tells; Fire, the Pulse and the EXTRA are held under Meret's dome; an overreached arm's collar is taken at its lit seat and the pad let be; one blow into Meret's guard and nothing while she listens; on a berth with a Clamp Tender up and no Bridle clamp in the room, the lock is flicked to the tender (the Tollkeeper's motor). Mother of Doors' door step ends at the east aperture's mouth (her checkpoint faces the pillar between the apertures); Meret's ship's door is the walk to the bay's pad on foot, after her hail and a launch back in, because her checkpoint is on foot at the bay. None of it has been played in a player.
- **D309 - A press answers what the arrow points at (November 14).** Which prompt a press answers was the nearest in reach, and of two at the same distance, whichever a set happened to hold first. Five chapters' plans are heard at Many Hands' chart table (chapters 6, 8, 9 and 10 stage theirs on the city's `ChartTalk`), on the exact spot of the table's own chart; after a rebuild, chapter 10's first step opened the route chart instead of Idra's plan, and a player who had left the chart errand undone would have had the same coin toss in every one of those chapters. The objective panel now tells the prompt service what its arrow points at (`PromptService.Wanted`, set by `ObjectiveHud`), and that prompt is offered when it is in its own reach and within half a metre of being the nearest (`WantedRoom`); two still level are told apart by name, never by order. The arrow does not reach past what the hero stands at: a prompt more than half a metre nearer still wins (`WantedPromptTests`). Stagings keep the one place a player has learned to go to for a plan; nothing was moved.
- **D310 - Harrowgate's harbour is seen at dusk (November 14, the polish pass on chapters 8 and 9).** The harbour read as a harbour and as nothing else: one repeating crest pattern for water, grey stone, flat light. It is committed to dusk: a low gold sun from behind the town with blue fill and haze, lamps coming on along the piers and the carrier, lit ports and mast lights on the moored hulls, and water that varies (open chop, calm in the lee of wall and quay, wakes, foam at stone and at each hull's waterline, broken streaks of lamp light). The same pass gave the Separation Works' keel the inside of hulls being cut apart (ribs, plating, torch cuts, seven cuts in progress), the Broken Ring's districts roofs of their own (the market's canvas, the crescent's family colours round a garden court) and the Terminator its road (generations of tread ruts, cairns, shed iron). Nothing a route uses moved: routes 44, 52, 56, 58, 59 and 61 regenerate identical. Stills: `Builds/quality/look/harbour-dusk-03`, `works-keel-04`, `ring-streets-02`, `term-road-06`. Still weak: six of the Terminator's eighteen stills are over the floor-density limit, and three of the Ring's fight frames have no district in them.
- **D311 - The anchor's release is framed on the anchor (November 14, the polish pass on chapter 10).** Seen for the first time in stills written by a test (`Builds/quality/look/anchor-release-02`, seven moments from the hold to the road flowing), the release had the camera drawn back onto the control well with the anchor small at the frame's top edge. `AnchorRelease` now frames the clamps, the chain and the bow, and the clamps' jaws open to 68 degrees and stand as jaws (folded back to 92 they read as stone arches). A great shackle on the chain stands over the four clamps, so the anchor is the largest thing in the zone. No flag the release sets changed (D306). The same pass: the Continuance's floors are painted by the work done on them and about twenty people stand at stations, windows and half-eaten meals (all 37 stills now pass the floor-density check); Last Mooring's islands are kept decks and the Tollkeeper's couplings sit in sockets; Sorrel's valley ramps up to shallower apertures. Routes 62 to 69 regenerate byte for byte the same. Still weak: the chamber before the release is mostly cold blue-grey, and Sorrel's apertures are still narrow notches.
- **D312 - The Warmaster's duel is sized on the share two chains measured (November 14, chapter 8).** Drask Vaal's sheet asks 80 to 200 s for the duel, half of a two-part fight, and carried a placeholder share of .3 because no chain had fought him. Two did on November 14 at 130,000: 87 s and 76 s, the second under his floor, which is a share of about .42. He is 150,000 and his sheet says .42; `TheDuelLastsWhatItsSheetSaysForEveryPair` now reads his own sheet's share and not the class's .3 (103 to 136 s for the three pairs, 99 s on the shared measure). The Seedthane is 39,000 and the Rotbloom 61,500 for the same reason from the other side: the shared measure's 20 s sample read either anywhere inside a quarter of itself in one day, so it samples 60 s and says Inconclusive under 50 frames a second, and both bars were set to sit inside their bounds on that steadier number (92 s and 134 s). `tools/boss_durations.py` still prints every fight against the plan's 120 to 300 s: a boss with a sheet of its own (the Seedthane, the Hulljack, Dessik Varn, Vessa Korl, the Warmaster and his skiff) is held to its sheet, not to that line.
- **D315 - A walk's way to a door it requires is declared, kept clear, and felt by a hero's body with every story standing (November 14, chapter 9's Coronach).** The first chain to play the Coronach (route 60) stopped against Jask on the dock office's front: four doors stand off the Decks' ways, the doors' prompts had pushed him 4.4 m off the stair he stands for onto the only floor between the office's crated benches and its open front, and every check passed because the route tests loaded the walk with no side story done, so he was never there. Now the walk declares its own ways to those four doors (`HomeBuilder.Coronach.DoorWays`), its people are kept 1.7 m off them as off the Decks' (the build stops, `CORONACH_DOOR_WAY`, if one doesn't leave a Decks way or end inside its door's reach on clear floor), Jask stands by the stair at (-36.7, 8.0), the route walks the declared ways, the generator keeps every leg a body clear of the walk's people and things as built (`home_routes.coronach_solids`), and `HomeRouteTests` loads the walk with every side story done and feels every chapter 9 leg on foot with the hero's own capsule (`EveryWalkOnFootEndsWhereAHeroHasRoomAndMeetsNothingSolidOnTheWay`), as well as asking the baked floor.
- **D313 - A body that cannot be struck from the floor is put back on it (November 14).** The Frozen Host's third rank would not clear in the player, twice: both Bridle-hounds of the fight had ended in the 1.2 to 1.8 m gap between Rig three's back and the north wall, one standing on the other's back, with the rig's corner between them and a lead whose every blow the cover check refused; they never leapt because they could not see him. At the source the gap is filled (cut-ice packs behind Rig three and the matching wedge at Rig one, `HoarfellBuilder.FrozenHost.cs`, and `NoThawRigLeavesAGapAHoundFitsInAndAHeroCantFightIn` measures every rig). In general (`EnemyBrain.KeepToTheFloor`, every grounded enemy but bosses, mines, serpents and passive ones): a body standing on no baked floor (another's back, a gap, inside something solid) or on floor not joined to its own or the lead's (a prop's top: an audit found 124 such tops across the Frozen Host, the Bridle Foundry, the Separation Works and the Terminator, none of which can be walked onto) is moved at once to the nearest joined floor with room for it. It never lifts a body, except up to 2 m out of a solid it has sunk into; leaps and throws pass the same check. `ENEMY_PUT_ON_FLOOR` is logged when a body is moved and `ENEMY_OFF_FLOOR` once when no floor is found.
- **D314 - A test that fells a boss to check what follows fells it the way its fight ends, and a check that walks every boss is one case per boss (November 14).** Three shared checks (the boss track gives the zone's back, no way out answers in a boss fight, every boss door is a checkpoint) walked every boss in one case and felled each with one great blow. A bar that holds at a line (the Last Warden's clamps) or takes nothing while it fights (the Foundry Saint) stopped the case there, so one held bar hid every boss after it for two days. The helper `Fell` lifts a boss's guards the way its own fight's end does and then asserts it is down, and each check is one case per boss ("zone/boss"). What that uncovered: the Mother of Doors goes in silence by design (her case requires the silence, then her zone's track); Driftreed's MIRE GATE sign stood 9.3 m from its arch against the rule's 7 (it is 5 m off now, on the camera's side); and chapter 2's panel after the homecoming names Keeper Abri with its arrow on her (D272 removed "Chapter 2 complete" on purpose, and the test now checks what D272 put there).
- **D316 - The Foundry Saint's frame is solid between its legs, and nothing that comes down at its fall keeps a body (November 14, route 59).** Its capsule left a pocket inside the drawn press behind the middle collars. In the player a hero's Extra carried him into it at 22 s; two fallen arms' high ends (a metre clear of the floor: too low to walk under, too high to step onto) closed it a second later, he stood shut in for the rest of the fight while his partner won it alone, and the settled press's box then wedged him under an arm, unable to walk to the lift, with the autosave just written. The fight now stands the whole drawn frame solid (10.1 x 12 x 4.9 on P, the settled box's own footprint). An arm coming to rest, or the settling press, steps anyone it would hold (on it, under its high end, touching where it lands, inside it) to the nearest clear floor on the camera's side; the old rule moved only those standing on an arm's footprint. Fallen arms and the press are cut out of the walking paths, so a partner and a walk go round them. Chosen over only clearing the pocket as arms fall: that would still let a hero walk into the drawn model. `FoundrySaintFallTests` (five of its six cases fail on the old behaviour, one of them at the very spot route 59 left him).
- **D317 - The Mother of Doors is retried from the east aperture (November 14, chapter 10).** Her checkpoint `Clamps` stood at the valley's head, 70 m from her door and facing the pillar, so every retry cost a 70 m flight (`EveryBossDoorIsACheckpoint` asks under 60). It stands at (7.45, 245) now, 57.5 m from her door: short of Dace's crossing and of her fight's box, a straight flight from the crater, both ships between the aperture walls. She is still shown from the valley's head on the way in, and her fight (D292) is unchanged.
- **D318 - A chapter 10 boss fight holds the way out (November 14).** The one-case-per-boss split (D314) showed three bosses with no seal on the door and no way out that answered in the fight: Meret's ship, the Mother of Doors and the Tollkeeper. A flight boss is fought far from every way out, and a warp refuses only within a boss fight's reach (D198), so a ship could fly back down the valley and warp home mid-fight with nothing to say it was leaving one. From the moment the door's fight begins until the boss falls, Sorrel's Second Sky's and Last Mooring's warps home refuse and name the boss (`FightHoldsTheWay`); before the fight and after it they are open as built. In the Continuance, Meret's door shuts the works' north door, the bay's only way back into the ship, and the hull berth counts as her fight's own, so its pad takes a party that launched mid-fight back up into it (it could not dock again until she fell). The Tollkeeper's arm counted as run out at .999 of the way while its line, bark and sound waited for 1.0: one measure now. Chapter 10's route test judged every leg first and every press after, so each press saw flags the route sets later; it judges in route order now. A place's name at the route chart's edge is moved inward whole, still over its lantern, rather than set smaller or wrapped.
- **D319 - The Debris Cutter is flown after, and sized on what that measures (November 14, chapter 9).** Route 61's first plays left 60 percent of 123,000 after sixteen rounds, and a longer scouting run lost the party once and won in 658 s. The fight's steps held the brake (2.1 m/s in flight) and fired only inside 5 m, at a boss that keeps 18 m off and changes lane at 13 m/s: the lead fired 7 percent of the time, never left one quadrant, and the partner never came within reach. The fault was the driver's, not the fight's: the hull takes a blow at any time, and shots do not weaken with range (the route's note that they did was wrong). With the Cutter locked in flight the driver flies at it with the brake off beyond 11 m, brakes inside that with the nose on it, and holds Fire within 28 m (`CutterHeading`, `TheCutterIsFlownAfterAndFiredOnWhereverItKeepsItsDistance`: without the hands, 0.04 to 0.14 of flat out over four runs). Fought that way the arrival pair gets 0.35 to 0.46 of flat out: the sheet says 0.35 and the Cutter is 155,000, about two and a half to three minutes. The leg past the second pair's cover crossed the channel at full speed and ran on into the wreck: it crosses to the world engineer's line at x 11 and brakes before turning north. Not settled: Taren with Sela falls it in about 95 s, and no bar fixes that inside the shared quarter-of-flat-out ceiling (about 161,000).
- **D320 - Meret is played through the pad and sized on what it measures (November 15, chapter 10).** Her three fights were green in their own tests and had never been played end to end. Through the driver's pad with routes 66 and 67's own steps (`MeretReplayTests`): her craft kept 62 percent of its bar after fourteen rounds, the lead scraping a shelter wing with the wing between them, and every shot her shield gave back had been fired while it was down and was still in the air when it rose; the gantry ended in 61 s against a floor of 80, each opened coupling dropping her bar to its line so that two thirds went with no blow; the well ended in 70 s with her stunned a quarter of the time and the companion clamped five times for standing still; and a break during her guard or her question left the guard up for good, her plate turning every blow while she kept striking, until the party fell. Now: shots fired through her shield's gap burn out, the shield stays down 4.2 s and up at most 8 s; a coupling is offered only when her bar is held at its line, and opening it takes nothing off the bar; the gantry and the well take part of a blow outside her openings; a move cut short clears her guard, and she cannot be broken through her guard or her question; the still-trap marks only the hero the player leads; the lift says the cage is on its way back up instead of saying nothing for 14 s; the chamber's bed eases from .337 to .101 with the warm light at the release, and a reload after it starts there. Bars 44,000, 110,000 and 140,000 at shares of .17, .24 and .29 (gantry 118 to 130 s, well 93 to 116 s through the pad). The driver's hands are `ContinuousReview.Meret.cs`.
- **D321 - Chapter 10's first two bosses are fought through the driver's pad at their doors, and sized on it (November 15).** The Mother of Doors' first play in the player was three defeats with her bar untouched. `MooringBossReplayTests` plays routes 63 and 65 from their doors. The driver held her clamps from a spot inside the works' face (chapter 7's stand, which on Sorrel's shelves is 5 cm from the seat), pushing into the works while the press was offered and never pressing; with clamp 1 shut nothing else woke, and her running wave hit both ships every 18 s, the driver and the companion each meeting it only on the frame it struck. Her wave now says that it runs (`Telegraph.Spreads`, `Reaches`) and both roll through it; her fourth shelf stands still with the party docked on it (its deck slid 10 m); a fight berth's launch waits 0.8 s after the lead last struck (a surge shoved him into the launch's reach and his next blow launched the party mid-stage); no pad is offered while a clamp's guns are awake within 15 m, as built. Clamp guns are 3,300 (were 2,200); the Tollkeeper's braces 19,000 and motor stages 22,000 (it fell in 101 s at half that). Through the pad: the Mother of Doors 140 to 152 s, the Tollkeeper 139 to 160 s, nobody down. Sheet shares .26 and .2, measured against a 60 s flat-out at mastery 5 (against the shared `BOSS_RATE` they are about .36 and .32). The Tollkeeper's yard length test is judged on its fight as played through the pad, with the same 120 to 300 s bounds, rather than on a count of blows at one over the share. The driver's hands are `ContinuousReview.Mother.cs` and `ContinuousReview.Tollkeeper.cs`.
- **D322 - The Mother of Doors' shelves take the party only in their turn (November 15, chapter 10).** Order is the fight's own rule (each clamp's guns wait on the one before). From her door on, a shelf out of turn still offers its pad, the way a locked warp still says what opens it, with words in place of "Dock": a later shelf says "The west clamp's works first", one whose clamp is already open "The south-west clamp's works next"; pressing it docks nobody. Docked out of turn, a party had stood on a shelf with nothing to do and no word why (`BridleClamp.inOrder`, `Berth.OutOfTurn`, `APadOutOfHerTurnSaysWhichShelfIsNextAndTakesNobody`).
- **D323 - In flight the companion reads every enemy's marking (November 15).** In every flight fight she rolled from incoming shots but left only the areas a boss had marked, and flew into all the others: flak rows, lunges, death blasts. A clamp gun's rows hit Idra four times in each Mother of Doors fight. `PartnerBrain` treats any enemy's marking in flight as it does a boss's (`InFlightTheCompanionLeavesAnOrdinaryEnemysMarkedRowBeforeItBursts`); on foot she reads as before. Through the pad the Mother of Doors' whole fight now costs Idra 18 percent of her bar, down from 42 to 65.
- **D324 - Meret's craft is fought at the player's rhythm, and her braced frame says why a blow glances (November 15, chapter 10).** Played through the pad after D320, her craft had the lead acting a third of the fight (33 to 37 percent: holding fire under her dome) and ran 80 to 170 s on what her rolled lance chose. Two shield relays now stand in front of her shelter wings, each with a beam into the dome: while the dome is up a relay is locked and shot (shots at a relay are never given back), shooting one through drops the dome for her lance's time, and it comes back after 12 s. Her lance is no longer rolled: it answers only a dome left standing 8 s. Each window gives up at most a twelfth of her bar. The bar says which it is (`HER SHIELD IS UP · SHOOT A RELAY`, `HER SHIELD IS COMING BACK`), and the first shot she returns posts a notice that explains the relays. Through the pad the lead now acts 89 to 95 percent of the fight, the longest stretch with no blow landing is 3 to 4 s, and three fights ran 101, 108 and 118 s. On the gantry a glanced blow gives a spark and a ring of metal, the first one posts `HER FRAME TURNS A BLOW` with what opens her and the lead says a line (`bossbraced.MeretGantry`), and the bar reads `HER ARMS BRACE THE FRAME · STRIKE WHEN IT OPENS` or `THE FRAME IS OPEN · STRIKE`. The crews' wait before the release was never empty (Tovra's three radio lines at 1.6, 5.8 and 10 s, the sequence at 13.5 s: the longest quiet is 1.6 s, held by `BeforeTheReleaseTheCrewsArrivalNeverLeavesMoreThanFourSecondsWithNothingInIt`). Bars 40,000, 104,000 and 140,000 at shares .15, .2 and .29. Not settled: the well runs 84 to 116 s and is pinned against the quarter-of-flat-out ceiling; the relays are code-drawn and had not been seen in a still when this was written.
- **D325 - The replay wears the best it holds (November 15; D312's bars superseded).** The driver wore only what fell into an empty slot: it reached chapter 10 at level 27 in a Refined Edge and Scrap parts with Choirwrought ones in the bag and 18,315 scrip unspent, so every boss time read off a chain in chapters 3 to 10 was a party's below the floor the tests size against (`DevLoadout`, D257), and two bars had been moved on those times. Every route after the Gear drill (chapter 5's, which drives the real page and stays as it is) now says `wearBest`: when a route begins, and whenever no awake enemy is within 30 m and no talk is open, each hero out wears the best spare part for each slot by the five numbers the Gear page shows, added (so a +5 Tempered Edge at 98 is kept over a plain Choirwrought at 95); weapons go first to the hero who fights with them; parts go on through the page's own equip call; nothing comes off another hero, nothing is bought or upgraded; each change is logged `QUALITY_WEARS` (`ContinuousReview.Gear.cs`, `ReplayGear.cs`, `WearBestTests`; the route lint requires the key from chapter 6 and refuses it before). Fought at their real doors through the pad by the floor party: Drask Vaal fell in 84 to 85 s at 150,000 (his duel test's arrival pair at 77 s, under his 80 s floor) and is 165,000 at a share of .41 (92, 97, 110 s); the Host Marshal fell in 107 to 122 s at 115,000 and is 125,000 at .49 (130, 135, 142 s; his slowest pair reads 266 to 280 s against the 300 s ceiling, so he can go no higher); the Slag Colossus is 94,000 (211, 185 and 195 s for its three pairs). Owed: the routes buy nothing but gels and one Refined Emitter, and without buying a player is short of the floor in chapters 3, 4, 7 and 10; the stalls come next.
- **D326 - The replay buys the floor parts it lacks at the chapter's stall (November 15).** A player with scrip buys what the stalls sell; the routes bought gels and one Refined Emitter. The gel "stock" step now takes a part's row and a count: it buys until the pair out holds that many (a resting hero's copies do not count, a dropped copy does) and fails, naming the scrip, if the stall says no. Route 16 buys the Refined set chapter 3 is sized against at Corra's (two Frames, two Drives, a Module: 1,240 of about 2,100 in hand), route 21 a Prime Module from Quill through his third answer (640 of about 3,300), route 39 two Tempered Modules from Lume (2,080 of about 7,900); gels are bought first wherever a visit has them, and `wearBest` (D325) puts each part on as the stall closes. A hero's second module slot is filled only after every hero out has a first. `PartPurchaseTests` plays each stall's real shop screen with the route's own steps. Found by doing it: no stall sells a Choirwrought Module or Edge before Neighbourhood Afloat, after the last boss, though the tests size chapter 10 on Choirwrought parts and the party holds about 14,400 scrip with nothing to spend it on; and Idra joins before the Host Marshal with no Emitter and no Module and no stall between.
- **D327 - Chain C's clamp guns hang on its south face (November 15, chapter 10).** Route 64 stopped twice in the player at Last Mooring's gap: the lead held fire on the west gun from 7 m for two minutes and nothing took damage. The gap's two clamp guns stood on chain C's own line, each 2 m body wholly inside the chain's 5 m solid, and the chain stops every shot: nobody anywhere could break them. A player saw flak rows cross the gap on the beat and shots sparking off the chain, and could slip through on the quiet beat, but "break them", which the guns' design offers and the route waits for, was impossible. At (-35, 267) and (-7, 267) they hang on the side the party comes from with 2.5 m of each body clear of the chain, and still cross their curtains over the gap. In chapter 10's berth zones the replay driver flies at a fight's body beyond the 30 m lock with the brake off (the east gun stood 33 m off once the west one fell, and a braked ship with nothing locked only waited). `Route64sLaneIsFlownThroughThePadAndRoute65GoesOnFromItsSaveIntoTheTollkeepersFight` plays the whole lane and on into the Tollkeeper's fight from the save the lane leaves.
- **D328 - The hero she follows has right of way, and a body against a wall keeps only its stick's share (November 15).** Three plain walks of one play stopped (`nov14-recon35`, routes 42, 55 and 56): the lead faced his point and travelled elsewhere at full pace, once off the highest island of the Skyfields, then stood pushing. Two faults together. The companion's place is 3 m behind the lead and 2 m to his right, so a lead who turns back past her (off a talk or a load) met her head on, her way to her place running straight through him. And since D197 (November 4) the motor turns its heading a step each frame, and a wall or a body took that step back off every frame, so a body in contact kept the heading the contact gave it at full pace, up to 150 degrees from its stick: the two pressed into each other and slid off together. Now she steps off his way to her own side and waits until he has passed, or walks on ahead where there is no room aside (`PartnerBrain.GivesWay`; taken out by D354, where the lead passes through her); and after a side contact only the stick's share along the slide carries on, with the plain blend from before D197 against a wall (`GroundMotor.SlideAlong`: 5.29 m of wall slide became 0.09 m). Not new on the build it was found on: the same slides are in two earlier plays that came free by chance. `GivingWayTests` walks each of the three routes from nine of her standing places (old: 2, 1 and 1 of 9 arrive; now 27 of 27, never touching). `FightPocket.OnDisable` no longer hands a zone back to heroes already destroyed at a scene unload (`FightPocketUnloadTests`).
- **D329 - Choirwrought before the finale, and a hero who joins comes dressed (November 15).** The tests size chapter 10 on Choirwrought parts "from chapter 9's stalls" (`DevLoadout`), and no stall sold a Choirwrought Module or Edge until Neighbourhood Afloat, after the last boss: a player reached the end with about 14,400 scrip and nothing to spend it on. Mira on the Decks sells the plain set once the Decks have voted to go (`home.meetingHeld`; her page says THE ROAD CRATES ARE OPEN · CHOIRWROUGHT), and Corra at Many Hands from the convoy's arrival (`chapter9.complete`; OFF THE DECKS' CONVOY · CHOIRWROUGHT), each on the line where a seller's price note sits (`ShopInventory.noteFlag`, `note`). Prices are the ladder's and Neighbourhood Afloat's: Emitter 1,480, Edge 1,560, Frame 1,620, Drive 1,420, Module 1,600 (a set 7,680, or 6,528 at Corra's kettle discount); the saves show about 11,650 scrip on the Decks after the meeting and 14,365 at route 62. Route 62 stops at Corra's and buys the Edge and Module the pair out lacks. Idra joined in chapter 6 with no Emitter and no Module and no stall before the Host Marshal: a hero who joins, or rejoins after chapters away, is brought up to the floor of the chapter she joins in (Idra a Prime Module and her off-hand Emitter a tier behind, as the test loadout dresses her; Sela at her turn back in chapter 10, where she had returned in what she wore in chapter 6), once for each chapter, marked by a `floor.<hero>.<chapter>` flag (`Lattice.Rpg.PartyFloor`, which is also where test fixtures now dress from): every slot that is empty or holds a part worse than the floor's plain part by the Gear page's numbers gets that part, what she wore goes to the bag, a better part stays on; an older save is topped up on load in the slots that need it, and a slot emptied later stays empty. `JoinKitTests`, `PartPurchaseTests`.
- **D330 - In flight the companion leaves a marking clear of anything large (November 20).** D323's way out of a marking came from the marking alone, and beside a carrier it crossed the carrier: over 24 repeats of `CompanionTravelTests`' live-carrier case on the old brain Taren crossed the Cantor's drawn body in 8 of 12 and Sela in 1 of 12 (the Cantor's volley cone is laid by its `EnemyBrain`, so she had only read it in flight since D323; her first way out was refused for the roll because the lead's hull was in it, and the roll's next choice, the opposite way, bent toward the head). The ninth was older: Ember Step dashed 10 m through a link, the dash's check looking only at cover; the same dash, three seconds in, is what made `TarenRegroupsAroundTheWholeAnimal` fail in two suites (it left her on the lead's side with nothing to regroup round: not the machine's load, 2 runs in 6 on an idle one). With a large body near (drawn more than 2.5 m from its middle) her way out keeps her hull plus 0.8 m off its drawn parts and the next 0.6 s of its motion, the margin her travel keeps, and a roll keeps clear of party hulls: the marking's own way if it is clear, else the shortest clear way, else the roomiest, rolled at once (`FlightBodies.cs`). A flight dash is refused through the Cantor (`FlightSeparation.PathClear`). `LeavesARowBesideTheCarrierTheLongWayRound` (10 of 10 failing before, passing after); the class 12 of 12 in five runs of five, worst clearance on the live carrier 1.89 to 2.70 m. D323's gains hold: through the pad the Mother of Doors' fight costs Idra 14 percent of her bar.
- **D331 - A boss length read near a bound is read twice; the Bellows 33,500, the Debris Cutter 159,000 (November 20).** The whole suite on the final tree (`nov20a`: 3,546 PlayMode cases, 3 failed) failed on three measures, none on the game. One minute of a party flat out is not one number: over the suites of November 14, 15 and 20 the same boss on the same bar read 5 to 9% apart (the Rotbloom 1,082 and 982 a second, the Host Marshal 1,915 and 1,772). The shared length test now reads a second minute and averages when the first lands within 8% of either bound (`QaPlanCombatTests.Scatter`; same bounds, and its line says both readings, the bar and the quarter-of-flat-out time). The Bellows had drifted onto its ceiling for real: 487 to 530 a second until November 15, 464 to 487 over seven readings on the final tree, 301 s at a quarter of that; it is 33,500 (35,000 before; 288 s at a quarter, about 2 min 50 s for the replay party; the asset edited by hand, since Hushwell's builder rebuilds the whole cave). The Debris Cutter's pairs were measured for fifteen seconds, which read Taren and Sela anywhere from 3,264 to 4,144 a second: the pair test now takes a minute a pair as the shared test does (two readings within 0.4% of each other) and reads twice inside 6% of a bound. Over a minute the fastest pair is Sela and Idra (3,898 a second), not Taren and Sela (3,641), and the slowest Taren and Idra (2,255): the bar's room is 154,200 to 165,200, and 155,000 sat on its floor, so it is 159,000 (124 s for the fastest pair at the sheet's share, 289 s for the slowest at a quarter, 208 s for the arrival pair). `FoundrySaintFallTests`' third fall failed three times of three on that tree and not for the fall: Taren, walked first, stops 2.4 m short of the lift's stand on the line Idra then walks, and since D328 a partner does not push through the lead, so she stood 0.42 m behind him and was counted stuck; up against a hero who is himself there she has arrived. All of it green in a mirror (`lead-nov20-len1`, `lead-nov20-len2`: the 22 length cases, the Cutter's pairs, the six falls, every case that names the Bellows).
- **D332 - Barge three's last skimmer comes round, and the driver finds a line (November 20, chapter 3).** Route 17 stopped twice at 'Barge3_Skimmers cleared' (November 15 and the whole-game scout of November 20, `nov20-recon40/s17`; D-less fix of November 15, flying at a fight's body beyond the lock, did not touch it). Through the pad two plays in six stopped the same way: the last Podskimmer, circling the lead with the hull between them, was pushed along its ring and in toward him at once, and held where that push met the hull square, never seeing him; the lead's lock sat on it 11.8 m off, inside the step's 12, and 430 shots went into the hull in 88 s. A strafer held against a solid out of its quarry's sight for 0.4 s now goes the other way round, and breaks away into the open if held again within 4 s (the Lancer and the Veil Ray keep theirs). In flight the driver flicks the lock from a body behind cover to one in the open when its line has been blocked a second, else flies brake off to a line; a walk that stalls or a chase that stalls or scrapes with something solid in the way flies a two-leg way round it (November 15's run ended with Sela ground against the hull, forty scrapes at 12 each). Neither in boss fights (`ContinuousReview.Cover.cs`, `FlightMotor.LastScrape`, `BargeSkimmerReplayTests`: six cases shown failing first; neighbours 108 of 108 and 123 of 123). Left open: a freed barge drifts through the flank knot's spot and costs 12 a contact; a partner left behind at barge two.
- **D339 - In flight the partner's steering never shuts every way at once, and follows the lead's own way back (November 21).** The whole-game capture of November 20 had the partner's ship sit still for the rest of a zone twice (nov20-whole s17, s54). In Xylos' nearspace Sela's hull came to rest 6 cm off barge two's loose pane "-20 -16" in its skimmers' second round, inside the 0.3 m her steering's sweeps keep past her hull (`FlightObstacles`). A sweep that starts overlapping a solid meets it at nought whichever way it goes, so all sixteen ways read shut, the plan was to stay put, and she sat there to the end of the zone: hull untouched, no scrape logged, still firing at what came near. Flown through the pad from the weather mirror it happened by itself in one play of two, 12 cm from the capture's place. Each plan now sweeps with the widest margin (0.3, 0.12, 0.03 m) that is clear where she sits. The second trap is what keeps a partner away once she is behind: steering had only the straight line and one corner, so with the lead round a bend she stopped at the first solid on the line to him (left 170 m behind round Nacre Grave's inner pass, Idra stopped at the inner wall for good). `PartnerBrain` now keeps the lead's way in flight (a point every 2.5 m, the last 240 m). A goal more than 20 m off is sought along that way first, to the newest point she can reach straight, unbraked; a nearer one is steered as before (one braked corner, else stop short): taken along his way, a fight's target behind the Gullet's fold sent her to his last point and back without a shot (`CompanionWallTests`). Nacre's own stop is not reproduced: Idra held still with her thrust on from 53 s at the inner tows and went idle at 60 s, in open water with nothing solid within 4.5 m of her hull. Seven plays through the pad and five staged at the stills' place, all on the old steering, never held her. The replay's frames.csv now carries the partner's place (`partnerX`, `partnerZ`). `PartnerFlightRejoinTests`. *Amended the same evening (the scout nov21-recon1, route 45, failed twice at 'leg 10 round the rock 1'):* following the lead's way at boost, Idra came into the Picket's pocket 9 at 27 m/s, ran six metres past her place sideways and sat on leg 10's line as he set off; he pressed on her hull and the driver went round (the driver reads only the lead's own scrapes; hers set nothing off). A ship now brakes to arrive (from her closing speed) and for a turn it can't make above 15 m/s; on her way to her place while he waits she goes round him, on the open side, not through him (the pocket's nose turn, D279, still brings her round behind it). While he flies toward her she follows to her place by him as ever, only without going round him, and with his hull about to meet hers she slides across his line, just far enough to clear it (a version that held her ground instead left her idle 18 m off after a fight while he closed at a regroup's braked pace, nov22-recon10 s10): the evening's first version also stepped off the line his nose pointed down and went round him as he came, and a lead flying up to his partner for route 10's regroup found her ten metres off for all eight seconds, three captures in three (nov21-whole s10 to s10c). None of it acts over an animal's own guide (FlightSeparation). (`PartnerBrain.Arriving`, `ShipRoundTheLead`, `LeadComing`, whose slide D354 took out, where the lead passes through her; `PartnerFlightRejoinTests`: route 45 from leg 9 through the pad, never ahead of him within both hulls of his line on a leg; the lead flown at her from 11 and 18 m in the Gullet and in open flight closes to 3.8 m.)
- **D340 - The menus' lists stand in columns, and a row is found by its key (November 21).** The whole-game capture of November 20 showed Mira's stall as a debug list: every row one centred string ("Scrap Emitter · Output +8 — 60 scrip · slot empty"), the page toggle, the pager and Back among the goods, the title off over the list with an empty block above her; the quest log's "Three Baskets" step ran under its row's border and a description onto the footer; the bench's costs read as fractions ("Scrap Alloy 45/6"); the chart's question covered the map's names. The shop, Gear, Party, Fabricate and the quest log now share one list (`MenuList`): a kind mark (a part's slot picture, a line drawn in code for each consumable kind and for materials, `ItemMarks`), cells in columns under headings, each cell giving way to a leaner wording rather than running out, and the strip about the row under the cursor in full (a seller's stall puts it over her, with the thing's name and mark). The shop's toggle is a tab strip naming both pages, the pager and Back are controls at the panel's foot, and the stall's name heads the panel from its left edge. A row's one string used to be what the replay driver and the checks read; a column row carries `ListRow.key`, which starts as the string did (the thing's name, a worn part's wearer in front of it), and `ContinuousReview.OnRow` reads that, so every route's `pickRow` finds the row it always found and no route was regenerated. Tests read a row's columns through `ListRow.Words` (its cells, "  ·  " between) and `ListRow.Cell`. Costs on the bench and on upgrades say "Scrap Alloy ×6 (have 45)"; the bench's levels say "Edges 1 (10 of 30)". `MenuLayoutTests` measures every stall, part, recipe and quest the definitions hold against its box and its column; seven of its eight checks failed on the screens the capture saw.
- **D341 - A struck body keeps its form, a particle is round, and what the game makes while it runs is what the build carries (November 21, effects).** The whole-game capture's large flat white shapes were three faults, and none of them a stripped variant or a missing texture: each draws the same in the editor, where no still ever runs a particle or a fight. (1) The Frozen Host's vent steam (s35/096, 097) and seven other particle materials in four zones (FrozenHost Steam, Coupling steam, Spindrift; BridleFoundry LitSmoke, Steam, Smoke; DrownedWaystation Mist; SeparationWorks Haze) drew `ZoneKit.Glow(fromFloor)`'s light-curtain texture, which is solid to its sides: each puff was a rectangle, and ninety additive ones stacked into stair-stepped white. `ZoneKit.Drift` now gives such a material the round `puff_soft.png` (`ParticleSprites`), and `ParticleSprites.RoundAll` did it once for the built scenes. Round, the vent columns were still a flat white plume: ninety additive puffs at .36 in a box under two metres wide, and with no tonemapper a little added light over snow is already white. A strand's glow made into puffs is now vapour (drawn over what is behind it, under white; eleven materials, `PARTICLE_VAPOUR`), and `Drift` thins vapour that stacks over .6 on a line of sight to .6 (the vents: .36 to .15, at the Frozen Host's next rebuild). Light (flames, embers) is never thinned: a first version did thin additive flames, in the Harrowgate builds of 06:38 to 07:28. (2) The Bellows falling (s08/086) and Mother of Doors at clamp 4 (s63/149) were the hit flash: `_HitFlash` lerped every pixel of a body to its colour, up to nine tenths of the way at HDR 1.25 with no tonemapper (flat white), and `HitFlash` held it at full through the whole stop (a boss going down holds 250 ms). Measured from the capture's video, a body showed a flash in 6 to 12% of a boss fight's frames (the Bellows 6.1, Dessik Varn 6.8, the Host Marshal 9.8, the Burrower 12.3), 44 to 79 flashes a minute, .08 to .09 s each on average and up to .47 s. The flash now lifts the body's light a little and burns along its silhouette (`LatticeToon`), runs on the blow's own .09 s through any stop, and a boss takes .6 of it. (3) The guardian plate's strip shared vertices between its two faces, its normals summed to nothing and the energy-bubble shader drew it as a flat slab of lime (s46/047): it is `Lattice/ShieldPlate`. The Fibre Wisp was drawn all along, as ten fifth-of-a-metre motes of `flare_01`, a spark whose bright core is under 1% of its square: one to three pixels each (s39/153); a swarm's motes are now beads of their own. The hurt and Flash edges are rims (the corners and the screen's very edge), and the hurt one clears in .3 s. Rules: a particle's texture comes to nothing on every edge; `flare_01` is a spark, never a body or a puff; `EffectsBuildTests` holds every particle the build draws to a round sprite, every `Shader.Find` and `Resources.Load` name in the game's scripts to something the build carries, and every material made while the game runs to a shader, keyword set and textures the build carries. Each was shown failing on the old state (`EffectsLookTests`, `EffectsBuildTests`, run `effects-before`). *Then (same day): a beaten boss's body ends as what it is.* What leaves (`BossDeparture`) walks out on its feet, bowed: its rig no longer plays Down (Vessa Korl lay sprawled with a leg in the air, s48/232), and someone on a humanoid rig neither sags through the floor nor sinks under it unless its way out is a drain. What shuts down stops where it stands (a humanoid's rig freezes, bowed; a rigid body bows five degrees); what is huge (4.5 m tall or 2.2 m round) and destroyed wilts where it stands to four tenths of its height (the Rotbloom stood whole with the party inside it, s26/457); only an ordinary body still tips over. A break never reaches the defeat roll (it runs on death alone): the Last Warden's 40 degrees was the rig's shared Stagger frame (D334). Combat steam is soft puffs that rise, lit warm at their birth, where it was fourteen of the flare's pinpoints (the grey sparkle round the Slag Colossus, s49/046). Swarms' motes are half-metre beads with a dark edge and a shadow under the mass.
- **D342 - Meret wears her portrait's head in her emergency frame; two generated bodies are regraded on their own UVs (November 21).** In the whole-game capture Meret Venn fought the last duel as a pale figure with no face or hair (s67/116). An editor render from that still's own camera (`LastMooringBuilder.EndingLook`, `Builds/quality/look/ch10-before/well-116.png`) drew her the same, so nothing failed to reach the player: Meshy's repaint of her body for the frame (`MeretWell`, a C17 retexture on her body's original UVs) had painted her silver fronds a smooth white cap, her face a dark slate with gold eyes and her boots white to the knee, and from the fixed camera her head, coat and legs were one white. Her two bodies are one mesh on one UV layout, so `tools/meret_grade.py` gives the frame her ordinary body's head and neck (the portrait's lavender-grey face and grooved silver fronds; the frame's dark collar kept), brushed-slate boots with their gold trim, a coat a little under paper white, and the frame's cyan lights in its emission. `tools/orrin_grade.py` does the same for Orrin, whom the reviewers took for a stranger under his own name (s69/014): his portrait's silver fronds and his reference's charcoal trousers, where Meshy had painted slate and tan. Both classify each triangle by where it is on the body in its bind pose (`tools/blender/dump_uv_triangles.py`), keep their source beside it as `…_ungraded.png` and read from that, and write art-src's texture and the staged one; a later intake `prepare` of either undoes it (run the tool again after one). No Meshy credits were spent.

- **D343 - What the camera's sphere is kept clear of, a dead boss's after-talk, and the mire's water in the player (November 21, chapters 3 and 4).** From the whole-game capture of November 20. (1) *The camera's sphere, not only its line.* The ground camera slides in wherever a .4 m sphere cast from the hero's chest back along its line meets rendered scenery (`CameraRig.GroundDistance`) and dives to its nearest. The Drowned Waystation's masonry on the camera's side of a floor was kept under the line (D119) as found by half-metre steps, up to half a metre late (so up to .41 m over it), and only along the one line through each point: at the back of the lower gallery's west alcove a hero stood .41 m beside a corner of full-height wall, the sphere met it .55 m out, and the lens went into the slope and showed space through the back of the ground (s26 301 to 306). Each point's floor is now found by marching the distance to the nearest floor (to 2 cm), and every point within half a metre either side of a line to a floor is held to it. Modelled over every place a hero can stand against a station wall: 42 such places dived the camera before, none after; no walkable slope leads out of a floor either way. `WaystationTests.TheCamerasSphereClearsTheStationsWallsFromEveryPlaceAHeroStandsAgainstThem` casts it in the built scene. The Walking Canopy (`CanopyHeight`) and the Works (`HomeBuilder.Works.Height`) give their bodies' walls the same half-metre rule and have not been checked for the sphere. (2) *A talk after a boss waits for the body.* A boss's body falls, lies and goes in 3.6 s on the game's clock (`DefeatPresentation`), and a conversation stops that clock: the Rotbloom's after-talk at 3 s froze it on its side with the heroes in its petals for every line. It waits 4.6 s (`WaystationTests.TheRotbloomsBodyIsGoneBeforeTheHallsTalkAfterItOpens`). The fall itself is still the shared 42 degree roll; a bloom that wilts would be `DefeatPresentation`'s. (3) *The licensed water is baked for Wendmire.* Driftreed's and the Waystation's water sheets carry a `BiomePatch`, but `LocalBiomeBake` baked only chapters 1 and 2 and Rootmarket, so the player drew them with the public toon water (navy camouflage). Both zones are in its list and its restage step, with a `Mire` palette of their own. (4) *The Floodcrown's seal* is drawn and stands at z 29.6, not 31.8: its 8 m body reaches 2 m past its capsule, and run aground at the south rim it stood in the roots (`XylosWorldTests.TheBasinSealStandsBeyondTheFloodcrownsReachFromItsBasin`).
- **D344 - A flight zone's haze is measured from the plane; a big flier keeps ships out of its drawn hull; a tow comes under and leaves away from the ships (November 21, chapters 9 and 10).** Seen in the whole-game capture of November 20. *The haze:* Sorrel's second sky's linear fog (70 to 330 m) was tuned for the flight camera's 26 m; from the valley's head her door frames the party and Mother of Doors from 314 m, and there it covered everything (s63/010). An editor still at that distance (`LookDev` view `door-as-played`) showed the same, so it was the camera's distance and not the player. `PlaneHaze` (World) moves the fog out by however much further than the play distance the game's camera stands from the plane, each frame after the camera has moved (as the whiteout sets its fog): the plane stays as clear as in play and the crater is hazed by its depth at every zoom. Set only as a camera begins to draw it never reached the picture in an editor still, so LookDev's run of stills doesn't show it; `LastMooringBuilder.SorrelHazeStills` draws the zone's views with it set for each camera first. Any other flight zone with fog and a view that draws back wide has the same fault until it carries one. The pale-pink squares in the haze were the crater's glow quads: URP's particle shader mixes fog toward the fog colour even when it adds, so a soft glow whose falloff was in its colour (alpha one) added the fog's pink at its black corners. Sorrel's soft glow now has its falloff in the alpha; Nacre, Last Mooring and the Returning Passage paint theirs the old way. *The hull:* the Debris Cutter's solid is its 2.6 m capsule round a hull drawn about twice as long, and Idra's tug sat inside its underside mid-fight (s61/065; the partner's own steering takes her there). `HullKeepsOff` (Combat) measures the drawn outline about the ships' height once as it wakes and puts any ship that has come inside it back to its edge after everything has moved; it is not a collider, so shots, weak spots, lunges (a ship mid-lunge is let be until it ends) and what the Cutter strikes are as they were, and it is off for the Cutter's run through the party. *The tow:* Bryn's tug came in to the body's side toward where it tows to, which was where the held party stood, and dragged the dead Cutter through Taren (s61/086 to 098). `TowedOff` now comes in 8 m under the plane to a point past a berth on the body's side away from the ships, backs up to it rising, and tows straight away from them going down before it turns for its mark 14 m under everything. *The engines:* each district's three flames were cones of unlit glow drawn solid, flat white funnels under every stern as it lifted (s61/058 to 112); they are now added to what is behind them, warm white at the nozzle and nothing at the point. Tests: `SorrelSecondSkyTests.FromTheValleysHeadTheViewDrawnBackToShowHerKeepsThePlaneClearAndHazesTheDepths`, `BrokenRingTests.NoShipSinksIntoTheCuttersHullWhereItIsDrawn`, `BrokenRingTests.TheTugTakesTheCutterFromTheSideAwayFromTheShipsAndTowsItClearOfThem`, `BrokenRingTests.ADistrictsEnginesBurnAsLightAndNotAsSolidWhiteCones`, `LastMooringBossTests.AShipInFlightStaysInTheViewThatShowsHerGoing` (her going framed her head alone in flight, s63/162 and 163), each shown failing on the old state inside the test first.
- **D335 - The HUD has one floor and one left column, and the boss's title card one place (November 21, interface).** The whole-game capture of November 20 found the HUD laying its own pieces on each other in the player. *The foot of the screen:* a prompt a fight asks for (`InteractionPrompt.InFight`: a clamp's dock, a pad's launch) is the one prompt shown with a boss bar up, and it stood at its usual row across the bar's name and epithet in every flight fight with a dock (s63/044, s65/055, s66/149). With a bar up the prompt's row now stands on the bar's band (`GameHud.PromptRow`). The bark column knew only the hint bar under it, so lanes and a big body that shut it from its home down let a bark slide onto the bar (s63/072); it now stops over whatever the HUD has fixed at the foot under it: the hint bar, the skill row, the boss bar, the prompt over it, the current gauge (`GameHud.FloorUnder`), and with too little room it keeps off the foot rather than the feed. The prompt slides off a bark as off a hero. *The left column:* a zone's gauge hangs from the vitals frame's measured foot, not from a number written beside it (the Picket's panel stood 22 units into the vitals and cut "RESTING SELA" in half, s45/095), and the pickup feed hangs under the gauge while it is up ("Saved" printed on the Picket's panel, s45/140; `GameHud.VitalsFoot`, `ColumnFoot`). A bark holds its time and hides under an EXTRA's cut-in (s47/080), and ends when the lead launches or lands. *The title card* has one place, across the top between the vitals and the objective, where the target card stands; that card gives way for its second and a half, and the bar's name is not drawn under it (amends D221, D226's seat and the middle seat after them: over the bar it lay on Sela's feet and over the bar's own name, s15/135, s63/031; in the middle on Taren and the Warden, s41/030). The party is held middle and low by the fixed camera and the bar is at the foot, so the top is the one band neither is ever in; a boss framed high can stand under the card for its moment. *The lock card* names a boss and her integrity as it names any target (amends D198 and D254: every boss fought on foot showed only "LOCKED · LB UNLOCK · D-pad SWITCH"); the bar keeps her break. An unlocked aim on a body nowhere on the screen shows no card (a Pikeguard's stood for forty steps, s48/229); a lock stands while its body lives. *Numbers:* one kind of hit on one body adds up on one number for its first second (a swing's rhythm left "48 48 48"), nothing under half a point is shown ("0!!"), a beaten body's numbers go within .6 s, and numbers keep off the zone's name, the feed, the bark, the quest card, the prompt and the boss bar. *A faded plate's words:* the canvas blends in linear light, so the words' own band behind each line is .92 (was .62); with the plate at .35 a twentieth of the world shows behind a letter, not a quarter (white words on a white hull, pale ice and a printed board, s65/066, s35/298, s57/155). Tests: `QaNov21HudTests`, each measured check shown failing on the November 20 layout first (behind a switch set only by that run, since removed).
- **D333 - The Fibre Wisp has a body of its own, and an enemy is held to being seen from the game's camera (November 21).** The wisp was drawn as ten motes of the shared flare (`SwarmMotes`), each a fifth of a metre whose bright core is a few centimetres: two or three pixels from the play camera, in the editor and the player alike, so on the Skyfields' pale stone the lock's card sat over empty floor (nov20 s39/153, s40/096). It is now `OrielBuilder.WispBody`, a prefab built with the definitions: a white-blue core that blooms, three rings of dark violet glass turning on three axes (dark against pale stone, rimmed against a dark deck), four threads of its light and a cold light of its own on the floor; its tell is the Rider's (the fibre lights, the body swells a quarter). `OrielCombatTests.AFibreWispIsPlainlySeenFromTheGamesCameraOnTheSkyfieldsStoneAndTheChoirsDeck` draws the game's own camera at 1920 by 1080 with the body, without it twice, and asks 2,400 pixels changed, 900 strongly, on both floors: the motes changed 67 and 39, the body 24,289 and 19,784. The same fault is in every swarm drawn as motes (passed to the effects engineer).
- **D334 - The Last Warden staggers on its feet, bowed over its staff (November 21).** Broken (its guard, or its own openings: a fouled staff, a staff ringing in the floor) it held the shared Stagger frame, its head not a hand lower than at rest (nov20 s41/036, 037 showed it hung rigid at about forty degrees, feet off the floor). `WardenStagger` (Combat, added by the boss's own start) poses it each frame over its animation, eased in over a fifth of a second: the drawn body held upright, the hips sunk a quarter metre with each leg a two-bone reach to where its foot stood, the back, neck and head bowed, the staff's point brought to the floor and the other arm out. `OrielCombatTests.BrokenTheLastWardenStaggersOnItsFeetBowedAndUpright` asks, in a yard, of a fouled Draw and in the engine hall: the lower foot within .2 m of where it stood, legs within 15 degrees of upright, the head within 30 of over the feet, and the head down a tenth of a metre or more; built wrong (no `WardenStagger`) it fails on the last (.01 m), with it the head comes down .27 m. The forty-degree plank itself was not reproduced in either place and stays open until the next capture shows s41 034 to 039.
- **D336 - Vessa takes the gallery by walking out; the Colossus's crust is black slag; the Foundry is lit by what it pours (November 21, chapter 8).** Seen in the whole-game capture of November 20. *Vessa:* her fall-back talk set `vask.vessaBeaten`, the step that names the Colossus, so the card read "Cool the Slag Colossus" with a third of her bar left (s48/221, 222); and at nought the shared `GeneratedAnimator` played "Down", so a boss who leaves lay sprawled by her way out (s48/232). The talk now sets its own heard flag, `vask.vessaFellBack`, and `vask.vessaBeaten` is set and reported in `VessaDown` as she leaves. She walks out to her placed `BossExit` by the shared path (D341: a body with a `BossDeparture` is not laid down by its driver); the local walk she had first was taken out once that path gave the same walk. *The crust:* the Colossus laid its crust as a pale see-through `LingeringArea` disc (s49/033, 046), the shape of a boundary with nothing under it. Each crust is now a lobed body of black slag with a pale ash rim just above the floor, under the same Slow area, shrinking away over its last half second: the boundary marks where and the body says what. Its reach, slow and times are as tuned. *The Foundry:* its grade was cold and its floor one flat stone, so nothing in it read as a foundry (s48/061, 075, 080, 216; s49/033, 046). Its light now comes from what it works: wall furnaces, ladle stands, a crucible on the hoist, the launders round the heart, a smithy and slag cars, each with its own warm light and a pale pool beside it, and the Colossus carries a crucible light that dims as it cools. The floor is riveted iron plate with sand in islands between, and the spoil is slag. Hot parts are parts of tall bodies, so nothing flat on the floor glows unmarked, and the hanging crucibles cast no shadow and shade is toned slate, not blue: under the warm light each crucible's round shadow came out a lavender disc on the floor, a purple marking's colour. Nothing hangs over a fight's floor under the camera: three cranes added for warmth (two over the charging floor, one over the gallery) laid bands of three fights' floors out of the play view, so they are gone, and the ladle line L2's rail runs over the camera's height (14.6 m; the ladles hang on rods at the height they had, carried by `PourChannel.hanger`; they wait up by it out of the play view and are let down to pour), and the gallery drones' girders are a pair of slender rails on thin hangers the floor reads past, out of the static batch so they are measured. The heat the cranes brought is back at floor level: metal in channels let into the floor under iron gratings across Vessa's gallery and round the north of the heart, the melt's pale yellow-white, never a marking's orange, its light painted on the plate beside it, flush and walked over. The camp's and the release's nameless people each wear a body of nobody named in chapter 8 and differ by height or what they carry (`HarrowgateBuilder.Folk`). Tests: `BridleFoundryTests.TheGalleryIsTakenAsVessaWalksOutNotAsSheFallsBack`, `BridleFoundryTests.TheColossussCrustIsBlackSlagUnderItsBoundary`, `BridleFoundryTests.NothingHungOverheadHidesAFightsFloorFromTheCamera` (it failed on the scene as it was: 105, 92 and 7 points under the cranes, 80 under the L2 rails) and the release's facing in `TheReleaseIsStagedFromTheColossussFall`, each shown failing on the old state inside the test first. It counts a point hidden when most of a marking's rim width round it is (a ring 20 cm round it), so a slender rail is not a hiding piece.
- **D337 - The Warmaster wears one body of his own; Harrowgate's marks are laid in stone (November 21, chapter 8).** Seen in the whole-game capture of November 20 (s50 to s53). **His body:** in the duel he wore his guard wing's armoured shape (the pikeguards are his generated war-frame recoloured), told apart only by the lock box; in town, at the verdict and in his cell a smith's red apron. No other kit body is like his portrait, and the war-frame was generated from his own reference (`Drask_Frame.png`), so it stays his and he is told apart by what code adds: a crimson mantle (a short cape over both shoulders, clasped in bronze, and a long gold-hemmed cape down his back, lined dark), built on the frame's upper chest so it moves with him, and he is drawn 1.12 times, a head over his guard wing. `HarrowgateBuilder.TownLook`'s `Warmaster` makes two prefabs from the frame: `Prefabs/Enemies/DraskWarmaster` (his `EnemyDef`'s, `drawnScale` 1.12) and `Prefabs/Characters/DraskWarmaster` (his `CharacterDef`'s natural: his table, the field after the yield and at the verdict, his cell, Many Hands). His capsule (2.5 m, radius .9) is unchanged, so nothing he hits or is hit by moved. His name label stands .55 m higher on every Npc of his. **His cell:** one live `Whereabouts` gone at `chapter8.complete` took him from behind his bars as the card came up, the party standing there; now a live one brings him in at `vask.deposed` and a load-time one takes him away once the chapter is over. **Rule:** a person who leaves for good leaves as a scene loads, unless the place is out of sight when the flag is set (Whereabouts' own rule, which the cell broke). **The town's marks:** nothing drawn on Harrowgate's floor is flat colour: the field's mark, the walks' runners and the border inside the field's kerb are textured stone (`HarrowgateTownTests.TheMusterFieldsMarkAndTheTownsWalksAreLaidInTexturedStone`); everyone standing faces someone or something (`EveryoneStandingInHarrowgateFacesSomeoneOrSomething`, and `Town.FaceSomething` at build); the harbour's finer ripple is at least 24 px at the play camera (`TheHarboursRippleIsWiderThanAFewPixelsAtThePlayCamera`). The town's braziers burn with the chapter's fire (`HarrowgateBuilder.Hearths`).
- **D338 - The Burrower is solid as long as it is drawn, and Hessa Korl is drawn a head taller (November 21, chapters 1 and 2).** Seen in the whole-game capture of November 20 (s06/025, 030; s15/135, 148). *The Burrower:* drawn five and a half metres long on a rig's upright capsule 2.8 m across, it let a hero walk a metre into its drill and stand in its mouth. Its kit now lays a capsule along its drawn length (94% of it, never wider than the upright one, so its flanks and the weak spots on them are where they were) that stops a hero at the drill and the tail, and a hurtbox of the same shape, so a blade or a shot that meets the drawn drill lands and the replay driver, which measures its reach to a body's hurtboxes, swings from where it is stopped (`BossController.Burrower`, `start`/`update`/`down`). Its charge passes through the party as before (it follows `Collide`), buried it has no length, and it surfaces under a hero without throwing them. What it can be hit by changed: its drawn front and back now take blows; nothing it deals changed. *Hessa:* `drawnScale` 1.3, the boss and the captain under guard alike, still inside her .8 by 3 m capsule, so what she hits and is hit by is as it was. Her body, the Gloomgrazers' and the Scrapmites' keep a rim in shade and a least light, as the heroes' do (`ChapterOneLook.BodyReads`). `ChapterOneTwoLookTests` builds the capture's state first in each case and requires the fault there.
- **D345 - The fixed camera's rules: a hero is drawn through what hides them, a fight's frame holds the lock clear of the panels, a big boss draws the camera back only as far as the party reads, and the lens never closes in (November 21).** The whole-game capture of November 20 lost both heroes behind the Foundry Saint (s59), showed Taren as a patch of dots inside the Slag Colossus's wreck (s49), framed locked enemies under the party panel or off the screen (s46, s48, s35, s40), held the Tollkeeper's arm fight 36 m back (s65) and put the lens inside the Drowned Waystation's gallery wall 6.3 m from the party (s26). (1) Every hero out is drawn a second time (`HeroThrough`, shader `Lattice/HeroThrough`, copies hung under the hero) only where something stands in front of the body by more than the body is thick (.55 m; a ship 2.4): a bright rim round a light fill, the lead gold, the partner pale blue. It needs nothing of what is in the way, so it holds for bosses, their arms and wrecks, walls and crates alike, and never draws over the hero's own limbs (the old ZTest-greater ghost did, E2-sorrel-03). The boss and scenery screen doors stay. (2) A ground fight frames what the lead has locked, or what his swing is going at in a fight that is on, with him; the lead, that target, a boss's feet within 20 m and its markings within 6 m of him stay over the skill row and under the panels (`PartyController.FightHeroArea` .14-.86 x .24-.78, `FightClearArea` .06-.94 x .15-.78, and .69 for anything in a side third, under the party panel and the objective card). A lock may draw the view to 42 m, a person-sized boss to 38 (36 before). In flight a locked target or a boss counts from 80 m (30 before), a body wider than 80 m is framed where it is nearest the ship, and a fight's frame sits over the boss bar and the skill row (.18 at the foot; .12 before). (3) A boss whose body is over 6 m draws the view back to 46 m (the lead 83 px in 1080); a body that doesn't fit by its limit has what room is left, as the partner does, and no longer holds the view at the limit (`CameraRig.FrameGround(..., subjectOptional)`). (4) The ground lens never comes nearer than its play distance: where a rim hides the hero's chest it leans, up to straight down in five-degree steps (`CameraRig.Lean`), and what the lean can't clear is drawn through. D198's slide-in is gone; its tests hold as written. (5) Ground and flight cameras cut between each other: the brain's two-second blend carried the lens low over the hull after a berth's launch (s66 151, 152). (6) The flight fight frame stands no further back than 125 m (`CameraRig.FlightFarthest`, a ship about 75 px), its middle leaning to the ship by what it cannot show. (7) A talk holds the lead in .18-.82 and the speaker in .1-.9, and finds a boss who speaks by the end of its id too (Warden, LastWarden). Tests: `HeroInSightTests`.
- **D347 - The Sunken Relay is lit and floored as a drowned station, and Hessa's hall by its lantern (November 21, chapter 2).** Seen in the whole-game capture of November 20 (s15/049, 135, 148, 194) and still after D338's rebuild (`tools/look_density.py` .70 to .91 against .22; its dressing stood against walls out of every fighting frame). *Light:* the cold fill is as strong as `TheSunkenRelayReadsAsUnderground` lets a buried station have (sun .38 in a watery blue-green, sky .229 grey) and the camera opens to the dark (exposure .25 to .75): the floor reads at 40 to 50 of 255 on screen, Hushwell's level, where it was 22 to 32, and the lamps, the lantern and the heroes still burn over it. That light lies on every floor as a slow caustic web (a `GroundShade` on a painted Voronoi map, `GroundStaging.Caustics`: the first underground, so the place reads as under water). *Floor:* each hall's courses are laid in 4.4 m bays between bands of worn brass whose least light lifts them a value over the stone (as dark as the stone they didn't count as a line, measured); in the Great Lantern's hall the bands are rings round it and the fan's spokes run on to the walls, glowing at the fan and gone within a dozen metres, so the floor leads to the lantern and no spoke out where Hessa is fought can be read as a marking. Bands, and the cables now laid across the floors from the wall runs to racks, stand under a marking's 6 cm lift. A `ZoneKit.Shape`'s tones reach the shader unconverted, so the relay's are written linear (its rubber was a pale grey line). *Fights:* rack banks stand either side of the gallery's fight with runs between them, fallen lantern posts lie north and east of it and across the vault's north end; two mirrors in the great hall's south corners take beams from the lantern across the top of every frame of Hessa's fight, with their light along the floor and in the water under them (particle materials take their queue from `_QueueOffset`, so that is what is set). The two lamp posts at its door burn lower (6.5 to 4.2): their pools outshone the lantern's light in the frame Hessa's fight opens in. The empty cable channels along the cut corridor's walls, brackets still across them, are where its cables were stripped. The vault's east rack moved two metres south and the wall run under RELAY CRADLE lies in a channel there: both stood across the sign from the camera after D338 (`EveryFloorSignInChapterTwoStandsClear`). The Gloomgrazers' rim is a cold violet-blue (.6, .5, 1) and Hessa's a hostile warm orange (1, .55, .28) with a least light of .32: D338's near-white grazer and pale-cream Hessa at .26 were the grey edges `AGrazerIsNotTheSameBlackAsTheHeroItBites` and `EveryBodyInTheDarkKeepsARimInShade` forbid. All drawn only and off the routes' ways, the fights' footing and the plates' stands; nothing a fight hits or is hit by changed. `LanternRoadBuilder.RelayLook` renders the fights with their enemies (Hessa at 1.3). Tests: `SunkenRelayLookTests` (no plain stretch over .22 at five fight spots, read as look_density reads a still; the lantern's light most of the brightest pixels as Hessa's fight opens), each on the capture's state first.
- **D346 - Many Hands' bay takes the licensed water, a way's ruts run along it, and Lastlight is a town of lodges (November 21, chapters 4 and 5).** Seen in the whole-game capture of November 20 (s28 to s37). The raft bay's navy blotches were D343's fault again: Many Hands was not in `LocalBiomeBake`'s list, so the built player drew the plain public water there. It is in the list and the restage step now, with a setting of its own ("Hold": still, deep, lamp reflections). Lastlight's soaking basin and the Frozen Host's vent pools carry no licensed patch and never fell back. The dark bands across Lastlight's snow that nothing cast were the ground kit's "Ruts" decals, each turned at random across the ways, in the editor as in the player: random ruts are gone from Lastlight and Many Hands, and sledge ruts are laid along the Reach way to the gate. The Reach, the commons and the gate have 22 hide lodges with lit doorways, drying frames and loaded sledges; the rib hall's climb has bone stepping plates to both high lamps and a post-and-rope rail on its south and west drops; the galley's tables stand on legs with food at every place and two fires under pots at the range, their solid boxes, seats and Ilo unchanged. Every container stack in the freight hall fades like Lanternwick's tall scenery (the camera engineer's ask, tested from the arrival and from behind each stack, failing first with one stack's fade off). Idra's whiteout spot in the Frozen Host moved to (-28.6, 106.6), under the windbreak: at s35/097 she was not inside Taren by the companion's doing but standing half a metre from where the route stops. By design and left: the cabins' glowing grid (the sealed breach's fresh welds), the Marshal's washed-out look at s36/062 (the boss see-through), the second Tender at s32/144. Left for cost: the garden pillar and slab, the lane and hangar floors, the Frozen Host's floor and walls. For others: Steady Hand is a person seen in the scene and has an emblem for a face, and Hask's body has no cream fur (townsfolk and portraits); at s36/062 the partner attacks the Marshal from the lead's own flank and stands in him (`PartnerBrain`). Rebuilt and restaged: Lastlight, the Frozen Host, Many Hands and its hull. Runs `ch45-after2` 260 of 273 with none failed, `ch45-hands5` 130 of 137 with one failed, the townsfolk engineer's new look case for Many Hands, which is theirs to turn green. Plain-floor measure .18 at worst.
- **D348 - Living bodies keep their own ground: the partner takes another side, a ship keeps off a living body, and a hound's head stops at the hero (November 21).** Four reviews of the whole-game capture of November 20 found living bodies standing inside each other. (1) On foot the partner's place was the body she fought, from wherever she stood: with the lead at its near side and her behind him, the way to it ran through him (the Host Marshal, s36/062: Idra 0.77 m from his middle, capsules touching, 30 degrees round from him). While the lead is at the same body, a blade or a hammer partner now takes a place on her own side of it at her stand-off, at least 75 degrees round from him and never within 1.1 m of his middle, goes round outside him to it, and holds a step back where no side is open (`PartnerBrain.MeleeStand`; a framed place is never within 1.1 m of him either). Not when she shoots, and not when the lead is elsewhere. (2) In flight a living body had no solid to her sweeps and her stand-off was 2.2 m, middle to middle: Sela's hull sat 0.57 m inside the Drifter she was shooting (s10b/089), whose drawn body reaches 1.13 m on a 0.55 m capsule. Her sweeps now keep her hull and its room off every living body by what it draws (`EnemyBrain.BodyRadius`), except the one she fights and what it is part of; that one she closes on to her hull, its drawn radius and half a metre (`PartnerBrain.ShipStandOff`). A body she is already inside the room of is no wall to leaving it; a collared animal keeps its own guide. (3) A hound's or a grazer's capsule is round its middle and its head 1.26 to 1.31 m ahead of that: walked in and lunged until the capsules met, its head stood 0.35 m inside the hero (s14/017). Its walk-in and its lunge now stop where its drawn front (`EnemyBrain.FrontReach`, from its meshes in its own frame) meets a hero's capsule, and the lunge bites from that mouth; nothing is pushed. `BodiesKeepApartTests`, each shown failing first on the old code. *Amended the same evening:* a hound's lunge is judged from its head, so it marks and commits from as far off head to hero as before and covers the same ground (3.3 m) before its front stops at him (`CorePlayTests`' pack lunge); and on foot in a fight the ground the lead walks to is his: she gives way to a walking lead as outside a fight, and takes her side of a body as he comes to it, not once he is there (the Tollkeeper's gate motor, route 65 step 124: she stood on his way striking, he slid off her into the housing and stopped 0.72 m short).
- **D349 - Words in the world are drawn whole, upright and out of the way, by rule (November 21, labels).** The November 20 capture showed about sixty words cut, turned or lost, and the old checks (size, level, one material) all passed. The rules now, all in `SignStyle`, `SignFade` and `PeopleMarks`: (1) every sign and name is drawn `SignStyle.Reach` (6 m) nearer the camera along its own lines of sight (`Lattice/SignText`, `Lattice/SignPlate` scale it about the eye, so it covers the same pixels): a lamp post, well, crate, awning or bunting in front no longer cuts it, and a wall over about 3.8 m still hides it (which makes that the builder's placement to fix); (2) a floor word near the foot of the screen grows to at most 1.1 times its made size (`Band`, in the shader), and a word whose em falls under three quarters of the type floor isn't drawn at all (the wide flight views); (3) a body over a floor word's letters takes it to a trace (.08: not to nothing, which `AWordUnderAHerosLegsStepsAside` rules out), whoever's body it is, and the old fade by distance from the hero is gone; (4) a place's name steps aside while a fight is on round the party, and every word goes at once, not over a tenth of a second, for a conversation, a menu, the card or a boss bar; (5) a word keeps 16 px (1080p) from the screen's edge and from any plate; (6) every person's name faces the camera from the zone's first frame, talk open or not, a sign keeps reading level whatever turns what it hangs on, and a name has a dark tag behind it in its own text (TextMeshPro's mark over an unseen copy of the name, the name set back over it: a plate of its own floated over every head). TextMeshPro writes every mark after every letter, so in one draw the tag came down over the name and held every backed name at about a third of its brightness ("! Orrin", found November 21); `Lattice/SignText` draws a text's marks in a pass of their own before its letters (a mark is the quad with no scale in its fourth texture coordinate), the tag stands .9 em past the line the name sits in, and a marked name's tag runs under the mark and the name at the mark's height. That also means the name counts before November 21 were not what they claimed: under its own tag a name's letters were too dark for the test to find (fewer than ten letter pixels, so it was passed over), and the names were in effect unread. Read now, with the tags at .45 and .4 em, 50 names fell under 3 to 1 against the ground round the tag; at .9 em, none; (7) as the zone loads, what is behind each sign from the play camera is read: on ground pale all through (its darker fifth .4 or more) the word is printed in the HUD's deep slate with a thin pale halo, over the lightest plate (a hanging sign's is pale); otherwise it keeps white ink, on a lighter plate where what is behind it is .26 or less and on the full one (.95, solid a quarter metre past the word and under it too) above that, Sorrel's sunlit floors held under .5. Plates stay under danger markings (the lead's ruling) and over the floor's decals and lamp pools (they already were).
