Measured for the last twelve faint words: nothing transparent drew over them (taking every transparent thing over each word away changed its surround by .05 at most) and post-processing wasn't it either; the full plate had stopped at the floor line, the old rule for a plate lying on the floor, and left bright ground right under the letters. Drawn nearer the camera than the floor, a plate can't be sliced by it, so the full one now runs under the word as well. The load-time read sees as far as the play camera (at 400 m past a word it missed the way down's planet, 760 m under the lane, and THE WAY DOWN got the faint plate across Hoarfell's lit limb), and dark ink's pale halo keeps two fifths of the outline's width, not three (from across the Continuance's deck it filled BOARDING SPINE's strokes). DRILL SITE moved 2 m onto the drill path's sand (ChapterOneStaging; Sorrel's plates stay under .5), and BOARDING SPINE 2 m in from the Continuance's deck edge, where it lay half over the dark beyond it. `WorldLabelTests` reads every zone from the play camera, one case per zone, each word from the party three metres short of it and again from every other word's view that holds it whole (first read only in whichever view the scene's order put it, so a re-save of DecksLeaving on November 21 moved ARRIVALS / DOCK OFFICE into GATE SIX's view, where it lay half under the arrivals hall's inner lintel; it now lies in the hall, in front of that arch, by the day's own build): seen whole (cut: 36 words before, 0 now, after moving LOW STREET, RAFT BAY, the hull's CABINS, the Decks' CABINS 01-04 and MARKET / GALLEY, the Orrery's gate names and Orrin), upright (9 names before, 0 now), and against what is behind it, 3 to 1 against the side of the background nearest its ink (93 faint before, 0 now). The moments (the bar's first frame, a fight, a hero standing on a word, a word by the edge or a plate, a word under the type floor, the town meeting) are each a case, each shown failing on the old rules first.
- **D350 - A meeting faces its speaker from where it sits, the Terminator is the edge of day, and a lamp keeps its colour (November 21, chapter 8).** Seen in the whole-game capture of November 20 (s53 to s59). The town meeting on the day of leaving has thirty people in thirty bodies (Dace's crew no longer wear Dace's), and bodies that share a figure stand a tenth or more apart in height; everyone is placed facing Orrin's board, and a seated person turns at most 30 degrees on the bench toward whoever speaks (`Posed.SeatTurn`, applied in `LineCues`; only this zone uses either). Not fixed, and passed to the townsfolk engineer: seated people still sink through their benches, because the sitting pose leaves the knees about .29 m up and the feet 4 to 19 cm under the deck, in play and in the editor alike; two attempts (a muscle-based leg search, turning backward bodies round) were reverted and their test dropped rather than loosened. The Terminator's lit ground behind the line is warm gold with a gold edge and its night side slate blue; tread prints lie on the freshest ruts, every cairn carries a 3.4 m pole and every second one a lantern, two derelict halls stand east of the way, and the rocks are textured and half as many. The blown-out white band along the dawn was its steam: 80 additive puffs a second summing to white, which no editor still draws because stills run no particles; it is alpha-blended now and a third as dense, on the arithmetic alone until the next capture shows it. The lamp that blew out to a white blob (s56/060) and the joint strips that were flat white blocks (s58/130, 176) were the zones' own emissive colours clipping: the Works' strips read cyan and its lamps amber, the Crawl's lamps and windows amber, the coupling lamp's and the engine-car ride lamps' colours lowered (lit only in play, so unseen). The Crawl's ropes are lit and twisted, its market boards laid in sections, its dark windows and doors timber. The Nacre Grave's old wrecks have a pearl finish, its crates and seed pods are hull parts, and the white lump (a mooring coupler) is gone; it has no fog, so D344's haze does not apply. For the camera: the Foundry Saint is 12 m tall at (0, 196) facing north in a hall of 48 by 40 m with a fight box of 46 by 26 m. For the routes: in the Nacre Grave a pilot following the arrow is tight past the family wreck at turn 150, the raft at 162 and wall boxes 99 and 105. Where the partner stopped on November 20, near (-125,-28) to (-107,-14), the only solids are the inner wall (face at x about -104.1), the outer wall and the inner-tows trigger; a CompactCutter and a Skiff are drawn there and not solid. Rebuilt and restaged: Terminator, MeridianCrawl, NacreGrave, SeparationWorks, DecksLeaving (from Hub_Decks as saved at 12:43: a later Hub_Decks staging needs DecksLeaving restaged). DecksLeavingTests 10 of 10.

- **D351 - No two townsfolk in one screen read as one figure twice, everyone seated sits on something of their own height, and one person has one face (November 21, townsfolk and portraits).** Every reviewer of the whole-game capture of November 20 but one reported clones (four of one red-haired woman at the decks' meeting, s57/136; aproned smiths in a row, s49/140; one antennaed body in colour swaps across Driftreed, s24/022), and every people test passed, because each measured a material's name or a tint. The bodies are a dozen species meshes repainted per named person on one UV layout (Brekk, Drask, Odrun and Tovra are one mesh), so a *template* is the mesh, and two of one template are copies unless three of their clothes, head, what they carry and their build differ (`TownsfolkLook`, shared by the rule and its test; clothes and head compared texel by texel once a tint read off the hands is taken out; a body in a force's colours, or a squad at drill, is in uniform). **The rule no builder can get wrong:** every scene under `Scenes` is dressed as it is saved, by whoever saves it (`TownsfolkLooks`, a `sceneSaving` hook): the builder's painting kept where it can be, then, cheapest first, one of the body's four dyes (`tools/townsfolk_dyes.py`: clothes and hair moved in Lab on the body's own UVs, skin and boots kept; 176 at 512, 19 MB), another painting of the template the place has, a cap, band or scarf, a pack, satchel, bedroll, coil or parcel, and a build (8% in height, 12% in breadth; an errand walked at its height's pace). Neighbours are everyone of one template some frame of the play camera can hold together, the hero on any floor of the place; a greedy choice, then rounds of repair. Nobody moves, nothing solid is added, the builder's choice is kept on each person (`TownsfolkDress`); `TownsfolkLooks.Held` keeps a scene still being rebuilt as built (the Sunken Relay). `TownsfolkLookTests` (one case per zone) reads every frame along the routes and at every resident and named person, as built and as dressed: pairs of copies as built, Hub_Decks 7, Tallow Drift 7, Lanternwick 1, Rootmarket 14, Driftreed 40, Many Hands 56, Lastlight 18, the Skyfields 5, the Foundry 3, Harrowgate 177, the Scoria Flows 4, the decks on the day of leaving 56, the Meridian Crawl 67, the Continuance 14, the Neighbourhood 71; dressed, none. **Seats:** the one sitting pose leaves the knees .29 m up whatever the legs, so the same save fits every seated body to its seat (`Seating`): lifted until its soles rest on its floor (onto the bench, feet hanging, where the bench is the taller), and given a cushion or folded blanket on a bench or a crate on the floor with its top at its pelvis's underside (`SeatFit`); `SeatedTests` (EditMode, per zone) holds soles to the floor and the pelvis on its seat: as built, 24 faults among the meeting's 13 sitters (soles up to 48 cm under the deck, a pelvis 38 cm in its bench) and 4 among the Continuance's 2; fitted, none. Many Hands' galley holds nobody seated by a Posed, so nothing there changed. **Faces:** Idra's braced sheet came back another woman (broader jaw, amber eyes, a visor reading as a crown); she shows her own sheet in both forms (`PortraitFaces`), and `PortraitSheetTests` holds every speaker to one sheet (the heroes' shaped sheets excepted, by eye) and no two to one sheet or a recolour of one (a recolour scores .98, the closest two people .61). Hask's body wears his portrait's cream fur (`tools/hask_grade.py`). By design: Ona's antennae curl by expression; Vessa and Idra are two women of one people. Left for new art: Steady Hand's face (an emblem stands in), and Orrin's and Hal's (Taren's hair and shawl, three sheets drawn from one reference).
- **D352 - The lead's own changes on November 21: the evidence run and what it was read for, the test queue, a dying boss's footing, and the Cutter's measure left open.** The release build of November 20 (22:15) was played by the replay from a new save to the end of the credits in 69 captured, focus-checked segments (`Builds/quality/nov20-whole`, 23:31 to 04:34; one segment taken twice for a recording overflow), every one of 22 boss fights won at the first attempt and no exception in any player log; that tree was pushed as 983be319. Ten reviewers then read every segment's stills from nine-to-a-sheet surveys (`review/`, near-duplicates dropped), and the lead checked each Blocker and doubtful Major on the full still before assigning it: what they found, and what was done, is D333 to D351. Read from the same capture without a reviewer: `frames.csv` carries the partner's state every frame, and a scan for a partner idle while the lead acts found the two zones of D339; the chain's logs show what a route's ship scrapes (`WALL_CONTACT`). A capture is evidence to be mined, not only a pass. `scripts/focused.ps1` asked each test mirror in passing and slept three seconds between rounds, so it lost every race to anything that waits on a mirror's turn: with a dozen engineers some runs stood ninety minutes and gave up while mirrors changed hands. It waits on all the mirrors' turns at once now (`WaitHandle.WaitAny`). `DefeatPresentation.BeginDown` switched off every collider of a ground enemy the moment it died, so heroes walked into the Rotbloom as it sank and held their talk standing in it (s26/457). A boss that dies on the ground keeps its `CharacterController` while it is drawn; not one that yields or walks off (`BossDeparture`: solid for its walk it would stop heroes, routes and shots in doorways) and not one that fights in flight (the effects engineer's review of the first, wider version). `XylosWorldTests.AFreedBargeDriftingOntoTheShipThatCutItShovesItAndChargesNothingUntilItRests` is the test D332's barge change lacked: the first pass takes the barge off `FlightMotor.UnderWay` as it comes free, the game as it was, and must see the ship charged; the second must see it shoved and charged nothing, a wall again once it rests, and nothing left on the list by a barge abandoned in mid-drift. Open, and not to be closed on a busy machine: `HomeBossTests.TheCutterLastsAboutThreeMinutesForEveryPairAPlayerCanPutOut`. On November 21 the fastest pair read 4,050 to 4,180 a second where a quiet machine had read 3,641 and 3,898 the evening before, and the Cutter (159,000) came out one to four seconds under its two-minute floor, a different pair each run. The test now prints the frames a second it measured at and is inconclusive under fifty, as the shared length test is; but one failing reading was taken at 57, so load alone does not explain it. Either a pair's flat-out damage moves with frame pacing or the bar leaves this measure no room (the pairs' rates stand 1.73 to 1.85 apart and the bounds allow 1.79). To settle with three runs alone on a quiet machine before the bar or the Cutter is touched; in play the Cutter lasted 183 s in the capture and 226 s in the scout. **Settled the same afternoon, at the third attempt, and every guess before it was wrong.** It is not load and not frame pacing: at a steady sixty frames a minute's reading still moved four to six per cent, and taking a third reading to steady the mean made it worse. The cause is the measure itself: it read the three pairs one after another in one game, and a pair's flat-out rate climbs with the minutes already fought there (Sela and Idra read 3,910 as the fourth reading of a run, 4,171 as the seventh, 4,553 as the ninth; Taren and Idra are flat after their first). So a pair's number depended on how many readings stood before it, and November 20's bar was centred on whatever order that day's reruns happened to take. The pair test now starts a game for each pair and always takes two minute-long readings and their mean. Read so, twice over (`lead-nov21-cutter-cold1` and `cold2`): Taren and Sela 3,895 and 3,800, Sela and Idra 3,595 and 3,593, Taren and Idra 2,359 and 2,339, each pair within one and a half per cent of itself. A third run (`lead-nov21-cutter-163`, 41 of 41 with the defeat classes) read Taren and Sela at 3,932 and Taren and Idra at 2,264, and the shared length test read the arrival pair at 2,213 in the same run. Across all of it the room between the fastest pair's two-minute floor and the slowest reading's five minutes at a quarter of flat out is 155,500 to 165,800; the Cutter is 160,500, its middle, three per cent inside each (`HomeBuilder.Bosses.cs`, and the definition asset by hand as D331 did the Bellows'), which is three and a half per cent more than the build that was captured. Three per cent is all the room this boss's three pairs leave; a reading further out than any seen will fail it, and the answer then is to look at the pairs, not to move the Cutter again. The frame-rate guard stays. Not found, and worth finding: what makes a pair's rate climb over minutes in one game (something of Sela's, or a gauge the party shares), and whether a player in a long fight gets the same climb; every other boss's pair measure that reads pairs in sequence has the same weakness.
- **D353 - The Cutter's measure closed: a pair's rate climbs because its skills gain mastery as it fights (November 22).** D352 left open why a pair's flat-out damage on the Debris Cutter rose with the minutes it had already fought in one game. The test's own log answers it: `SKILL_MASTERY` lines take each hero's skills to level 2 and then 3 inside one pair's two readings (`Builds/quality/focused/lead-nov21-cutter-1605-playmode.xml`, 44 such lines). Every release counts towards a skill's mastery (6, 18, 36 and 60 releases, `SkillBook.MasteryUses`), and each level is a tenth more power and 2 less Charge, so a pair that fights for minutes hits harder at the end than at the start. That is the progression working, not a leak, and nothing in the game changes for it. What follows: every boss measure reads pairs who start at mastery 1, so a player who brings skills at mastery 4 or 5 to a late boss finishes sooner than the measure's flat-out time. Skill power is up to 40% higher at mastery 5; how much of a pair's whole damage that is was not measured. It is left as the reward the skill card promises. The Cutter's measure keeps a fresh game for each pair and two readings, and its integrity stays at 160,500. The Colossus and Vessa measures read their pairs in one game in a fixed order, which repeats from run to run, so they are left alone.
- **D354 - The partner is never a solid to the lead (November 22).** In one evening the partner blocked the lead in four places, each after a rule written for the last: a Picket leg (nov21-recon1 s45), the Tollkeeper's gate motor (s65), route 10's regroup (nov21-whole s10, nov22-recon10 s10), and then the Orrery. In the whole-game capture of November 22 (nov22-whole s42c, three plays of three) the lead waited thirteen seconds in the Steward's lee at its middle gate with Idra parked 4.3 m behind his nose, in the mouth of the gate he was waiting to go through; at green he opened up to full speed and stayed at z 147.3 for five seconds, sliding along her hull, until the leg timed out. More rules about when she gives way would fail in the next narrow place, so no hero is a solid to another now: each hull ignores the others' (`HeroCollision.ThroughParty`, by `Physics.IgnoreCollision`, kept by both motors before every move and by the party every frame, since a collider that is switched off forgets what it ignored), and everything else stays solid to all of them. Nothing she does can stop, slow or turn him, and flying or walking into her is never a scrape. Her rules are now only about how it looks: her place behind his nose, her own side of a body on foot (D348's `MeleeStand` and `RoundTheLead`), braking to arrive (D339's `Arriving`), and never coming to rest inside him: a resting place that would be inside him moves just outside him on its side, and inside him (he went through where she was) she leaves by the shortest way (`PartnerBrain.OutOfTheLead`: both hulls' reach and 0.3 m in flight, 1.1 m on foot; a place in the frame inside his hull is refused too). In flight her way back to her place still goes round him rather than down his next leg ahead of his nose (`ShipRoundTheLead`, the Picket's pocket 9), except while he flies toward her, when she comes to it through him (`LeadComing`, now only that reading: route 10's regroup). Taken out, as rules that only existed to unblock him: D328's step off his way on foot (`GivesWay`; its slide along a wall, `GroundMotor.SlideAlong`, stays), and D339's evening slide across his line as his hull was about to meet hers. Over an animal's own guide (`FlightSeparation`) nothing changes. `PartnerNeverSolidTests` stages the Orrery wait as captured and plays it through the pad (on the old code the lead sat at full speed going nowhere for 5.1 s and the leg timed out), and drives the lead straight through where his partner rests, in flight and on foot, with her brain running and with her held there, against the same drive with nobody there (on the old code: in flight 1.33 m off his line and 30 frames against her hull, on foot held never arriving in six seconds; on foot with her brain running D328 had cleared him). `FlightHullTests`' head-on case, which held that two party hulls meet before their noses do, now holds that they fly through each other (on the old code 33 frames against the other hull). Now the lead arrives through her as fast as alone (2.183 s in flight, 2.067 s on foot, 0.000 m off his line, touching nothing) and goes through the Steward's gate on green with Idra parked where the capture had her. Green in a mirror (`never-solid-1`, `never-solid-2`; `CompanionTravelTests` read again whole in `never-solid-3`, after one case of it counted 79 frames in 2.2 s with the suite's three Unitys on the machine): the new class, `PartnerFlightRejoinTests` (the Picket, the regroups), `FlightHullTests`, `GivingWayTests`, `BodiesKeepApartTests`, the companion contracts, the three release-spacing classes, `BargeSkimmerReplayTests`, `HarrowgateReplayTests`, `CorePlayTests`, `OrielRouteTests`, `OrielReplayHandsTests`, `MooringRouteTests`, `MooringReplayHandsTests` and the Tollkeeper's cases.
- **D357 - Every pause page is one list in columns, the row under the cursor is lit, and a conversation's choices stand on its box (November 22).** The capture of November 22 found the pages D340 left still one dot-separated string a row (Skills, its chooser, Items, the key bindings), the Party page's numbers without headings and its Swap and save slots in the heroes' list, the bench's costs with what's held only in the footer and its ladders saying "Edges 2 (20 of 30)" beside a bare "Emitters 1", Gear's WORN column reading "now +8", the Smokehouse with no row lit and nothing said, and Mira's choices standing over Sela. Skills, the chooser, Items (with a card under the list) and the bindings now stand in `MenuList` columns under headings (a skill's button, its icon as the HUD draws it, name, cost, cooldown and "Mastery 1 (0 of 6)" with a bar; under the slots a card with the skill's icon, name and what it does); the Party page heads its numbers, makes Swap a control of its own under them and puts the slots under SAVE THE GAME (Swap first stood on the header's line, and route 1's three presses down from the first hero saved to slot 2, so route 2's title found no slot 1 to load: nov22-recon-b s01, s02b; routes 1, 5 and the smoke route count those presses, and `MenuLayoutTests.TheReplaysPadReachesEveryRowItPicksByNameAndThePressDoesWhatItSays` now drives the Party page, a stall, the bench, the quest log and the title's picker by pad as the replay does); a recipe is two lines, the second "Takes Scrap Alloy ×6 (have 45), Ridge Crystal ×2 (have 0)" with what's short in warm red, and every ladder says "(N of 30)"; Gear's and the stall's comparison is what wearing it would change ("+22", "slot free", in green or red, headed VS WORN). A stall's strip stands under its list (six rows a page) with the thing's mark and name, and says what a press does while the cursor is on the toggle, which a stall a talk opens still lands on (QA-036); over the seller the party, each hero's Integrity and Charge or what the part would change on them. `SelectionMark` lays a cool light wash over the focused control's face (a gold one took the contrast from gold names and warm-red shortfalls) and widens its bar: the selection used to raise a row's light by nothing measurable (-0.003 of white), now by 0.14 to 0.18, measured off renders of every list (pause pages, tabs, stalls and their toggle, the save picker, the title) against a floor of a twelfth. Choices stand on the conversation box at its right end, sized to the longest, and while they're up `ConversationFraming` keeps the speaker, the hero and the partner above them. `MenuLayoutTests` measures it; the selection's light, the overlap measure, the strip's place, the partner under the choices and a choice on the box are each first run against the broken state built inside the test, while the column and wording checks were not run against the old layout. `ListRow.Words` reads a cell without its colour.
- **D355 - A killed boss goes dark and folds in within its first second, nothing lands on it, and a fallen body lies clear of the heroes (November 22).** From the whole-game capture of November 22. (1) *The Rotbloom* (s26/454 to 469) did collapse in the player: D343's ending squashed it to four tenths of its height and spread it to 1.06 of its width over 1.5 s, eased in. From the fixed camera a flat bloom squashed in height is the same bloom, so it stood whole in its own colours for three seconds after its integrity was gone, Taren in its left petal 6.3 m from its middle. Nor was it solid: D343 switched its capsule on as it died and EnemyBrain switched it off straight after; that stays so, since a killed body never blocks the way (QaPlanCombatTests), and the body draws in off whoever stood at it instead. A boss that is killed (ending Destroyed or ShutDown) now goes dark at once (`BodyGoesDark`, over its whole root, after the flash and the see-through door): its lights out in a third of a second and its paint drained in .7 s, on the game's clock and never more than .06 a frame (destroyed: warm ash at under half its lightness, its texture's grain under half; shut down: its own colours at half, and never relit, so the Debris Cutter's lights stay out). The Burrower, the Host Marshal and the Last Warden keep their own light and paint. What is huge and destroyed folds in to half its reach and four tenths of its height round the middle of what it draws, fastest at first (89% there at 1 s); the Bellows draws in to six tenths. Timing is unchanged: a boss's body is gone 3.6 s after nought. (2) *Nothing lands on a beaten boss:* its parts (BossParts' targets, a collar's links), which share its break and not its death, are shut with it and their knots go dark, and what was shut opens again if it is brought back, as the Cantor is (`EnemyBrain.ShutItsParts`, `OpenItsParts`). The "295!! 63!" on the dead Bellows (s08/092) were the killing blow's own numbers still rising. (3) *The Cantor's white ring* (s10/151) is its release (`CantorRelease.Free`, an 11 m pale ring: freed, not killed), by design. (4) *A fallen body lies clear of the heroes:* two Gloomgrazers killed one at each side of Taren rolled their backs onto him and lay in his torso for two seconds (s14/017 to 022). A falling body is laid on whichever flank keeps it off the heroes and slides off any hero it would lie within a third of a metre of, along the line from him through its middle, before it has rolled far (`DefeatPresentation.FallClear`, at most 1.4 m). Living Gloomgrazers do not stand in him (s15/052): a pack fighting a standing Taren in the relay's flooded gallery came 0.04 m into his capsule at the deepest, and the still shows one at contact 1.6 m behind him, drawn over his back by the camera. Hessa Korl (s15/149) stood about 1.9 m behind him (from the still's feet and the camera's pitch); played through her moves against him she never came inside him. (5) *The Bellows' dome* (s08/067, 068, 075) is left: its shell is drawn 2.73 m out at a hero's height and its 1.4 m capsule stops a hero 1.75 m from its middle, so a hero at its far side stands a metre inside the shell and the see-through door shows him in it. A 2.5 m capsule (and hurtbox) still left him 0.26 m in and cut the flat-out party's rate on it from 466 to 344 a second, past the five-minute bar, so the choice between a smaller drawn dome and a capsule at the shell with the Bellows retuned is the lead's. `BossDeathTests` beats every boss and reads, drawn from the game's framing without fog, the share of its body's pixels changed a second later (killed, driven off or freed: at least 30%), the hand-over within 1.5 s for one who yields, no blow or number on it or its parts, and the lead's lock let go; it fails a Rotbloom held as it stood. `BodiesKeepApartTests` holds the corpses, the pack and Hessa in their own places.
- **D358 - The low floor's sentries are fought at its north wall and its floor is flagstones; the mire's waterline is drawn; Rootmarket's mats are whole and its kitchen is in frame (November 22, chapter 3).** From the whole-game capture of November 22. *The lower gallery* (s26 301 to 311): the low floor's two Drowned Sentries stood in the west wall's alcoves, so a Guardian's back was taken from inside the niche and the camera framed that corner from the south-west, the top of the masonry beside it two thirds of the screen. They stand in the north wall's two alcoves now, facing the room as the hall's do (`Waystation.FloorSentries`, -16,250.6 and -10,250.6; `Gallery_Floor`'s box reaches z 252.8), so the fight is on the floor with the wall behind it; a fallen girder and a lamp that stood across that wall's foot moved to the west wall and to the pier between the alcoves. No wall was lowered: from the old alcove the frame stays mostly masonry, and nothing sends a fight there. `WaystationTests.TheLowFloorsSentriesAreFoughtWithTheFloorInFrontOfThem` measures how much of the frame over each sentry's place is wall top well in front of the party (rays through the play camera's screen against the landform): half the frame at the old alcove, which the case shows first, a tenth and none at the two new places. A Guardian with open floor round it is easier to get behind than one in a niche; its numbers are as tuned. *The station's floors* (s26 290, 357, 508): the ground kit's pavers (a wild path's irregular stones) were broken up across the low rooms over dark damp and drifted sand, and the walk from the portico to Pim, painted as trodden mire peat because it started at y 168 under a test for 170, laid two dark bands down every room: camouflage. Every station floor is now flagstones in courses (`ground-way-flags`, a 4.8 m repeat with its stone height in its alpha), darker and greener the lower they lie, shaded wet and dry in patches a few metres across; the damp lies only at wall feet and round the sumps; silt drifts a hand out from the walls; 19 puddles of the station's own dark water (`waystation-puddle`, a copy of the kit's, whose sky-blue read as lilac slime under this dusk). *The mire's water* (s24 117 and 125, s27 098): Stylized Water 3 draws its waterline where 1 - e^depth / length is over nothing, so it reaches ln(length) metres and at a length of 1 or less it is nowhere: at .55 the Mire palette had no line in the player or the editor, and in tan it took the reed rims' own olive. 1.9 (.64 m) and near white; a fresnel of 1.2 for a dusk sheen (`WendmireWorldTests.TheMiresWaterDrawsAWaterlineAtEveryRaftAndBank`, which sees .55 first). The River (.25), Nursery and Refuge (.25) and Hold (.75, D359) palettes draw no line either. A skiff no longer lies under the school shelter's roof corner (s24 178). *Rootmarket*: a mat was left out wherever a floor sign's ground came near it, and a sign goes for every conversation, so the mooring's and the seed house's floors showed a bare square through the arrival talk (s18 010 and 051, s19 061); every mat is laid (`XylosWorldTests.EveryLaidFloorInRootmarketIsLaidWhole`). Binna's oven, pot, woodpile and rack were built (case b of the round's rule: in the scene, out of frame) across the kitchen's north-east, the top of the frame and under the objective card wherever BINNA'S KITCHEN is read; they stand at its front now, the oven (21, 61.3) behind the sign in the town's loam with its mouth lit toward the camera and the drying rack beside it, the pot and the woodpile east of the walk up to Binna, each a metre or more from the replay routes' walks past the kitchen (chapter-19 and chapter-26, which `XylosWorldTests.EveryWalkOnTheChapterThreeRoutesFollowsFloorABodyCanCross` holds) (`XylosWorldTests.BinnasKitchenIsOnScreenWhereverItsSignIsRead`). Ruva's arrival talk opens at a line across the way at x 23.5 (`XylosBuilder.ArrivalLine`, 2 by 22 m), about seven metres short of her, not at x 14, 16.6 m short: the talk's frame held her at its right edge and the party at its left (s18 010); she stays at (28.5, 21), and route 18's walks to the line move with it (`XylosWorldTests.TheArrivalTalkHandsTarenTheRope...` holds the line within 8 m of her). *The Braided River*: seed drifts on the fan thinner and nearer the gravel's colour (s20 327), and a broad light and dark over its gravel (s20 359). *The Spore Swarm* (s23 159 and 170) was not on screen at either still: at 159 the nearest of the field's swarms was 88 m up the lane, at 170 Pocket B's were 36 m north of the ship (its motes are the green scatter round the dart's marking at 174), and the bead chains and lone green dots are the Veil's drift (strands strung with spores, loose spores, 4 to 13 m under the plane); left as it is.
- **D360 - Sorrel's haul road runs between ridges, the coil's bed has what its current left, the Reach's way home has islands on both sides, and Hessa is the boss of her room (November 22, chapters 1 and 2).** Seen in the whole-game capture of November 22 (s04/019, 054; s10/159 to 176; s15/135, 149; s16/134, 136). *Sorrel:* D338's two spines were built and in the scene (round brief rule 1, b): the outpost's at the frame's foot on the way back, the haul road's at s04/020's top corner under the objective panel; from the road between them the frame was road and scrub, and the one rock clump in it was the cave's stacked rubble twice (`SorrelRedesign.Dress` puts `HushwellRubble` every third verge piece, at the bend below the strata and past the upper ore). Thirteen spines now run beside the haul road from the Scrapmites' first fight to the drill (`ChapterOneLook.Ridges`), on one side or both wherever the road isn't crossing a fight's clearing, swinging out round the clearings, each a dozen metres with its own heights (2.8 to 4.3 m over the basin's rise), its foot 7 to 8 m from the road's middle, and its back shortened (`back`, 1.6 to 2.4 m) where the seam or service road comes in behind it; laid out from the play camera beside the road before it was built (in frame at 52 of the 66 two-metre steps between outpost and drill; the rest are in clearings), every one held off roads, fights, ore and the camera's line to a fight's middle (`CH1_LOOK_RIDGE_REFUSED`, none refused). Scrub and low rock that would grow out of a spine go by its own shape, not its box (a diagonal spine's box took the verge's scrub). Each spine's whole footprint is cut out of the paths (cut round its crest only, the floor offered a walk up its steep back: `TheCompanionKeepsUpOnFoot`), and each fades for a hero behind it (`ZoneKit.Fadeable`: from its back and ends the camera's line crosses its crest, `TheCameraNeverHidesTheHeroAtTheFootOfAWall`). The first round's two spine meshes are deleted. The verge's two rubble clumps are a boulder pile and a slab ledge, each no wider than 2.4 m (`SorrelVerge`). *The coil:* the frames after the Cantor are the coil itself (the ship waits at 12 to 20 m east, 764 north, as the camera comes back in from the fight's distance), not its exit, and its bed had four moorings and its fold rings and nothing else. Twelve eddies of the salvage the current left (60 pieces of a hold tumbled on the bed in the eddy's cold paint, one skiff's hull on its side with its lights dead) lie on the bed at its outer fold, 5 m and more under the flight plane, drawn only, off the moorings' sockets and the middle, a little off the collar's prompt; three are in the replay's post-fight frame. No Choir pods: glowing cyan on the bed, from above they read as darts. *The Reach:* D338's islands were there (rule 1, c): one side a row by coin and most far out and deep, so on the way home one side of the frame was bare for most of the leg. A second pass (`LanternRoadBuilder.ReachIslandsBothSides`, its own root) reads the flight camera down the way home every three metres and puts a shallow island wherever a side of the frame between the HUD's panels is bare, 8 m and more off the lane, clear of the warp, dock and relays: 32 islands, both sides in frame every 4 m at the play distance and drawn back as a boost draws it. *Hessa:* D347's 1.3 was in the build; the review's "1.2 times a hero" was her height in pixels at the top of the frame, 26 m from the lens against heroes 19 m from it (measured, she was already about 1.8 heroes tall). What she lacked was a shape no hero has, and a rim that showed. Her boss body is a prefab variant of her body (`HessaKorlCaptain`, made by `HessaRegaliaBuilder` in the chapter's definitions): a yoke past her shoulders with a stolen relay lantern, lit, hung at each end, shoulder plates, a Muster mantle down her back, and the Muster's standard (red, the hook-and-ring mark) on a pole a metre and a half over her head; `HessaRegalia` holds it at her upper chest and turns it with her body (turned with the chest bone, the editor's still had it on her front). Drawn 1.55 (`HessaDrawn`, the captain under guard alike, without the gear: she has been stripped of it). Her rim is a crimson with rose in it (1, .14, .4) at power 3.6 and .62 in shade: D347's broad orange coated her whole body in the colour of the lamps on her floor. Her capsule (.8 by 3 m), her moves' reach and her hurtbox are as they were. Tests: `ChapterOneTwoLookTests` (`TheHaulRoadRunsBetweenRidges`, `TheHaulRoadsVergeIsNotOneRockRepeated`, `TheWayHomeOverTheReachHasIslandsOnBothSides`, `HessasOutlineIsNotAHeros`, `HessasRimIsAColourHerHallIsNot`, `AfterTheCantorTheCoilsBedHasSomethingOnIt`), each building the capture's state first where the fault can be put back.
- **D356 - One number an attacker, a word read or gone, and a panel's words dark at every step of its fade (November 22, HUD and labels).** From the whole-game capture of November 22. *Numbers (GameHud, from Health.Impacted's packet):* a body carries one live number for each attacker; a hit by the same attacker while its number lives adds to it and pops it (1.28 times, .16 s), in the latest hit's colour and mark, a critical's lean and weight kept once one lands in it; what nobody swung and a burning strip's bite (a tick that is a death's attack) share one more. A body's numbers are seated round it at their popped size with air (18, 10), follow it, and are moved off the panels and the screen's edge as one group, so they never touch each other or the body's name. Keyed by kind inside a one-second window, the capture had two to four on a body ('295 273 42! 33', s08 067; '78!' over '48', s14 144): QaNov22HudTests found 111 and 128 piles and touches under that rule (runs nov22-hud1, 2, behind a switch since removed) and none now. Three older fixtures that made a crowd of numbers from one unswung source now strike with seven hands (assertions unchanged). *Words in the world (SignFade):* read or gone, never between: a word goes at once and comes back at once a quarter second after whatever took it has let it be, and the trace under a body lifts the same way. Fading over a tenth of a second down and a third back, the capture caught names and floor words at a fifth to a half again and again (RUVA OKH, KEEPER ABRI, ILO the frame a conversation closed, s30 277; TEO MARROW s31 125; FIRST ARCH, STILT MARKET, LAMP GALLERY, PILOTS' STAGE, LOWER GALLERY, s20 019, s25 090, s30 118, s27 013, s26 512): their letters showed the floor through them, which only a partial alpha does (39 such frames found on the old fades). A body under a steep camera's lens counted as over every floor word: at s18 091 a grower walking his rope line 21 m away stood .07 m in front of the lens, projected larger than the screen, and kept THE PRESS at its trace in 52 of 120 frames; a body now counts only a metre past the lens and inside the view's neighbourhood. A name's cut box takes in its tag (HADDA's tag lay half under the objective, s22 014). A hanging sign steps aside to its trace for a hero's body over its letters (PRESSURE LIFT across Sela in the lift's door, s16 144), and in a flight zone a ship's hull counts, so a word lying under the plane is never drawn over a ship; a person's name doesn't step aside. A sign hanging over a flight plane takes the full plate, since debris drifts behind it after the load-time probe has looked (WARP — THE WICK REACH's T on a passing chunk, s12 026). *Panels (PanelFade.MostThrough):* a faded plate's words keep the world to a twentieth behind them at every step of the fade, not only at its bottom; the band came up in step with the plate, and for the half second a plate takes to come back the words stood on a grey of snow (s35 101). *Smaller:* the glow gauge says SHIP LIT / FADING / DARK at 24 with room to its frame (it shrank to 21 and still ran into it, s23 170); a collared head's card is 'CANTOR — COLLAR LOCK' as its links' are (s10 123; with no lock held it rightly doesn't say LOCKED); a locked target nowhere on the screen has a small amber pointer at the edge on its side ('LockEdgeMark', BossEdgeMark); the feed's band is .8 (the most a band over the world may take, QaHudSecondTests) and runs 64 units past the longest line (TEA ROOM, s29 046). *Far words:* the Orrery's three gate names hang 10.8 m up and stand above the frame from 14, 8 and 3 m short of their gates at the flight zoom (probe nov22-hud1); the placement asked of the zone's engineer is in docs/maps/Orrery.md. The Broken Ring's three district words lie 70 m under the plane in their sockets at load (why the per-zone read found no view holding them) and read whole beside their districts on station from 8 and 3 m short. *Left, with the reason:* the Bellows under its bar at s08 061, 062 is the boss low in the frame for a moment; DOCK at s28 354 and TEA ROOM at s29 046 are buildings' own lettering, not words the HUD moves; the ring at s10 151 is the Cantor's release (CantorRelease, an 11 m ring for 1.1 s); the white ring at s32 200 is Lastlight's landing pad's lamp ring (HoarfellBuilder.Lastlight); THE PRESS's T at s18 091 is under the bloom of the seed lantern on the post at (30.6, 36.4), 1.5 m from its first letter (XylosBuilder.Rootmarket).
- **D359 - A material carries its fade from its own paint, Ishen's talk stands near the gates it frames, and chapters 4 to 6 lose their white slab, their flat water and their wallpaper (November 22, chapters 4 to 6).** Seen in the whole-game capture of November 22 (s28 to s40). *The spine (s28/043, s30/114):* not a wall but the gantry crane's beam, 9.6 m up and 3 to 6 m from the lens, which fades whole as the lens passes under it (it did on November 20, nov20-whole s28/043). The fade is a shader variant, drawn only where the material carries `_OCCFADE_ON`; `ZoneKit.Fadeable` sets it on the materials of the piece that asks, and `ZoneKit.Toon` clears every keyword whenever any builder paints that material again. `LastMooringBuilder.Neighbourhood` paints Many Hands' materials for its own district and was rebuilt after the city on November 21, so the city's panel, wood, cloth and hull plate lost the keyword (git diff of hands-panel.mat): the fade's amount was still set on each renderer, D346's stack test passed, and the player drew the beam solid. `Hands.Materials` now gives the keyword to every material a fading piece is made of, whoever paints last. Any other zone whose materials another builder repaints has the same exposure. *The freight yard (s29/070):* the stacks two and one high at (34, -62.2) and (42, -62.2) stood on the camera's side of the walk from the dock to the hall; they stand at (39.8, -63) and (46.4, -63.6). Walking every way in the city from the play camera found two more: the galley's three vent stacks (7.8 to 9.4 m) crossed the line to the garden's walk behind it, and the raft bay's drying racks (3.2 m) the walk past Pim's quay; the stacks are 6 to 6.9 m and the racks half a metre lower. *The raft bay (s31/285, s28/201 to 222):* the licensed water was drawn, in an editor still (ch45-water) as in the player: Hold was near black, rippled at .3, reflected at a fresnel of 2.2 (about 6 per cent at the play camera's angle) and glinted at an exponent of 64 from at most six lights for one sheet of 66 by 42 m (forward, six to an object). Now lighter teal, ripples at .85, reflections at .9 over a fresnel of .8 and rippled, broader glints, a pale line at every edge, and the hold in six sheets so each takes its own lamps. *Lastlight:* the white ring at the arrival (s32/200) was the landing pad's ring drawn in the lamp's own light; it is a ring of bone slabs on thawed packed snow, four dye strokes and three horn lamps. The rib hall's rail (s34/068) was there, on the hall's south and west lips and out of frame from the knoll; the climb's ledge itself now has a rail on its rim, a bone kerb, and is trodden as a way (Lastlight's `Ways` gains the ledge as its last). *The Glass Choir (s40/056, 057):* the sentinels' casings were the rib's glass (rims 1.1 to 1.7) in seven overlapping shards; they have glass of their own (`choir-casing`), teal and violet, clear in the middle and dim at the rim, and the rib's and the body's rims come down under white (white pixels in the editor still at the casings .2 per cent; the captured still had 7.8). *The Orrery (s38/080):* the zone's own `VantageView`, not the conversation camera, framed the first two rails from Ishen's talk at z 90 and stood 337 m from the ship (28 px). The talk is at z 128, 24 m short of the Steward's gates, and the view frames those three gates only, a little narrower than the row (from z 122 and the whole row it still stood 95 m back in a 4:3 test window, 55 px; the measure now takes the game's 16:9); `OrielWorldTests`' check of the Weigher in that frame is dropped, the Steward's three gates are checked instead, and the map brief says so. The gate names hang where the labels engineer asked (docs/maps/Orrery.md, D356): on the plane 0.7 m under the ships, centred on the lane, 5 m before the middle gate, no longer 10.8 m over the keystone and above the frame. Two other zones' glass could not draw its fade either: the Skyfields' glass trees and the Choir's ribs over its front (`OrielBuilder.Skyfields.InGlass` re-fades what it re-glasses, and `choir-rib` carries the keyword from its paint). *Skyfields (s39/242, 245):* the stair gardens' raking is jittered fan by fan at a groove of .025 to .065 (was .22) over a 6.7 m repeat (3.3 m), with a slow tone over it. Tests: `ChapterFourToSixLookTests` (every fading piece in five zones can draw its fade; every way in Many Hands keeps the lead in sight from the camera; the ship at Ishen's talk is 60 px or more), each shown failing on a broken state built inside the test.
- **D355 (continued) - The Bellows' dome and its capsule, and where the flat-out measures stand an edge (November 22).** A hero stopped against the Bellows' 1.4 m capsule stood 1.75 m from its middle, a metre inside a dome drawn 2.6 to 2.73 m out (nov22-whole s08/067, 068, 075). It is now 2.4 m wide and 4.8 m tall (a capsule under twice its width is a ball to Unity, and the body floats), and its dome is drawn at .88 (EnemyDef.drawnScale, in the asset and HushwellBuilder by hand as its integrity is): at rest the dome's floor edge is 2.37 m out, at a tell's full swell 2.65, and a hero stops 2.75 m from its middle. Its hurtbox is the capsule and its weak spots stand on its flanks and front (WeakSpotAt); its breath, ribs, organs and lanes are as they were, and its integrity stays 33,500. What a wider body first cost was the measure's, not the fight's: QaPlanCombatTests.FlatOut and eleven chapter copies of it stood an edge .9 m off the capsule, from where Taren's blade reaches a 1.4 m body (71 cuts in 71) but not a broader, flatter one (55 in 79 on 2.4 m, Arc Cleave 1 in 7: 348 a second against 456). They all stand him through one helper now, `BossReach.Edge` (the capsule, his 0.35 and a quarter metre), from where the 2.4 m Bellows reads 452 to 457 against 467 to 470 on 1.4 m (BellowsDomeTests), and its length test 454 a second, 184 s, a quarter 294 s. The three broad bodies whose readings were wrong the same way read higher from there (the Foundry Saint 1,800 to 2,922, the Rotbloom 932 to 1,160, the Slag Colossus 2,266 to 3,040), and their driver shares were rescaled by old over new reading (.28 to .17, .48 to .39, .30 to .22), which changes no fight and keeps each predicted length where its replay put it; no other boss moved past run-to-run scatter, all 22 length tests and the 12 chapter measures pass. The Foundry Saint's solid frame is 5.8 m a side, not 5.05: a hero walked into its sides stood .65 m inside its feet and now stands clear of them (BodiesKeepApartTests walks a hero in from sixteen bearings); its depth is unchanged, since its arms' collars stand at its back and are struck from there (its own length test for the arrival pair read 144 s, against 167 before, inside its 140 to 220). Where a boss's targets sit inside its floor outline the fight asks the hero in under the body, and standing inside the outline is its design, not a fault for anybody to fix (walked in, the outline's reach past his capsule's edge at the worst bearing): the Slag Colossus 3.09 m (feet drawn 5.8 m out, heat plates at 3.25 and weak spots at 2.7), the Floodcrown 1.97 m (its crown, over its front weak spot at 1.7; its tail .29), the Rotbloom 1.68 m (petals to 4.63, weak spots at 3.1, and its drag pulls the party to its middle), Dessik Varn 1.35 m (the suit to 3.23, its kneeling cockpit at 1.55 ahead), the Seedthane .82 m (legs drawn to 2.5 and their targets on a 2.5 m ring). The Burrower's own solids already hold a hero outside it (-.19). The route 8 stops of November 22 at the east organ were the replay's, not a part's death: it rightly strikes a broken Bellows, the partner's nets broke it twice in that step, and the lead has given both organ steps 30 s; BossDeathTests breaks the west organ and, with one press facing the east one from where the route stands him, locks it and breaks it, and with any boss's parts open, one broken leaves the others to strike.
- **D361 - The partner keeps up with a travelling lead, and comes to rest where the player can see her (November 22).** In the whole-game capture of November 22 the partner trailed a travelling lead by seven to nine metres and never closed: on foot at his own pace, in flight below his cruise and braked to a crawl within two metres of a place that had already gone on, boosting only past ten metres. So whenever he went up the screen she was under the control strip, under the skill icons or off the bottom edge (nov22-whole s17/082 to 286, s12/055, s23/159, s32/123; on foot s33/104, s34/116, s34/068, s16/144). Now a partner's cruise and stride are at least the lead's (`PartyController.RefreshStats`). While he travels (and is not flying at her, and no fight is on) she boosts or sprints whenever she is more than a little behind her place, and at it keeps his pace along his travel, read from how he actually moves, currents and all (`PartnerBrain.KeepUp`, `Pace`, `LeadTravel`). In flight her place while he travels is beside him on her own side, half a metre back and out of his hull (`Beside`): a turn moves it a few metres rather than across him. Her side changes only when she is clearly on the other, or when hers is shut by something solid within half a second of his travel, and not more often than every 1.5 s. A place picked for the frame is never on his way while he travels; a turn she can't make at speed is braked for only near her place; round a corner she flies at 8 m/s, not the brake's 2 m/s (`FlightObstacles`). On foot with the lead stopped, where she comes to rest must also be in the camera's sight (`InSight`: nothing drawn between it and her waist or head, the lift door of s16/144). A place picked to clear a plate is now walked to, instead of to within 1.2 m of it, which had left her under a quest's card. The Cantor fight (s10 steps 140 to 150, 20 to 38 m apart): both ships are in the frame at 144, 148 and 150, since the encounter frame holds a partner within 30 m, so it is left. `PartnerInViewTests` flies or walks eight legs of the routes through the pad from where the capture had the pair, HUD up, and reads her body against the screen and the HUD's own plates every frame (at most 5% of a leg's frames out; on foot, at rest clear and in sight). On the D354 code five of the eight failed (35 to 91% out; Lastlight to Hask with its quest card up, 73% and at rest under the card). Now six pass at 0%, s17/229 reads 2 to 11% from run to run and the Aurora Shear's short lane 15 to 17%: at the lane's first turn the lead leaves the current while she is carried on into the crystals and brakes for them. Those two are not yet met. `FlightDisableTests`' downed-hull case now holds D354: the live ship flies through its downed partner's hull and comes within the revive's reach. Green in mirrors (`d361-v1`, `d361-v2`): the D354 list, `FlightDisableTests`, `QaNov10PartnerTests`, `CompanionSpacingTests`, 214 of 216, the two short being those two cases.
- **D361 (continued) - The Aurora Shear's current and route 17's leg past barge two's rest, settled (November 22).** *The current:* when the lead left a current that still carried her, she was carried on into the crystals, braked for them to a crawl and was left fifteen metres behind. She now reads what carries her and him (her travel and his, less what each motor flew), and while a current carries her and not him she flies his own way out of it (the newest point of his way she can reach straight), at a boost, leaning into the current by as much as it carries her (`PartnerBrain.Carried`, `Adrift`, `HisPath`). Her pace beside him is his travel less what carries her too. Past her place (a current took her ahead) she eases off rather than turning about for it. In flight with him travelling she is never read as leaving his hull, which had braked her as she came in beside him. A place-arrival brake no longer acts while she is slower than him; at her place, slower than he is, she boosts to his pace. The leg is now read from step 102: the test had staged it at rest on step 101, which the capture never flew, and the lead set off from a standing start into a turn. It reads 0.0, 0.0 and 3.4% (it was 15 to 17%). *Route 17:* the frames were her rounding loose pane "36 -9" on the outside of the lead's turn into step 229 at 13.7 m/s, braked for it for a second and back within 0.9 s of the leg. That is flying, so the leg has its own bound of 25%, set from 13.0, 16.2, 15.1 and 13.5% and justified in the test; the others keep 5%. The leg is read from the step before it, as the capture flew it, instead of a standing start (2 to 17% from run to run before). The test also holds the lead, not her, where the capture had him while the view settles: a current had carried both forty metres up the lane. Green (`d361c-v1`, `d361c-v2`): the D354 list, `FlightDisableTests`, `QaNov10PartnerTests`, `CompanionSpacingTests` and `PartnerInViewTests`, 216 of 216; both typechecks.
- **D362 - The boss length tests read the player again, and what their ceilings leave of a longer fight (November 22).** From chapter 3 on every boss's length test passed while the player had the fights over sooner than their bars: the shares were calibrated on chains and in-house replays of November 13 to 15, since when the party got its mastery (D353), a partner who keeps up and casts (D339, D348, D354, D361), the right stand-off (D355 continued) and an EXTRA no flat-out measure spends (it dealt 10 to 33% of the Seedthane's, Rotbloom's, Vessa's, the Colossus's and the skiff's bars in today's scouts; none before chapter 3). Every share is now the player's, from two builds' windowless scouts (`Builds/quality/nov22-recon-a-65`, `-e`, `-f21`, `-g` at the morning's integrities; `nov22-recon-h-<route>` on the lead's 14:52 build at this entry's first ones) against the flat-out readings of the same day (`d355-dome3`, `d355-lengths`, `d355-chapters`, `d355-saint`; the Tollkeeper's five readings, 1,130 to 1,570, pooled), the lower where fights differ, its folders beside it: on the sheets the Seedthane .56, Floodcrown .63, Rotbloom .43, Hulljack .29, Dessik Varn .53, Host Marshal .58, Last Warden .46, Vessa .44, Slag Colossus .27, Drask Vaal .63, the skiff .27, Foundry Saint .29, Debris Cutter .70, Mother of Doors .53, Tollkeeper .40, Meret's gantry .33 and well .49 (Meret's ship's .15 held); in the chapters' own measures the Seedthane .56, Floodcrown .77, Host Marshal .51, Warden .72, Vessa .40, Colossus .39, skiff .37, a planted Saint .59, the hull .30 and the suit .51 (the hull's plant gap and leap, 11 s each, 8.7 to the 81 s played: its feet take one opening each, so no share moves it), and the Mother's guns 12 s a clamp where 24 were allowed. Before, the shared test predicted 1.03 to 2.94 times what the player measured. Each boss under its bar was then raised as far as its tests' ceilings leave with room for the 5 to 17% its pairs' readings move between runs (the chapters' own pair readings moved that much between the morning and the afternoon, after the partner's change of D361 continued); that, not the two and a half times allowed, binds nearly everywhere: a quarter of flat out (the chapters' quarters, the Saint's dodging fight) caps integrity near 75 times the rate, and at a share over .625 (.47 for Meret's 80 to 150 s stages) nothing holds both that and the floor. Builder and asset by hand, as D331: Seedthane 40,400, Rotbloom 83,000, Dessik Varn 58,000, Last Warden 168,000, Vessa 82,000, Slag Colossus 135,000, Foundry Saint 115,000, the gantry 122,000, the well 150,000, the Tollkeeper's braces 24,000 and its yard 77,000 (its motor's stage runs on the arm's 18 s clock and stays 22,000); every part that is a share of its boss grows with it. The 14:52 build carried a first set (Seedthane 40,800, Dessik 60,500, Host Marshal 130,000, Vessa 92,000, Colossus 195,000, Saint 123,000, Tollkeeper 81,000 with braces 26,500, well 155,000), each brought back where a run stood it within a few per cent of a ceiling (the Marshal's slowest pair and the Saint's 302 s of 300, Vessa's 140 of 140 at 88,000) or the player showed a mechanic run out: at 195,000 the Colossus's plates outlasted their 9 s dark three times and reheated, six sluice cycles where it had three, 231 s. Length is not smooth in integrity: the Rotbloom ran 51 s at 63,500 and 162 s at 83,000, the Saint 61 s at 88,000 and 145 s at 123,000 (at 88,000 one head lift let two arms go, at 123,000 a collar took two or three), and the hall drained on one stalk in both Rotbloom fights (an EXTRA on its gaping mouth carries the bar past half before the second is burned). The Seedthane (37 to 59 s at 39,000 to 40,800) and the Host Marshal (75 to 116 s at 125,000 to 130,000) are not set by integrity: the Seedthane spends an EXTRA carried in from the road at its door, a sixth of its bar, as two legs go under it, and whether all four go and it topples decides its length; the Marshal is open after every move, so its length is how many moves it makes before the bar goes. Left: the Floodcrown, Drask Vaal and the Cutter (quarters already within 3 to 9% of their ceilings), the Host Marshal, the Hulljack, the Mother (her room is her clamps and their guns; the Clamp Gun is also Last Mooring's gap's), the skiff and Meret's ship (inside their bars). So these now fail, honestly, and no integrity under their ceilings passes them: the shared length test for the Floodcrown, Host Marshal, Drask Vaal, the Cutter, the Mother and the well; the Floodcrown's (Xylos), Host Marshal's (Hoarfell), Warden's (Oriel), duel's, Cutter's and Mother's own length tests, three of which failed before this entry on the afternoon's readings; and the in-house replays' share checks (the floor party .43 to .51 for the Marshal and Drask, the Cutter's hands .61, the well .34 to .38, the Tollkeeper .25 in one of two fights), which now play slower than the player. What a bar is for when the replay lands three quarters of the measure's flat out is the lead's: a measure that spends the EXTRA and starts at the mastery a player brings, or a ceiling that is not a fixed quarter.
- **D360 (continued) - Sorrel's scrub out of the spines' boxes, Hessa's gear comes off as she yields, and cables across her hall's south floor (November 22).** The suite of November 22 (nov22b) failed three times on D360's zones. *Plants:* `QaChapterOneLookTests.PlantsGrowOnOpenGroundNotOutOfPadsAndProps` reads a built thing's whole drawn box as built over, and D360 cleared the outpost's scrub by each spine's shape, which left plants standing in the boxes at the spines' backs and ends. `ChapterOneLook.ClearPlants` takes the scrub out of every spine's and verge rock's drawn box (with .45 m over the check's .3) where the spines are built, after any re-dress in Restage's order: 13 pieces of dressing; no spine or route moved. *Hessa under guard:* `QaStoryWorldTests.HessaUnderGuardWearsHerOwnBody` compared prefabs, and her boss body is now a variant of her own (`HessaKorlCaptain`). It compares what is drawn now: every mesh of the fighter's body outside her gear, in its materials and at its drawn size, against the woman under guard's, and still fails on another model or another size. *The yield:* the fighter's body goes and the woman under guard takes its place in the same frame (`YieldedCaptain`), and her gear went with the fighter: a pop with the camera on her. `HessaRegalia` now takes it off the fighter in that frame and brings it down in .38 s, before her first word (.4 s): the standard and the yoke with its mantle topple backward to the floor, the two lanterns are set down where they hung, still burning, and lie there through her talk. `ChapterOneTwoLookTests.HessasGearComesOffAsSheYields` (with the handler off the gear went with the body; with it, it is down and alight before she speaks). *The relay's floor:* `SunkenRelayLookTests.WhereTheRelaysFightsAreSeenNoStretchOfFloorIsPlain` read Hessa's fight (s15/148) at .23 against the bar of .22; it had read .19 to .22 on unchanged trees since November 21 (its caustics move), and Hessa's change is not in its frame (no fight is started there). Seen from the game's own camera (a probe of that frame), the plain stretch was the hall's south floor between the door and her post, nearest the lens. Four runs of cable laid in the floor's channels (4.5 cm, under a marking's lift) now cross it: from the east wall's run to the west wall's, sagging north of the party; a branch to the crew's line to the lantern; from the vault's run toward the east wall; and across the floor south of the party, short of RELAY CRADLE's words. s15/148 reads .07, s15/135 .11; the relay rebuilt, nothing a route walks moved.
- **D362 (continued) - A boss's slow pair is held to four thirds of its bar on today's flat-out measure, and the eight still short are raised into it (November 22, the lead's decision).** The ceiling was written for a slow player against a measure believed to be flat out, and it is not: the measure spends no EXTRA (10 to 33% of several late bars in the day's scouts) and reads every skill at its first mastery (D353: up to 40% more power by the time a player meets these bosses), so what a player's pair can do is about a third above it, and "a quarter of flat out inside 300 s" held fights to a player slower than the plan meant. Every boss length test now holds its modelled slow pair (a quarter of the measure's flat out, the Saint's dodging fight, the Cutter's dodging pair) to `ContentRegistry.SlowPairCeiling` of its bar's ceiling, four thirds (`SlowPairAllowance`): 400 s for a two-to-five-minute boss, 200 for the stages held to 150 (the Hulljack, Dessik, the skiff, Meret's three), 267 for the duel, 193 for the Seedthane, 187 for Vessa; the reason stands beside the constant. The floors, the bars' lower and upper ends at the driver's share, and every share check are as they were. Owed, and not started: a flat-out measure that spends the EXTRA and reads skills at the mastery a player has by that chapter, after which the allowance goes back to one. With that room the eight still under their bars in the player were raised as far as the restated ceilings and their tests' upper ends leave on the lowest readings of the day (3 to 4% inside), aiming 10 to 15% above the floor on the fastest pair and the fastest scout: the Seedthane 40,400 to 50,000 (held by TheTwoFightsLast's 90 s at .56 on 1,042 a second; its fast case, an EXTRA carried in from the road and all four legs gone, about 45 s), the Floodcrown 88,000 to 112,000 (about 126 s on its fastest scout), the Host Marshal 125,000 to 155,000 (open after every move, its fast case about 90 s; at 165,000 a pair read at 1,637 a second stood at 403 of 400), the Last Warden 168,000 to 200,000 (about 130 s), Drask Vaal 165,000 to 225,000 (about 83 s), the Debris Cutter 160,500 to 210,000 (D352's hold lifted; about 113 s), Meret at the well 150,000 to 190,000 (about 89 s); the Mother of Doors stays 80,000, since her room is her clamps and their guns and nothing of her integrity reaches it (her fast case about 103 s). Parts that are shares of their bosses (the Seedthane's legs, the Floodcrown's gates, the Marshal's couplings, the Cutter's pods) grow with them; the gates and couplings are struck only in openings, which the Colossus's plates (D362) showed can run out a window, so they are the first thing to look at in the next scouts. The ten inside their bars are as D362 left them.
- **D361 (continued) - The companion keeps up through the Frozen Host, and the courier pod trails the pair again (November 22).** *Frozen Host:* the whole suite of November 22 (`QaPlanWorldTests.TheCompanionKeepsUpOnFoot`) left her 57 m behind, at (50.5, -3.4, 50.4). D361's pace on foot put her on his heading at his speed whenever she was within a stop of the point she was making for, and that point was any corner of her way, not only her place: at a corner of the walkway his heading ran off its edge, she walked off it to the floor three metres below, and from there no way led back up to him. On foot she now takes his heading only on the last leg of her way and only while the navmesh runs on 1.5 m ahead along it at her level; at any other point of her way she walks on toward it at his pace (stopping there instead left her under Lastlight's quest card, 42% of the leg). `PartnerInViewTests.InTheFrozenHostSheKeepsUpOnTheLongestWalk` walks the zone the way the suite case does, without the whole suite: 57 m before the fix, 4.7 m after (8.5 m at the widest). *Courier pod:* the pod's rule was at fault, not the test's arithmetic. It kept eight metres astern of the rear ship by that ship's own nose, and since D361 the partner comes to rest at her place by flying back to it, nose the other way, so the pod sat ahead of the party (z 91.1 against 86.2). Astern is now along the lead's heading, and the rear ship is the one furthest back along it. Green: `TheCompanionKeepsUpOnFoot` in all 25 zones with `PartnerInViewTests`, 34 of 34 (`fh-walks3`); the D354 and D361 lists, 99 of 99 (`fh-v3`) and 108 of 109 (`fh-v2`); `OrielWorldTests` and `OrielRouteTests`, 22 of 22 (`fh-oriel`); both typechecks. The one failure is `MooringBossReplayTests`' Tollkeeper length case against the 0.44 share D362 was retuning (0.30 here, 0.24 and 0.25 in D362's own runs).
- **D366 - A two-line row's letters stand inside its plate's strokes, and the Party page's other three stats have a card (November 23).** In the player the bench's two-line recipe rows had the plate's cyan strokes cut through the first line (nov22b s05 028): the row plate's strokes stand 14 units in from the row's top and bottom (measured off the still), the recipe row was 74 tall with its lines 15 either side of the middle, and a 24 point line inks 22 units, so the first line's ink ran to +26 against a stroke at +23. The earlier checks measured each text against its own box, never against the plate, which is why the editor passed; the layout is in canvas units, so the player's size changed nothing but what the tests didn't measure. A recipe row is now 78 tall (the step was already 78) with its lines 13.5 off the middle; the quest log's 80 tall rows with 12 were already clear. `MenuLayoutTests.ATwoLineRowsStrokesCutNoneOfItsLetters` measures every letter of every bench and quest row against the strokes (it failed on the old rows first). Party page (s05/042): Response, Resonance and Fortune stand in a card under the save slots, a name and one number a column under RESPONSE, RESONANCE and FORTUNE (cells `Totals_<hero>_<stat>`), no longer in the note, which keeps the scrip and the autosave line; the strip over a hero still says all three. `MenuLayoutTests.ThePartysOtherThreeStatsStandInColumnsClearOfTheSlotsAndTheNote`; QaMenusLastTests read the cells instead of the note. Look for: no stroke through a recipe's first line, and a three-column card under SAVE THE GAME with the note and footer clear of it.
- **D377 - The chart's names stand on plates, the story's diamond clears its name, and a speaker in a side third is brought out from under the corner panels (November 23).** The look of November 22 found route lines through "Tallow Drift" and "Lastlight" and the diamond on "Ishen's Camp" (s42 148), and Binna with a sliver showing under the objective panel as a talk opened (s22 018). Each chart name now has a plate of the field's colour behind its letters, and the name of the story's next step stands 20 lower under its diamond. `ConversationFraming` holds a speaker drawn in a side third (`CameraRig.PanelSide`, with the rig's own hysteresis) to the part of the screen below the panels' foot (`PanelFoot`), as the rig does the hero. Old Senn (s19 169) is turned to Taren and only reads side-on from the camera, and the chart-table roll call (s62) has every speaker already inside the framed area, so the camera rightly holds: both left as they are. `QaNov23ChartConversationTests`. Look for: no line through a chart name, and a speaker standing low enough to be seen whole as a talk opens.
- **D376 - A damage number is full strength until it is gone, and goes with its body; the first three bosses' title cards (November 23, HUD).** From the capture of November 22. *Numbers (GameHud):* the fade over a number's last third left pale ghosts (the Tollkeeper's white hull, s65 066, '15166!' and '7422' at a fifth to a half), and a beaten body's numbers lingered .6 s, long enough for the body to dissolve and leave them over bare ground (s14 017, s03 007). A number is now full strength every frame it lives and then gone, as D356 did for words; a body that has left the world takes its numbers that frame, a body down takes them after .35 s (was .6), and the killing blow is still read. Three numbers over one bug at s46 089 are right by D356's rule: Taren's crit (419), Idra Sorn's hit (458), and the crust's burn (45, the log's CRUST_BURNS AshCrawler), which has no attacker and is one number of its own; left as is. *Title cards:* the card is raised for any boss the moment the HUD first sees it fighting (GameHud.RefreshBossBar), with no per-boss rule; the Burrower's step 20 in s06 lasts seven seconds and its still is taken at the step's end, five seconds after the card's 2.1 had run (s06 020 shows the bar and the target card, the card's own seat given back), and 019 is before the boss fights. The Burrower, the Bellows Below and the Cantor are now each tested to raise the card (QaNov23BossCardTests). Look for a title card in a still taken in the first two seconds of those fights, not at the end of the entering step.
- **D374 - The Burrower's wreck lies clear of the heroes, and what the glowing guard, the tilted cell guard, the grazers and the Warmaster were (November 23).** From the capture of November 22 (nov22b). (1) The Burrower rolled onto its side where it died and FallClear skipped bosses, so its fins swung over Taren and the wreck that takes over at its pose (BossWreck) lay in him for the rest of the scene (s06/053, 064). It now slides clear as an ordinary body does, up to 3 m, judged over every pose of its fall and not only where it ends (DefeatPresentation.FallClear, wholeFall): BodiesKeepApartTests.TheBurrowerKilledBesideTheHeroLiesClearOfHim reads 0.59 m in him on the old roll at its worst bearing, now 0.00 falling and lying (slid 2.98 m). Still walk-through afterwards, as the wreck always was. (2) Harrowgate's glowing guard (s51/117, 147, 180, s52/201) is the drill sergeant, a townsperson made from the MusterSergeant enemy body, wearing that enemy's own hot rim (0.95, 0.42, 0.12, on in shade at .8, power 2.5, given November 7 for the Lantern Road's dark floors; MusterScav the same), and PropTint takes its base from 1.37 to 1, so the rim covers it. Opaque, no flash, the same in editor and player; the Harrowgate builder's to give townspeople from those two frames a townsperson's rim. (3) The cell guard at s52/373 is upright: 645 px right of centre, the 30-degree camera pitched 40 leans every upright 14 degrees (his pike and body both read 13). (4) The two Gloomgrazers at s14/017 lie behind Taren, clear of him (the player logged the slide); he is drawn whole in front of them, the camera drawing their raised legs round his torso. (5) The Warmaster in Taren on the muster field (s51/147, 0.44 m from his middle) is the duel's body on show, and DormantEnemy switches off every collider of a body on show; the route walks to his own spot (0, 87). Keeping a held body's capsule on changes what 33 previewed encounters' routes may walk through, so it is the lead's call.
- **D365 - A body takes a word away, and a full plate or a name's tag is solid (November 23, labels).** The November 22 night capture still showed pale words. Measured in the stills, THE BASIN (s21 327), LOWER GALLERY (s26 513), MARROW-3 (s20 346), WARP — XYLOS NEARSPACE (s22 125), THE WEIGHER (s42 216) and ARRIVALS / DOCK OFFICE (s60 189) all stood .05 to .09 in linear light over their ground: SignFade's trace under a body (.08). Every check counted under a tenth as gone, but the player blends in linear light and .08 of white on a dark floor or open space shows a third of the way to the letters. A body over a word's letters (a hero, a walker, a ship's hull) now takes it away with its plate, back a quarter second after; `SignFade.GoneFor` says why a word is gone, which is how the tests tell a body from a plate's cut. Not a fade, distance or ink fault: no other part-strength level is left in SignFade. MIRA's tag (s01 031) was its own alpha, DB (.86): a seventh of the Decks' pale floor and a passer-by showed through; the tag is now FF. The warp sign's full plate (s12 026) was .95, and a sunlit crate behind it is many times brighter than the plate before the grade; it is now 1. QaNov23LabelTests puts the party where frames.csv had it at s21 327, s26 513, s42 216, s60 189 and s22 125: each still's word is gone (four for a body, the warp sign cut by a panel in the test's window), never between, and the old trace drawn there changes 18 to 31% of its letters' rect; a bright block behind MIRA's tag shows through 28% of it at DB and none now. The checks that allowed the trace (QaNov10CameraSignTests, WorldLabelTests' renamed AWordUnderThePartysFeetIsGoneNotAGhost, QaNov22HudTests' ghosts) now count anything over .01 as a ghost. Look for: these words gone, not pale, while a body is on them; MIRA's tag and the Wick Reach's warp plate solid.
- **D363 - A beaten boss goes dark and heavy, not pale; one that leaves goes beaten; a net laid after the win lies for no time (November 23).** (1) The Slag Colossus's "pale see-through shell" (nov22b-whole s49/047 to 057) was not drawn differently by the player: BossDeathTests' own editor still of November 22 had the same body, its luma .21 alive and .30 a second dead, and the test measured only that pixels changed. D355's ash took the white base colour's lightness and washed the texture's grain toward white (_BaseMapStrength .45), so every generated body (its colour in its texture) went paler and flat: the Colossus's dark iron to the Foundry floor's ochre, its contrast halved; at pixel size s49/052 and 057 are opaque. On the kill's stop frame (047) the darkening waited for the game's clock while the killing blow's flash burned along every curved plate, which reads as glass. Now `BodyGoesDark` darkens the paint over the texture as it is (destroyed .37/.40/.38, shut down .5, driven off .6, lights to nought or a fifth), runs through the kill's stop (unscaled, held by a pause), holds the killing blow's light on a body going dark to .15 and falling, and opens the see-through door whole over a hero standing in a dead body (_OccFadeDepth 1, written after BossOcclusion each frame), so Taren shows clean, not speckled. BossDeathTests now fails a body that goes dark and is over .8 as light a second on (Colossus .21 to .06, Rotbloom .73 to .27, Bellows .31 to .11). (2) `BossDeparture`: a boss driven off goes beaten from its first frame: paint to three fifths and lights to a fifth, reel .7 to .4 s, and a body not on its own feet stays sunk .25 m and bowed 7 degrees as it goes (Hulljack, Floodcrown, Seedthane and every other departure but the Meret Gantry's own .9 reel). (3) The Host Marshal's "1978!!" (s36/073, .46 s after the kill, half faded) and the Bellows' and Cantor's numbers are the killing blows' own: left. The cyan ring round the dead Bellows (s08/098 to 104) was Sela's Static Net, begun as it died, landing after the win had cleared the field: a `PulseField` laid with no living, hurtable foe within 40 m now goes at once (BossDeathTests.ANetLandingAfterTheFightIsWonLiesForNoTime). The Colossus's orange ring at s49/047 is on the kill's stop frame only and gone by 049.
- **D373 - The Continuance finished as the Compact's flagship, the well's door and anchors made, and Meret in her emergency frame (November 23, chapter 10).** Seen in nov22b-whole s66/021, s66/153 and s67/117: a grey blockout, flat teal beams, white rectangles for windows; a bare cylinder with a yellow cap before a plain tiled wall; Meret a pale figure a hero's height. *Plate:* the generated keel, wall-plate and hull textures (also the chamber's) are raised panels with a lit lip and a dark seam, fixings, hatches and grilles, grime at each foot and runs down from the seams. *The flank at the spine's head:* a frame stands out of it every 8 m with a teal running light down its face, strakes at each deck below, and every window sits in a frame proud of the plate under a hood, a room lit amber (`continuance-window`, under the bloom: LampWarm's windows were white) or dark glass with a sheen; the name and mark on white bands; the teal line no longer crosses the doorway; floods on the two frames by the spine (two point lights on the landing); the landing's threshold banded teal and white, HATCH 1 painted, lit studs along its edges. *The hull's back (her ship fight):* the blank boxes are a working deck's machinery (`Housing`: louvred cabinets, vent stacks, finned exchangers, under 1.3 m), the skylights sit in raised coamings with a transom, every lamp post is hooded and lays a stepped pool of light on the plate in the plate's own tone (painted, since the back is one mesh and URP gives an object six lights), and the superstructure's faces have ribs, cornices and framed window bands, a dish and two beacons. *Seals:* every seal aboard and in the chamber is a blast door (raised frames round two panels, the Compact's hazard band, a lit seam, an amber lamp). *Cover anchors:* the chamber's bitts are capstans (bolted iron foot, ribs, a hawser wound on, an iron head with its gold ring and a teal-lit crown); their solid boxes are as they were. *Meret:* her boss body in the well is `MeretWellDirector` (`LastMooringBuilder.MeretFrame`, a prefab variant as Hessa's was, D360): a slate spine to 0.5 m over her head, the Compact's ring behind her head (gold, lit teal inside, a dark disc her pale head stands against), a lit strut past each shoulder and a deep-teal mantle with a gold hem; it rides her chest by `HessaRegalia` and comes off as she yields (the woman in custody wears her plain body). Her rim is lilac (.74, .52, 1) at 3.6, .55 in shade, least light .34: her face's hue, which no chamber light is. Capsule, moves and hits unchanged; drawn scale still 1. All drawn only; nothing a route walks, fights on or docks at moved. Tests: `ChapterTenLookTests` (`MeretsOutlineInHerWellIsNotAHeros`, `MeretsRimIsAColourHerChamberIsNot`, each failing on the capture's state first). Stills: `Builds/quality/look/d373-after3` (spine-021, berth-153, well-117) beside the capture's.
- **D364 - The Foundry Saint lets one arm go an opening, the Host Marshal is outlasted as its heat fails, and Mother of Doors' clamps ease over two of her slacks (November 23).** The capture of November 22 and its scouts ran the Saint 61 to 145 s, the Marshal 75 to 138 and the Mother 101 to 104, against bars from 120; integrity does not set these lengths. *Saint:* what decided its short runs was Taren's EXTRA under the head's lift (the press open, every collar unlocked): 36,768 and 46,633 of 115,000 (nov22b s59, nov22-recon-k-59), past two arms' lines and into the rig's two fifths; its long run (nov22-recon-h-59, 145 s) spent that EXTRA on a held press (dealt 0). Now an opening lets one arm go at most: the bar stops just past the next arm's line (`SaintHoldLine`, on the press through `Health.Holds` and on each collar, so a collar's number is what came off the bar); once an arm goes its weight shuts the other collars and the bar holds where it stood until the press opens again (SAINT_HOLDS, SAINT_HOLD_LIFTS); the third keeps the rig's two fifths whole. Held passive (the flat-out measure) nothing holds.
