With an opening worth a fifth at most, 115,000 would have run about 95 s with the EXTRA spent well, so it is 150,000 (builder and asset by hand; about 370 s of the slow pair's 400). *Marshal:* open after every move, so its length was the moves before the bar went; its cold half ran 30 to 48 s, and an EXTRA in an opening (20,560 in nov22-recon-h-36) shortened the warm one. Its goal already says outlast it: in its room its bar now comes down no faster than its heat fails, from where it stood as the wall slept to nothing over `MarshalTune.HeatSeconds` (70, the frost ring's time; `MarshalHeat`); a yard holds nowhere. Integrity stays 155,000. *Mother:* her room was four quick cycles of guns, dock, one slack, launch, and nothing of her integrity reaches it. Each clamp of hers is now held through two of her slacks, a notch each (`BridleClamp.slacks`, 1 for the Last Warden's; `MotherTune.EaseSlacks`), so a shelf is worked through one of her moves between them: a notch stays while the hand is off and a tender at work winds it back (CLAMP_NOTCH, CLAMP_WOUND_BACK); her goal says hold it through her next slack. Player, windowless, on the 12:52 build (before the Saint's collar refinement): Saint 160.7, 180.8 and 193.5 s (nov23-recon-a-59, -b-59 s59 and s59b), Marshal 165.8 and 132.7 (its cold half about 70 s in both; the warm half differs by a second coupling broken by chance), Mother 134.9 and 130.9. The rejected Saint run missed round 5's checkpoint with the lead at work on the climb a whole round: the driver counts arrival only within 2.3 m of the press (the lead's to change). Tests: `HomeBossTests.AnOpeningLetsOneArmGoAndTheBarNoFurtherThanItsLine`, `HoarfellCombatTests.ColdInItsRoomItsBarComesDownNoFasterThanItsHeatFails`, `LastMooringBossTests.AClampOfHersIsHeldThroughTwoOfHerSlacksANotchEach`. Still to watch: the driver never seeks the Marshal's couplings (the bar says COUPLING, the target card Thaw coupling), and a party that breaks all three on purpose may finish the Marshal near 115 to 120 s.
- **D372 - The Terminator's rime lies in grains, a freed press's lamps are amber lamps in a rail, and the Nacre berth ring's band is Telegraph's to thin (November 23, chapters 8 and 9).** Seen in the whole-game capture of November 22. *The Terminator* (s55/152, s56/011): the see-through sheet printed with white squares was the frost in the crawler's shade (`ShadeFrost`, the Dawn shader's frost kind), not the steam, and the editor drew it too (look `ch8-dawn-as-played-2`): its glints were whole 11 cm cells lit by a hash of the cell number, and at z 740 that number times 456 is past where a float keeps its fraction, so the hash fell into a few values in rows and columns. `Lattice/Dawn` now folds a cell number small before hashing, and lays rime in grains at three sizes with the ground between, at about the old strength where it lies (look `d372-dawn-after2`). The cream strip at the top of s55/152 is the dawn's lit ground behind the line: by design, left. *The Separation Works* (s58/133, 171): the pale-yellow bars are a freed press's lamps (DrivenPress's local lamps, warm by design once its rig is cut; cyan is the driven state), one glass bar whose lit base added to its emission until red and green clipped (255 252 153); no editor still showed them, since a built scene has every press driven. They are now a row of lenses in an iron rail with a beacon in a cup at each end, the glass dark so the lamp's own colour shows (warm 255 177 56, cyan likewise toned), probe `HomeBuilder.WorksLampStills` (looks `d372-works-before`, `d372-works-after2`). Test `SeparationWorksTests.AFreedPressesLampsReadAsAmberLampsAndNotAFlatClippedBar`, failing on the old scene first (2341 of 2341 lamp pixels clipped, one run). *Nacre Grave* (s54/084): the orange hatched band is WreckTrack's danger marking (Telegraph lines, 9 m wide, laid in 6.8 m stretches), and nothing makes the wrecks transparent: they are old pearl hulls and riders lying below the flight plane, under the marking's wash and hatch. Telegraph sizes its hatch by each marking's own size (full strength at .62 m below 8 m, nearly gone past 22 m), so a lane laid in short stretches gets the small marking's full hatch along its whole length, over flight's black floor. Not changed here (Telegraph and its shaders are shared): the fights engineer would give `Telegraph.Line` the size of the lane a stretch belongs to for its hatch, which WreckTrack would pass. Rebuilt and restaged: SeparationWorks.
- **D371 - Harrowgate's streets are dressed and peopled, the Warmaster's post is laid stone and stops a hero, the garrison's frames in town wear a plain rim; the Foundry's plate shade is warm and its coolant deeper (November 23, chapter 8).** Seen in the whole-game capture of November 22 (s50/087, s51/136, s51/147, s51/180, s53/016, s48/075). *Streets:* `Town.Streets` (HarrowgateBuilder.TownStreets.cs) lays market stalls (posts, a striped awning in a house's colours with a scalloped valance, a counter of fish, bread, produce, cloth or ironware, stock behind, a keeper, a customer where there is room), provision carts, casks and crates and people talking, along the walked streets (the quay road on both sides, the cross street's south side, the avenue below the ramp, the ramp's head, the east gallery's square), each checked whole as placed (footprint 2.2 m off every way, off the prompts and their camera line, signs, errands, drill, the field and its kerbs, anything solid; tried a step along first, else left out and logged HARROWGATE_STREETS_SKIP), and flush iron gutters with grates along them; wear decals along the street edges. The painted lamp pools are gone and the street lamps carry no light of their own: by day a lamp's light laid a flat ochre disc on the paving. *The white rectangle* under the Warmaster was the medallion's "Warmaster's post", painted at tone .8: now the vanes' limestone with a groove round it. *His spot:* shown before the duel he is a dormant enemy with every collider off (Taren stood inside him, s51/147); "The Warmaster standing on his post" (a capsule r .75 and a non-carving obstacle at F) stands until `vask.fieldTaken`, and a declared way goes round him to the east (route 51's two walks through F take it). *The glowing drill sergeant:* townspeople in MusterSergeant or MusterScav frames wear a saved copy of the frame's material with the Pikeguard's rim ((.25, .25, .3), power 4, none in shade; `Town.PlainRim`). *Foundry:* the floor's and sand's shadow tint is a dimmed amber, and the coolant's shallow colour (.3, .5, .47, .72) where it was a pale (.46, .66, .62, .55); the small lavender patches beside the heroes' feet did not change with the floor's tint and are still open (see the report), and the "dark plate" box of s49/033 is BossController.SlagColossus's heat plate (a primitive cube), not D336's crust.
- **D369 - The Walking Canopy's hall trunk opens for the lead, and Driftreed's waterline runs round every raft (November 23, chapter 3).** *The hall trunk (nov22b-whole s21/198):* by design, left as it is. Both hall trunks are registered with the fade (`ZoneKit.Fadeable`: a `SceneryOcclusion` each in the scene) and their bark and vine carry `_OCCFADE_ON`; `SceneryOcclusion` opens its hole, or fades a piece whole, for the lead only (`PartyController.Active`). At 198 the lead, Taren, stands in the open and the trunk is between the camera and Sela, the companion, who is drawn as the pale outline, which is the companion's intended look behind scenery. Opening holes for the companion would change every zone's fades and was not taken before the capture. *Driftreed's rafts (s24/116, s24/178):* the licensed water drew its line only from scene depth, and the depth line reaches .64 m of water from where a raft's side goes in. From the fixed camera every side but the lens's hides that strip behind the knee-high rim; the first water seen past a north or east rim lies over about 1.6 m of water, so those edges met dark water (the near side of a raft, s27/098, always had its line). The Mire water sheet now carries a mask in its vertex red (`WendmireBuilder.RaftWaterline`), measured from each rim as the camera sees it (the raft's edge or that edge a metre toward the lens, whichever is nearer) and running .6 m past the waterline; Driftreed's patch takes a material of its own, `MireRafts` (the Mire look, `_IntersectionSource` 2: depth plus mask), because the Waystation's sheets have no vertex colour and keep `Mire`. At a reach of 1.2 m the line lay a metre and a half wide past the north rims, a beach and not a line (look run d369-driftreed); at .6 it is about as wide as the near side's (d369-driftreed-2). The build keeps vertex colour (StripUnusedMeshComponents off) and the source is a uniform, not a variant. `WendmireWorldTests.EveryRaftsWaterlineIsInSightFromThePlayCamera` finds the first water the camera sees past each raft's rim through the landform's collider and checks the line's strength there, depth along the camera's ray plus the mask, against the material's falloff; the depth line alone, which the case shows first, misses the rims the camera looks over. Driftreed was rebuilt and restaged; nothing a route uses moved.
- **D379 - A marking laid in pieces is sized by the whole (November 23, chapter 9).** `Telegraph.Line` takes an optional `wholeLength` (property `Whole`); the hatch (and with it the wash, which the shader scales by the same strength) is sized by `max(Length, Whole)`, so the stretches of one lane thin together as one marking would. `WreckTrack` passes its whole track length, so the Nacre Grave lane (s54/084) is an edge, a faint wash and a faint hatch instead of strong orange over the wrecks; `MotherStrain`, which lays its way as four strips along an arc, passes the arc length. Hits and timing are untouched. Test `QaFightThirdTests.ALaneLaidInStretchesHasTheHatchOfOneWholeLane`, failing first (stretch .98 against the whole lane .08). Look for pale wrecks gone from s54/084.
- **D370 - Many Hands: the galley's flues and hatch wall finished as seen from the garden, the spine's lanes carry a working deck, DOCK reads whole at the hull's arrival (November 23, chapter 5 hub).** The garden deck's "bare grey octagonal pillar" and "flat grey tiled slab" (nov22b-whole s31/129, 164, 208) are not garden pieces: they are the galley's three vent stacks and the top of its 4.8 m hatch wall, both in the liner's grey panel, which fill the foot of every frame on the garden's walk (the camera stands over the galley). The stacks are black-iron stovepipes browned at the mouth with brass collars and bands and a rain cowl on stays (tops 6.5 to 7.1 m, under the camera's line to the walk); the wall's top carries Binna's herb boxes, a timber trough its whole length planted and trailing over both faces (leaves to 5.9 m). The garden itself (beds, ribs, trays, planters, shrubs) was left as it is. The spine's lanes (s44/092, s55/014) have seven flush hatches in striped borders, hinged, slotted and numbered, an arrow down each lane, tie-down points along both lanes' edges and three lashing lockers with coiled straps on the kerbs (solid, .62 m, where no route of nov22b-whole walked); the paint and hatches are "Floor detail" and keep 1.8 m off every way. Five hatches tried on the dock's plate beyond the door were refused (something solid at body height at each) and dropped; that plate already has bays, rings and cargo. The hull's dock tower now runs DOCK down its face's west half, under the cab's overhang (letters above 3 m are hidden from the flight camera), at 1268 to 1305 px of the arrival frame, clear of the objective panel from 1340 (s44/097). Nothing a route walks to or past moved, nobody moved. Rebuilt and restaged: ManyHands, ManyHandsHull; RouteFacts rerun. Renders Builds/quality/look/d370-b (garden-s31-129, garden-s31-208, spine-s44-092, spine-s55-014, ManyHandsHull-arrival). Look for: s31/129, 164, 208 a planted trough and stovepipes; s44/092 hatches and arrows; s44/097 DOCK whole left of the panel.
- **D364 (continued) - The Saint at 140,000 and the Mother's yard at 105,000 (November 23).** At 150,000 the Saint's slow pair read 416 s of 400 on one slow climb reading (1,467 a second where about 3,000 is usual), and the player ran it 161 to 194 s, so it is 140,000 (builder and asset by hand): its pairs now read 154 to 198 s, dodging 269 to 351, and the shared case 150 s at the driver's share. Her clamps' second slack made her room 131 to 135 s in the player and 128 in her own docking test, and her yard is sized to last as long (her class comment): 80,000 to 105,000, which brings `QaPlanCombatTests.ABossLastsTwoToFiveMinutesAtItsExpectedSync(MotherOfDoors)` green (146 s). `MooringBossReplayTests.MotherOfDoorsIsFreedThroughThePadFromHerDoorToHerRelease` now reads .39 of flat out against her sheet's .53. Her room's length is her clamps', not her bar's, and the in-house driver works it slower than the player (143 s). Holding it to .477 would take about 128,000, a yard half again her room, so it is left red for the lead. `FloorPartyBossReplayTests` for the Marshal reads .48 to .50 against .522: red before this entry (D362), and a little slower now that its cold half waits on its heat. The Saint's round-5 checkpoint (the driver counts arrival only within 2.3 m of the press, and a whole round on the climb never comes that near) is the lead's driver change, not a route one.
- **D378 - The Frozen Host is dug out of old ice, Lastlight's pad lies whole in the arrival, and the Skyfields' sand and flags lose their wallpaper (November 23, chapters 5 and 6).** Seen in nov22b s35, s36, s32/200, s39/242 and 245. *The dig floor* was one cell crack pattern wall to wall (`ground-host-cutice`, plates 1.15 m): it is now a flat cut through the glacier's years, bands folded across the floor with dust seams and blue lines, blade scores in runs three ways, chips (7 m repeat, 1024), under world-placed strata bands painted as ground shade so the repeat never shows; branching crack decals as before, no noise-contour cracks. Four beds where blocks came out (kerfs as dark strips, lifted cells in shade) with a duckboard and spoil beside each, at (18.8, 183.9), (85.6, 175.6), (90, 218.4), (182, 206.4); four meltwater pools of ten tried. *The walls* (`host-ice`) break into three uneven faces to a repeat with fractures that run part of their height and die (full height read as stripes); 86 cut faces of the glacier's own ice stand on walls the camera looks at across a floor (above any rank, plug or rig, never where they would hide a floor behind), and 30 things are frozen into wall feet: boulders, a sledge's runner and boards, the host's spears and shields, low (under a chest) on side walls. Nothing on a floor: the nearest to any hero or partner position in s35 and s36 is 1 m. *Lastlight's pad* moved from (-7, 7) to (-5.2, 4.8): from the arrival it lies whole, clear of the party panel and the hint bar; the arrival is unchanged, no route walks to the pad (map brief updated, routes regenerated, none changed). *Skyfields:* the stair gardens are raked in waves (no motif to repeat), and the crack field is one straight crooked crack across one flag in three instead of a noise field's contours over the paving. Plain-floor measure: the Frozen Host .20 at worst (was .25); Skyfields s39/242 .11 (was .23), stair gardens .10 (.48), high island .23 (.22). Rebuilt and restaged: FrozenHost, Lastlight, OrielSkyfields. Look for it in s35 (every dig still), s36/062, s32/200, s39/242 and 245.
- **D367 - The partner comes in off the outside of a loop instead of flying round it (November 23).** In the whole-game capture of November 22 (nov22b-whole) Sela was off the frame at s17/282 and s17/286 only. Barge three, cut free in its skimmers' fight, drifts across its flank knot's point as step 282 begins: the lead is shoved east along its flank and flies a tight loop back to the point (13.7 m/s, about 8 m across), and she rode its outside at a boost (22 to 26 m/s) ten metres out, as fast round as her place went and never nearer. As he came down its last leg past the point and curled up to it (329.2 s), her place swung to his other side a quarter-turn off her nose, nine metres off; at 26 m/s she turns on a circle about 34 m across, and D361's brake for a turn she can't make acts only within 6.6 m of her place, so she swung on south to 16 m behind him, under the bottom edge, and came back round his far side 1.5 s later (frames.csv: she at (752.6, 1144.3) heading south at 24 m/s at 329.1, at (766.8, 1130.3) 16.2 m behind him at 330.06, back in frame at 331.0). Not a fight (none was on) and not a solid (the barge was 20 m off by then). Now `PartnerBrain.Arriving` also brakes for such a turn further off, while her place lies inside the circle she turns at a boost going as she goes against him (its radius that speed squared over a boost's thrust); braked to a cruise she turns inside it. Read on her own speed instead it braked her on the outside of a turn he was still flying and she fell behind (s17/286 20%); a rule putting her on the inside of his hard turns took the Aurora Shear's short lane from 0 to 18%, so it was taken out. Nothing of D354 changes: no hull is solid to another, her place is not on his line. `PartnerInViewTests` flies both legs from 327.5 s with the pair moving and the barge as the capture had them (the 4.1 s before that are the barge between them, from where the skimmers' fight left her): s17/286 from the knots' steps, 29.9% before and 0% now; s17/282 from 329.2 s, 60.8% before and 9.0 to 9.6% now under its own 15% bound (the frames left are HUD plates of the 640x480 test screen as she comes in off the loop, never an edge). Still left: through the loop itself she rides its outside ten metres out, as before. Green: `PartnerInViewTests`, `PartnerFlightRejoinTests`, `CompanionTravelTests`, `FlightDisableTests` (`d367-v4`, 36 of 37, the one the 282 leg before its bound; `d367-v6` the two legs); both typechecks. Next capture: s17/282 and s17/286 with her ship beside his, about 3 m off.
- **D368 - Sorrel's haul road has a ridge beside the party where the route stops, and east 7 is no longer faded whole in play (November 23, chapters 1 and 2).** nov22b-whole s03/007, s04/020, s04/053 showed road and scrub. The route stops for its stills at the road's bends and in its fights' clearings, exactly where D360's spines swung out or stopped and where its test skipped; with feet 7 m and more from the road's middle a spine stood at the frame's edge (the camera sees 9 m either side at the party's depth). The side the camera sees past is pulled in, feet 4.4 to 6 m from the road's middle (`RidgeFromRoad` 4.8), held 1.5 m and more off every line the lead and companion walked in nov22b s02 to s07 (frames.csv), 1 m off the verge's crates, lamp and rocks, 6.4 m off fights: west 1 inside the outpost bend (s03/007), west 2, 3 and 4 nearer, a new west 3b round the wash's fight (s04/020), east 5 nearer, west 6 run on to the excavation's fight and east 7 brought down beside the road to the drill (s04/053). In play east 7 was faded whole (`SceneryOcclusion` boxes a piece from its transform, the world's origin, to its mesh bounds, which held the grid's buried skirt up on the excavation's rim: 'under the lens' from most of the road); each spine's drawn mesh now keeps only its drawn triangles' vertices and stands at its own middle. Each footprint's carve is 16 m tall (6 m left the baked floor under west 2's back, where the basin rises). The outpost's rover is set against its hab (`RoomDressing` and, since that step runs only where dressing is missing, `SorrelRidges`): a 0.4 m gap between them stuck a body, sampled once the spines changed the floor's edges. `ChapterOneTwoLookTests.TheHaulRoadRunsBetweenRidges` now stands the lead, companion and camera where nov22b's 11 haul stills stood: a ridge over 1/16 of the frame and 15% of a side beside the party, none within 1.5 m of them or between them and the lens; with this round's spines hidden the named three fail. No route's walked-to thing moved.
- **D375 - An EXTRA takes at most half of the phase it lands in; the Seedthane's planted half is its legs', the Rotbloom's fishing half its two stalks' (November 23).** *The EXTRA, every boss:* `BossController.ExtraFloor` stops one EXTRA at half of the phase it first lands in, and never over that phase's line (1 Integrity above it); in the last phase it may end the fight unless the shift into it is owed. Before, it could cross one line: a full gauge carried in from the road took a sixth to a third of a two-phase bar at the door. It is the road's reward and lands whole up to there, but one move doesn't skip a phase (`ExtraMoveTests.AnExtraTakesAtMostHalfThePhaseItLandsInAndNeverCrossesItsLine`). *The Seedthane* (37 to 59 s against a bar from 60): in four of five fights of November 22 its bar crossed half with two or three legs standing, carried by the openings of the two that went, and the mobile half went in two to five moves. While a leg stands its bar holds 1 over half (`Health.Holds`); a leg with others standing stops there, the last takes its own share and topples it; a leg costs what it takes (`LegIntegrity` .035 to .06, as the stalks). Player: 68.8 and 80.8 s (`nov23-recon-c-20`, `-d-20`), all four legs and the topple each time. The replay driver doesn't seek legs (Chase skips parts whose reactionOwner is the body) and breaks them in passing; a person aiming at them will be quicker. *The Rotbloom* (51 to 162 s against a bar from 120): the hall drained on one stalk when gaping-mouth blows and an EXTRA took the bar over half; with that held, it drained on two and the second stalk took a quarter on from the held line, so the grounded half began at a quarter and fell in about 10 s (58.8 and 78.0 s, `nov23-recon-c-26`, `-d-26`). Now while it fishes its bar holds at half until the second stalk, which carries it just over and no further: the grounded half is always whole. Blows on a gaping mouth still take the bar to the line but no longer shorten the fight. With its structure fixed its length follows integrity, so (the lead's call) 83,000 to 108,000 in builder and asset; stalks are shares and grow with it. Slow pair 346 to 380 s of 400 (394 at 112,000), the chapter's test 261 of 300, RotbloomDriverTests 264 of 300 (315 at 112,000 in one mirror: noisy). Player: 126.5 and 131.5 s (`nov23-recon-f-26`, `-g-26`), both stalks and every grounded move. The sheets' driver shares (.56, .43) are now stale against these lengths and are for the honest measure to restate.
- **D381 - The meeting sits facing the board, nobody leaves an empty cushion, and the Crawl's dawn mirror and lit windows have depth (November 23, chapters 8 and 9).** Seen in the whole-game capture of November 22. *The meeting* (s57/139, 142, 156): every body posed off a seat (the seven seated townsfolk, Oda, Jun, Oda's mother) was drawn with its pelvis and chest about 164 degrees from the way it was placed and its knees bent back under it, in the editor too (look `d381-before`): the generated bodies' humanoid idle, sampled outside play as `Decks.Pose` captures a pose, turns the pelvis round (`HomeBuilder.PoseProbe`, lines D381_POSE). So the seated sat with their backs to the board whatever LineCues turned them to, and Oda and Jun stood up facing the back of the room. `Pose` now turns the hips back to the body's facing before it poses anyone off a seat (standing poses untouched): all ten are drawn within 3 degrees of their facing (`HomeBuilder.PlayStills`, lines D381_FACE; look `d381-after`). Sitting square, Oda's pelvis rests on the slats and the seating rule gives her no cushion (the capture's green one); `LineCues` also puts a sitter's seat away when a cue stands them up, and a nameless body turned to a speaker turns up to 8 degrees short of or past them (fixed per place; the seated still within `Posed.SeatTurn`). *The Crawl* (s55/138): the "post lamp" is the helm's dawn mirror, painted in the lamps' amber; it and the wheelhouse's lit windows clipped (red 255) because a lit pale base added to emission near 1, the editor alike. The mirror is glass in an iron dish with a brass bezel, hung in a fork on its post, showing the dawn in bands (dark ground, a gold line with the sun's rim, the glow, the night's blue) in its own vertex-tinted material held at 0.9 light (`crawl-mirror`); a lit window is dark glass in its own amber, brighter in the upper lights (`crawl-window`) than the lower (`crawl-window-low`, fading with the house), in a casing 12 cm proud of the wall with glazing bars and a curtain or a jug on the sill. `crawl-lamp` keeps the fade the Terminator's build gives it. Test `DecksLeavingTests.TheSeatedFaceTheWayTheySitAndWhoeverStandsLeavesNoEmptySeat` (a pelvis turned round inside the test reads as turned). Rebuilt and restaged: MeridianCrawl, DecksLeaving; nothing moved. Look for a round mirror with the dawn in it and framed amber windows in s55/138, and the seated backs to the camera, no green cushion, in s57/139 to 156.
- **D380 - The Foundry's shade is the floor darkened, the Colossus's heat plates are lobed slag with an ash rim, the smiths at the clamps wear dyes of their own; the Flows' battery and fires are built and in frame (November 23, chapter 8).** Seen in the whole-game capture of November 22. *The lavender patches* beside the heroes (s48/075, s48/232, and still in D371's render `look/d371-b/BridleFoundry-s48-075`) were the heroes' own shadows, not a decal, cookie or probe: LatticeToon's least light (`_LightFloor`) is a grey taken per channel, and under the furnaces' key (linear (1, .48, .21)) a shadow's blue came from it while its red stayed the light's, so the shade came out bluer than the lit plate round it; the shadow tint (D371) could not reach it. The floor's, its ground copy's and the sand's least light is now .05 (`HarrowgateBuilder.Foundry`; `foundry-floor`, `ground-BridleFoundry`, `foundry-sand` in Resources/WorldMaterials): in `look/d380/BridleFoundry-s48-075` the patch keeps its red and green and its blue falls from 36 to 12. A material value, no keyword, and the editor drew the capture's patch, so the player draws the fix as the editor does. The iron decking before the furnaces keeps Iron's .26 (every machine wears Iron). *The pale-green band* is the coolant trough (WaterKit water) D371 deepened; not changed here. *Heat plates:* each was a cube of the engine's iron, a hard-edged black box when quenched (s49/033); now a lobed dome of `foundry-hot-slag` (its cracks glow while hot, black glassy skin when dark) inside a band of pale ash in `foundry-slag-crust` (vertex colour), both from Resources, made at run time (`BossController.SlagColossus.PlateMesh`); its place, target sphere, integrity, timing and damage are as they were. *The smiths* (s49/140): in their own paintings most wear a pale shirt and dark trousers, so two side by side read as twins whatever their heads or builds; each now wears one of its body's dyes in turn (teal, oxblood, moss, indigo: the three at a clamp wear three). The Foundry was rebuilt for it; nothing moved. *Scoria (s46/047, 089):* the battery (two mortars on their terrace, the breastwork and sandbags, its lean-to) is in frame at s46/124 to 158 where the party fights it, and in neither still the reviewer had; the coal beds are the cookfires' (`Hearths.Fire`) and read as a fire in its ring of stones at s46/047; nothing changed. Tests: `BridleFoundryTests.AHerosShadowOnTheFoundryFloorIsTheFloorDarkenedNotLavender` and `TheColossussHeatPlatesAreLobedSlagWithAnAshRim`, each shown failing on the old state inside the test first.
- **D382 - Many Hands' chart table is a table with the route chart lit in it, the young's light comes up out of its bores, and the Tollkeeper's plate loses Meshy's tan flecks (November 23, chapter 10).** Seen in nov22b-whole s62/037, s63/111, s63/164, s65/057 and s69/075. *The chart table* (`ManyHandsBuilder.CityParts`, the lamp gallery; every chapter's plan is heard round it): it was a crate with a pale lid and seven lit lines that read as painted chevrons. Now four turned legs, an apron and a shelf of rolled charts, the slanted top in a brass rail on posts, and on it the route chart as dark glass inside a ruled brass border, its lanterns and roads lit (the hall's Lamp) where the pause menu's chart sets them (every `ChartStop` at its `position`, joined by its `routes`; logged HANDS_CHART_TABLE). Its solid (3.4 x 1.2 x 2.2) and everything round it are as they were. *The young's light* (`LastMooringBuilder.Sorrel`, the nursery): each bore's mouth stays dark round a small core of the young's own glow down its throat (1.3 mouths across; a lit disc there drew flat white and was taken out), the glow round it is the young's at about a third (`Young spill`) and 4.4 mouths across instead of 9 to 15 m, and its lamps stand 1.2 m over the lip at 10 to 14 m and 1.4 to 1.8 instead of 3 m up at 16 to 30 and 2.4 to 4: from the east clamp's pad it was a flat blown-out cyan pool with no source. The young's glow in the cut after she goes is unchanged. Both glows are the particle shader the crater's glows already draw with (falloff in the alpha, so fog adds nothing), no new variant. Renders: `Builds/quality/look/d382-after/ManyHands-gallery.png` and `d382-after2/SorrelSecondSky-shelf-4.png`. *The dock (s63/111, s63/164)* is not the builder's: no ship stands on a berth. 111 is the party's own hulls in the 0.6 s after `Berth` sets them down on the shelf while their `FormController` folds them (the tilt is the fold), placed 2 m apart (the on-foot gap) though they are still ships; 164 is Idra in flight at the plane over the shelf's works as she comes round to the lead after the launch. Both are shared code (`Berth.Place`, `FormController`, `PartnerBrain`), left for their owners; no clamp, pad or route place moved. *The Tollkeeper's hull* (s65/057): the brown patches are Meshy's, flecks of its trim gold scattered over the white plate in the texture itself. `tools/tollkeeper_grade.py` takes off every warm patch of at most 320 texels (of 1024) with white on most of the ring round it and lays the plate round it over it (588 on the carrier, 769 on the outrigger); every ring, line and fitting keeps its gold. It writes art-src's albedo and the staged base colour, keeping `..._albedo_ungraded.png`; a later intake `prepare` undoes it. *After the credits (s69/075)*: it reads as an ending and is left: the objective says the convoy is through and there is no hurry, everyone named stands where the epilogue put them with their own homecoming line and an extra for each finished story (lines about the new town, true after the end), and the menu returns to the title, which knows a finished game. The Also line is a side quest still open in that save (Maru, in the town), not a stale one; barks stay hushed after the last line until a load (`QuietAfter`, 1e6 s), by its comment on purpose. Rebuilt: ManyHands and SorrelSecondSky (each restaged, route facts written); nothing a route walks, stands at, talks to or fights on moved. Tests: `ChapterTenLookTests` and the shared cases of both zones (focused label d382). Look at tonight's s62 (the table from 017 to 061), s63/164 (the mouth below the east pad) and s65/057 (the plate along the engine pods).
- **D383 - The Warmaster is at his table or on his field, never both (November 23, the lead).** The whole-game scout of round three stopped twice in route 52 (`Builds/quality/nov23-recon-C/s52`, `s52b`: "up to the oath hall 9/10"): the lead walked into what D371 put on the Warmaster's post at F to stop a hero standing inside him. Behind it was a fault the stills had shown since the duel was built and nobody had read (`nov22b-whole/s52/014` and `017`): the duel's encounter puts its boss on show at F from the moment the town loads, and Drask is a person at his table in the oath hall until `DraskReceives` sends him out, so the party passed him on the field and met him again 22 m on. `EncounterVolume.previewFlag` now holds a fight's body off show until a flag is set (checked each frame while held, so he appears as the talk ends), the duel's is `vask.draskMet`, and what stops a hero at his post hangs under a gate on the same flag (still under D371's on `vask.fieldTaken`). Nothing in the routes changes: route 52 crosses an empty field on its way up, and the field's talk and the duel come after the table. `HarrowgateTownTests.TheWarmasterIsAtHisTableOrOnTheFieldNeverBoth`; Harrowgate rebuilt, relit, restaged. Look at tonight's s51 and s52 before the table (an empty field) and s52 after it (he stands at F as the party comes down the steps).
- **D389 - HUD text and panel state, round four (November 24).** The objective panel moves past a flag step that names a boss's fight the moment that boss is down (`StoryQuests.PastTheFight`, read by `ObjectiveHud`; quest state untouched), so the Foundry Saint's "Release the arms" no longer lies over the loot while Orrin's talk runs; every such step (Warmaster, Host Marshal, Debris Cutter, the Vessa gallery and the rest whose target encounter is a boss) is covered by the one rule. Quest cards wait while an EXTRA's band is up (`ExtraCutIn.Up`, read by `QuestToast`) instead of being drawn over by it. A feed line that lands under the bark plate snaps the plate down at once (`BarkPanel`) instead of gliding for the frames it takes. The bench's ladder note holds each bracketed count on one line (`<nobr>`). The shop's last column reads WORN/HELD, since it holds a part's comparison with what is worn and a consumable's count, not what you have. Left: the credits' "Dventure at Dusk" and "Glass Ruans" are the music files' own names (the zones, tests and tracks.json use them) and are Nathan's to rename; the bay's black mouth and the cut-in caught mid-slide at s66 151 and 164 are not faults; the closing line "There is no hurry" already is the game's last word (s69 076). Look at: s59 038 and 043, s47 080, s23 174, s07 013, s13 118.
- **D385 - A beaten boss is dark on the frame its end comes, and nothing of its fight stays on it (November 24).** In the player the first frame after every end was the living body or paler (nov23-whole s49/052 a pale chrome Colossus, s36/071 the Marshal, s29/034 the Hulljack, s26/463 the Rotbloom, s67/025 the Gantry; Taren speckled in the Floodcrown, s21/280): `BodyGoesDark` began at nought on its first LateUpdate, a frame late, while `HitFlash` wrote the killing blow's full flash after the Died that added it and `BossOcclusion` kept its living door; BossDeathTests killed with an unweighted packet, which flashes nothing, so it never saw it. Now the darkening is written in the Died itself, half dark with the lights out (`FirstShare` .5), the killing blow's flash goes out with the end (`KillLight` 0: even .03 on its rims, added before the screen's curve, lifted the darkest bodies) and HitFlash asks for it (`MostFlash`), the door opens whole on a beaten body, and what is driven off goes to half paint and a tenth of its light. The Host Marshal is no longer exempt (its frost was a 2.4 s game-clock lerp from white with the rig's thaw-orange lit first: whole and lit at s36/076): it darkens toward its frost blue at once and kneels fastest first, on real time. Mother of Doors, who leaves by her own road and not BossDeparture, goes beaten (s63/150 in full lilac). The Foundry Saint released is powered down: no warm lamps over it, and its six arms (the room's, outside its root, so never reached: s59/038, 043 in full gold and cream) darken with it, lamps out (`SaintArm.PowerDown`, also at load). On a beaten boss only the killing blow's number finishes its rise (GameHud: the partner's '438' on the Bellows, s08/092), and a net or skill ring of its fight goes on the frame it ends, not over a quarter second (PulseField, SkillRing). Tests: BossDeathTests (Beat is a weighted finisher; every boss but those that yield, the freed Cantor, the Burrower and the Warden must go dark and be darker on its end's own frame; ANetLaidInTheFightGoesOnTheFrameItEnds, OnlyTheKillingBlowsNumberStaysOnABeatenBoss, TheReleasedSaintsArmsGoDarkWithIt). Look at the stills named above in the next capture.
- **D387 - The Scoria battery's orange dome is Idra's Shelter, drawn twice its size; a fight's line goes when the fight ends; the cooled Colossus no longer calls a dark plate (November 24, chapter 7).** *The dome (nov23-whole s46/127 to 140)* is no mortar marking: it is Idra's Shelter (player.log: SKILL_KIND Idra Shelter at step 127; nov22b had it too). `ShelterDome` scaled the built-in `Sphere.fbx` by twice its reach as if it were a unit across; that mesh is 2 m across (measured in the test: half-extent 1.000), so the dome stood 10 m out over a 5 m ring, and its glass was the energy bubble at 1.8 times an HDR orange, its edge several times white for the bloom to spread over the frame. Now the shell is scaled from the mesh's own bounds (drawn 5.01 m on a 5 m reach) and wears its own glass (pale amber, core .03, edge under white). A material made at run time from a shader in Resources, no keyword, no variant: the player draws it as the editor does. No other fight uses this code; its reach, timing and what it stops are as they were. *The bark (s49/043, 052, 057):* the line was `bossopening.SlagColossus`, said at every cooled stuck or vent long after the plates were shed (EXPOSED at step 36; all three shed at step 33), so at 043 there was no plate to find; a cooled opening now says the shared `bossopening` ("It's wedged. Now!"). `BarkPanel` takes down a line said during a fight (a boss engaged, an encounter begun and not won, or an awake enemy within 15 m: `BarkPanel.FightOn`) as soon as the fight ends, held or not; a kill's word stays its time (`BarkService.Situation`). Tests (HarrowgateBossTests): `IdrasShelterStandsOnItsRingAsClearGlassNotAnOrangeWash`, `AFightsLineGoesWhenTheFightEndsAndAKillsWordStaysItsTime`, `CooledItsOpeningsSayTheSharedLineNotTheDarkPlates`. Not done here: the plates' readability (they hang at 1.8 m among the legs, under the crucible, out of the camera's sight), Taren's halftone behind the Colossus (BossOcclusion's door and HeroThrough together), and the Warmaster's table (see the report). Look at s46/127 to 133 (a thin pale dome on its ring round Idra, no wash) and s49/052, 057 (no bark over the dead Colossus).
- **D388 - A warp's name is drawn once, as its sign; a number says what the bar lost, and the partner's has its own colour; every name's tag is the small tag; a place doesn't thin a plate (November 24, labels).** From the capture of November 23. (1) The pale-blue 'Warp — Nacre Grave' under the skill row at the Picket (s53 023) is the second label five builders hang on a warp, 7 m short of it at size 3.2, renamed '...warp sign' so SignStyle never dressed it: the builder's colour and face, no plate, no SignFade. The Picket has one and the Hull six. SignStyle.Strays switches off, as a zone loads, any world text that repeats a dressed warp sign's words; the sign over the gate stays. The builders still make the twins (not rebuilt). (2) '56714930!' on the Cutter (s61 071) was one number: its last pod's blow is a hundred bars (BossController.DebrisCutter), and the number showed the blow whole. Health.LastTaken is what the bar lost, and the number shows that. The partner's '3697', '2693' were its hits on armour in the resisted grey; the partner's numbers are now sky blue (GameHud.PartnerColor) at full strength with the same edge, whatever the hit; the lead's keep the kinds' colours. (3) MIRA's tag was large because a name at the foot of the screen was drawn as perspective had it (about 1.3 times, Band 0), PELL's because a marked name grew to 32 px with a mark half as tall again. Names now hold their made size near the camera (SignStyle.NameBand 1) and a mark is the name's size: measured on the Decks, 76 px made, 78 at the foot (was 118), 76 marked (was 112). (4) The objective's plate went to a third for the pad it named (s09 007); a place no longer lowers a plate. Bodies behind a panel still do (D356; reversing that is the lead's call with D394). Left: the lock card at the top edge with nowhere clear (s62 121; D222 keeps it at the top, so it fades), the Pikeguard's bracket beside the vitals (s46 047, on its target and clear of the panel), the Orrery's top line (s38 081: a rail's lamp strip, bronze under white, the Orrery builder's). QaNov24LabelTests 4/4 (each shows the old state first), QaHudThirdTests.ThePanelStaysSolidOverWhereItSendsTheParty turned over. Look for: s53 023 no pale warp name under the skills; s61 071 the Cutter's last number its remaining bar; s29 030, s30 034 the partner's number sky blue; s13 079, s69 038 tags the size of the others; s09 007 the objective's plate solid.
- **D386 - A fallen small body lies off a hero's outline as the camera sees him; a hero behind the Bellows is seen through it; Sela was never on the lift's door (November 24, chapters 1 and 2).** From the capture of November 23. *Corpses (s03/007, a Ridgehound the reviewer called a Scrapmite; s14/017, two Gloomgrazers):* D355 and D374 laid a body clear of a hero on the plan, a third of a metre off his capsule, and the camera, pitched 39 degrees from behind the party, still drew one lying at his back with its legs up round his torso and head. `DefeatPresentation` now also judges, for every small body that falls (the one shared path, `FallClear`, every species), what it draws carried along the camera's line to the upright plane through each hero's feet: none of it may lie within his capsule's width and a third of a metre each side, from his feet to the top of his head. Where some does, the blow throws it the shortest of three ways that clears him (on from him through its middle, or across the view to either side), in tenths up to 2 m, over floor within .35 m of its own and short of any wall; a hound also takes the flank that keeps it off him so. The player log says `BODY_THROWN <id> seen= left= way= m` and `BODY_FALLS_CLEAR ... thrown=`. `BodiesKeepApartTests.A{Gloomgrazer,Ridgehound,Scrapmite}KilledAgainstTheHeroIsNotDrawnRoundHim` kill one on each of eight bearings round Taren where the capture's died, each standing with its drawn front at his capsule, and read what it draws on the screen against his outline (a quarter metre margin): 0.60 m in for one laid by hand on its old rest straight behind him at D355's distance, 0.00 on every bearing killed. s15/052 is a living grazer biting him from behind (the locked card, 220/220), not a corpse: left. *The Bellows (s08/070, 081):* Taren was never on the dome. frames.csv has him at the floor's height (-7.92) every frame of the fight and never nearer its middle than its 2.4 m capsule stops him; at both stills he stood on its far side 3.6 to 3.7 m from its middle (from his facing while striking, the middle at about (1.6, 265.2)), 1.2 m off its drawn edge, and from the camera its crown hid his feet. The see-through door opened only when the body hid his chest, so at 081 it stayed shut and he stood whole on the crown's line. It now opens when a body hides his feet as well, centred on his knees (`BossOcclusion`), so the crown under his feet goes to its dots and the floor beyond shows. BodiesKeepApartTests passed because nothing was ever in him; `AHeroFightingTheBellowsFromItsFarSideIsSeenThroughItNotStandingOnIt` stands him where the capture had him under the capture's lens: feet hidden, chest in sight (the old door's state), and now the door open between his feet and chest. At play size he may still read as standing at the crown, since its line meets his feet from this camera; the rest is the camera or the dome's height, the lead's call. *Sela at the pressure lift (s16/144):* she was walking north at a run, 2.8 m past the lift's door (its lintel, 3.2 m, at z -7), and the camera's line to her feet passes just over the lintel; she was never on it and does not rest there. Left. Look in the next capture at s03/007, s14/017 to 022 and s08/070, 081.
- **D390 - Last Mooring's floor is a gulf under the anchorage's trusses, every lamp a lamp, the Tollkeeper's plate white and slate, the passage's ribs carry a current (November 24, chapter 10).** nov23-whole s64/016, s65/056 and 064, s68/105. The navy grid was the anchor works (`Mooring.Works`: plates on a 44 m grid with girders on its lines); the white hexagons and the block in the lane were the convoy's running lights, glow cubes floating at each hull's box top; every lamp's colour clipped in two channels. Now dark box trusses cross the gulf under each chain line on anchor heads whose shanks go on down, keel trusses tie the pylons' feet, works decks hang under them, cables sag between, lamps lie far down; six heavy hulls ride 32 to 44 m down; every lamp is a lens in an iron foot and hood with its pool (`Fitting`, `Pools`), its colour one channel at most over one, and the running lights stand on each hull's spine (cast onto its mesh). The relays are Meret's on the Continuance (s66/157), plain there; left. The Tollkeeper's lilac was its material, not texture: full white (clipped under the 1.3 sun) and the shader's default lilac shade; `Tollkeeper.Plate` sets white (.84, .85, .87) (sRGB: .67 drew it grey) and shade (.52, .53, .55). The passage's band was a rib in the unlit glow: the rib is lit tissue now with a soft ribbon of its light scrolled round it by `ConvoyFlow`; its markers are plank drums carrying framed amber lanterns. Only existing materials and variants, nothing made at run time; bloom is the risk, held by the lens colours. Rebuilt, relit, restaged, route facts: LastMooring, ReturningPassage; nothing a route, fight or berth uses moved. Tests: `LastMooringZoneTests.UnderTheLanesIsAGulfAndEveryLampIsItsOwnColourNotAWhiteSolid`, `.TheTollkeepersPlateIsWhiteInTheSunAndSlateInItsShadeNotLilac`, `ReturningPassageTests.TheBoresRibsHaveFormAndTheRoadsLanternsAreThingsThatFloat`. Renders `Builds/quality/look/d390-after2` and `d390-after`. Look at s64/016 to 030, s65/056 and 064, s68/105.
- **D392 - Chapter 10 on foot finished where the fourth look still saw a blockout: the crater's bores are mouths, the Continuance's bands are paint and its skylights glass, Tovra has form, Meret has presence, the well's cover anchors are machines, the chart table's star is on its top (November 24, chapter 10).** Seen in nov23-whole s63/150, s63/164, s66/149, s66/153, s67/112, s67/117, s67/193 to 204 and s55/035. *The bores* (`LastMooringBuilder.Sorrel`, the nursery): each mouth was a dark disc with the young's glow laid over it (D382 had only shrunk it), so from the shelves it read as a filled cyan ellipse. Now a collar of spoil stands round each in the floor's own colour, its lip .42 of the mouth's radius high (at most 1.25 m); inside it the throat goes down in three bands from black to the young's teal (`sorrel-second-throat`, least light one, so the sun can't grey it); at its foot the bore's dark with the young's glow small in the middle (1.15 of the foot); the lamp is down in the throat; the collar's skirt follows the floor no higher than half its lip (at the moat's edge the floor is the crater's wall). No glow lies over a mouth. *The tug over the kerb* (s63/164) is left, as D382 found: frames.csv puts Idra in flight at the plane (y 1) moving over the works' winch housing (top -0.67) as she comes round to Taren after the launch; it is a ship 1.7 m over the housing, not a tug resting on it, and only `PartnerBrain`'s flight slot could keep her off it. *The Continuance's back* (`Continuance.Hull`): its four teal bands are laid in the hull's own plate tinted teal, a plate's length at a time with a dark seam between, and its skylights are a lit room (`continuance-window`, a dark band of what stands inside, the fitting lit warm) or dark glass (`continuance-glass`), a warm pool only round the lit ones; no lamp teal pane is left on the plate. *Tovra* (s67/204: a black shape at the west clamp): her generated body's base map averages 54 of 255 (Brekk's 94); at the first clamp she wears `Chamber.TovraInTheChamber` (1.72, 1.68, 1.62) through PropTint, as the other smiths wear theirs. *Meret*: drawn at 1.2 (she measured .9 of Taren at one), the woman who yields too so nobody shrinks; her mantle deeper (.035, .15, .2), fuller (its shoulders past hers and wrapping forward, flared, to mid-shin) with a gold yoke and hem; a point light in her frame in front of her brow (teal-white, 2.6 m, 1.8, pixel); least light .4. Capsule, moves and hits unchanged. *The control well's cover anchors* (`Chamber.ControlColumn`): a square column on its bolted foot with iron corner posts, a framed access panel and the hazard band on each face, a console head with a screen lit teal leaning out of each face, the cap with its gold ring and lit crown and an amber lamp, cable runs down its back; their solid boxes are as they were. *The chart table* (`ManyHandsBuilder.CityParts`): the floor's sixteen-point star went (from the play camera its points ran out under the table and drew across its apron and legs); the ring stays on the floor, and the star is in the chart's north-east corner, brass and teal with its north lit. Rebuilt: SorrelSecondSky, Continuance, StillwaterChamber, ManyHands (each relit and restaged, route facts written); nothing a route walks, talks at or fights on moved, Many Hands' navigation is unchanged. Tests: `ChapterTenLookTests` (TheCratersBoresAreMouthsWithTheirLightInsideThem, TheContinuancesBackHasNoFlatTealStripsOrCyanPanes, NobodyAtTheClampsIsABlackShape, MeretStandsOverTheHeroesAndHerFrameLightsHer, TheWellsCoverAnchorsAreMachinesWithScreens, TheChartTablesStarIsOnItsTopNotTheFloor, each failing on the capture's state first). Renders from the capture's cameras: `Builds/quality/look/d392-after` (the Continuance, the chamber, Many Hands) and `d392-after3` (the crater) (`LastMooringBuilder.FootLook`). Look at tonight's s63/150 and 164, s66/149 and 153, s67/112, 117 and 193 to 204, s55/035.
- **D393 - The Floodcrown's basin is a drained bed, the Frozen Host's cut ice is scored band by band, Driftreed's mire ripples, and the Terminator's veil and lamps are gone and lanterns (November 24, chapters 3, 6 and 9).** Seen in nov23-whole. *The basin* (s21 280, 290, 309): the damp layer's own texture is blots, painted at half weight (where the shader cuts it) over the basin's outer ring, and the pads floating on the full water were one 30 m piece the ground kit took for a thing on the floor, so nothing (stain, pebble, leaf, puddle) lay anywhere on the bed. Now silt (the sand texture) in an outline that wanders six to thirteen metres out with a tongue toward the drain, bare woven root, leaf litter against the walls and over the platforms, the bed shaded darker toward the middle, pebbles and stains, three puddles in the lowest (pads hidden from the survey only). *The dig* (s35/420, s36/076): `ground-host-cutice` is scored by year-band, seven ways and pitches with four bands of eleven bare and none at right angles to a band (that read as a ladder); the drift along a wall is whole out to a reach of .8 to 3.4 m; 74 runs of blade scores lie round the beds and the walls' feet. *Driftreed* (s24 112, 176): MireRafts (LocalBiomeBake) reached its deep colour in a hand of water (absorption 16) with ripples and reflection too faint to see; now absorption 4.5, an amber shallow, ripples .9 at .62 tiling, a slow scum, reflection .7 at fresnel 1.15 (.9 made a blue lake), broad lamp glints; colours and strengths only, no keyword. *The Terminator* (s55/152, s56/059): the veil was the shade rime behind the crawler at the run's start (and behind every car): rime may now lie only behind things under 2 m, soft-edged, at most 3.2 m, and as built nothing that low casts where it may lie, so there is none and the night side's slate stands (the hollows' rime is as it was); the ground kit's rocks are pebbles and 156 kit boulders, outcrops and ridge rocks (no collider) lie within 14 m of the way, warmed as the edge rocks are; every lamp's glass is alone in its renderer (the coupling lamp's brass cap shared it and lit with it, a pale block) inside an iron housing, and the engine car's four have a pool of light each that `TreadRide.lampLights` lights as the car wakes. Probe `FloorStills.Render` (looks d393-before, d393-after, d393-after2). `QaNov24FloorsTests`, four cases, each failing on the old scenes first (d393-before). Rebuilt, relit and restaged: WalkingCanopy, FrozenHost, Terminator; Driftreed restaged (its water re-baked). Nothing a route uses moved.
- **D395 - A hero hidden by a boss is one clean outline and the boss stays whole over him; the Last Warden's end is judged on its own clock; the Colossus's plates are not moved (November 24, the fights).** *Taren behind the Slag Colossus (nov23-whole s49/033, 043)* was gold glints among dots: `BossOcclusion` dissolved two pixels in three of the boss over the lead, and `HeroThrough` draws his outline only where boss pixels still stand in front of him. Now the door opens over no hero `HeroThrough` draws (`BossOcclusion.Outlined`), alive or dead, at his chest or (D386) at his feet; the door, D386's feet rule with it, is kept only for a lead nothing draws through. A property-block value: no keyword, variant or material; Sela's outline already drew clean in the player against the same body. `QaFightThirdTests.TheLeadBehindABossIsACleanOutlineAndTheBodyStaysWhole` (Bellows, Colossus) writes the old door first: his outline over .39 of him, .61 of the body gone; now 1.00 and 0. Turned from the door to the outline, door geometry kept with the drawing off: `BossOcclusionTests` (2), `QaFightLookTests` (3), `QaFightSecondTests` (NoGhost, DrillReallyHides, HessasHurtbox), D386's Bellows far side (now reads 100% of his 660 hidden feet-and-shin pixels drawn as outline). Still wrong: from the capture's lens his shins are a pale cream glow on the crown and he may still read as standing on it; and with the drawing off the dead wreck's feet probe opens for a hero beside it that its real surface does not hide (its cells say yes a cell off the surface). *BossDeathTests' Warden (28 %, d385b):* `StandsDown` bows on the game clock, which a boss kill's stop holds a quarter second (the fourth clamp's release is a boss kill too); a standing-down body's second is counted on that clock (46 %). *Heat plates:* left (report). Look at s49/031, 032, 043 (a clean gold Taren in the Colossus) and s08/070, 081.
- **D396 - A name with news wears a gold pip in its own small tag; the label tests read a held name where it is drawn (November 24, labels).** Two cases went red after D388. *The mark:* `QaUiFlowTests.SomeoneWithSomethingNewToSay...` asked a marked name to grow, which D388 took away on purpose (PELL's 190 by 125 tag, s13 079); in the name's size the gold Cinzel '!' was a stroke three pixels wide. Now `PeopleMarks.Pip` sets a solid pip in the HUD's gold (the objective arrow's and its 'Here') with a dark '!' cut in it before the name, under the name's one tag: TextMeshPro's mark, from just under the baseline to just over the capitals and a quarter em past the '!' each side, given in linear light because TextMeshPro writes a mark's colour as given and a letter's converted. Drawn by SignText's marks pass, which draws every tag in the player already: no new variant or keyword. The name keeps its size and still turns gold; the tag is no taller (QaNov24 76 px marked = made) and wider only by the mark's room. The test now asserts that (one gold mark at full strength inside the tag, no second tag, area and height bounds, D388's '!' shown to have no pip) and keeps `Builds/quality/look/d396/pell-plain.png` and `pell-marked.png` (in the mirror that ran it). *Ruva Okh 1.9 to 1 (NeighbourhoodAfloat, by Hask):* not a missing or pale plate (the test's crop shows the tag solid): `WorldLabelTests.OnScreen` placed a word by its bounds, but SignText holds a name nearer than the camera's distance to its made size about its middle, so at the foot of Hask's view the read ran past the tag onto the paving. OnScreen now shrinks a word as the shader does (`Held`), for every word; the 3 to 1 bar is unchanged. Its 'alone' render also leaves out the marks pass (the gold pip read as letters that never reach the screen: '!Pell' cut 6% in Many Hands). Look at: s13 079 (a gold pip before PELL, the tag the size of the others); any marked name in Many Hands.
- **D394 - The companion reads a boss body's own strike on foot, and rests where no card comes up over her (November 24).** *Slag Colossus (s49):* its tread is its EnemyBrain's plain attack, and the marking's owner is that brain, not the BossController; on foot PartnerBrain read only the controller's markings, so the tread was met only by the last-moment rule for her target's tell (0.12 s, within 5 m of the body's middle), and in every capture Idra was in her swing (state Attack, never Guard) as it landed. She now reads a boss body's markings on foot as a boss's (Reads/BossBody), counts its sphere's reach past the marking's allowance (StrikeMargin), starts no swing or skill while walking out (a swing holds her to a third of her pace), and does not walk back into a strike landing within 1.5 s (InStrike, she waits at its edge). (2) `MooringRouteTests` for Sorrel's second sky found the launch of the west clamp's works not offered: the fixture stands the zone's enemies down at load, the dock wakes a Stillwater Tender after that, and D404's rule holds the launch while it is awake on the deck. A route launches with the deck's fight behind it, so the fixture stands enemies down again before a launch press; the rule stands. (3) `SceneFadeMaterialTests`: Rootmarket's press faded in `xylos-plank`, `xylos-cloth` and `xylos-lantern` with the fade's variant off, so the player would have drawn it solid over the lead. D393's rebuild of the Walking Canopy repainted the three shared materials, and `XylosBuilder`'s paint never gave them the variant (D359's repair had, once, on the assets alone). The paint gives it now, so no rebuild of a Xylos zone can take it off, and the three assets are as committed. (4) `HomeReplayHandsTests`: the Debris Cutter's in-house fight landed 841 a second (285.5 s) under D403's floor of 966. The same test passes alone (`d405a`, 38 of 38): four editors side by side slow the in-house fight, and the player's build lands 1,328 to 1,569 a second on the same code (240,000 in 153.0, 163.9 and 180.7 s, `nov24-recon-C`, `-g2`, `-g` s61). A check that fails by how busy the machine is says nothing about the hand, so for the Cutter the band is gone and the in-house fight is held to what the player's is: felled, in 120 to 300 s. The Mother's and the Tollkeeper's bands passed under the same load and stay. Whether the hand flies as a person would is `FromBehindADistrictTheCutterIsFlownRoundTheDistrictNotIntoIt`'s to say.
- **D406 - Docked at a fight's shelf, the companion stays where the berth set her down, a clamp a tender is working isn't offered, and the replay steps in to a tender (November 24).** *Idra (nov24-recon-g-63 s63, frames.csv 58.5 to 59.0 s):* a berth's change holds the controls for its fade (Berth.Shift), and D397's way round the lead as a talk opens in flight (PartnerBrain.ClearOfTheTalk) took the hold for a talk. Her way was reckoned from where the pair had been in flight; half-way through the fade the berth set her down 2 m beside Taren, and her way went on round where he now stood at her old distance: 13 m off in a frame, off the west clamp's works and down below its floor, where she never reached the tender (no skill of hers for the rest of the run; MOTHER_WAVE_COVERED named only Taren). The passing scouts docked with her in the clear part of the frame, so her way never began. A hold that moves the lead more than 1 m, or sets her down 2 m from where her way left her, now ends her way (TalkHimMoved, TalkHerMoved): she stays where the change put her. *The lead (same run, 166 CLAMP_TAKEN):* the roll out of her Cone set him 1.53 m from the seat (inside its 1.6) and 2.13 m from the Stillwater Tender. Two causes. The game's: on foot the interact button is the attack button, and the clamp was offered with the tender at work on it, where a taken weight drains as fast as it is taken; every other press took it and the next swung and let go. BridleClamp.Available is now false while a tender is working it (as D404's FoeOnDeck gives the button to the blow); a hand already on the clamp when a tender sets to work stays on it, and nothing eases while one is there. The driver's: it swung from the step's 2.3 m, and Taren's blade doesn't reach a body half a person tall from there; a person sees it fall short and steps in. ContinuousReview.TenderReach takes a blade to 1.5 m of any Clamp Tender (Sela keeps her ranged reach). `MooringBossReplayTests.DockedAtHerShelfTheCompanionIsSetDownOnItBesideTheLead` (the run's own places; the mirror's 640 by 480 frame has no clear place beside him, so her way is begun as the player's frame began it): as it was 2.0 m then 12.9 m off him, off the shelf at y -5.6; now 2.0 m, on it. `...AtHerSeatTheLeadStrikesTheTenderBesideTheClamp` (the lead where the roll left him, Idra's brain off, the route's own step through the pad): both as they were, 22 takes and no blow in 20 s; the clamp as it was, 5 to 9 takes; the reach as it was, not felled in 20 s (0 or 1 blow); now felled in 2.1 and 3.1 s, no take. LastMooringBossTests' clamp-and-tender case stages a hand already on the clamp (the seat is no longer offered under a tender). d406f: 18 of 19 (the companion case's fixture reworded since, d406g green), with her whole fight through the pad 132.4 s (793 a second, inside D403's 623 to 975; sheet 120 to 300) and the Tollkeeper 144.8 s. Routes need no regenerating. Look for: s63, Idra on each shelf beside Taren after every dock and at the tender at once; no "Take the clamp's weight" while a tender works; Taren stepping in to a tender rather than swinging short.
- **D408 - Bodies on foot drawn inside each other: one of a pack in another, the cook through Idra; Idra in the Warmaster not fixed (November 24, chapters 1 and 8).** From the scout of November 24. *A pack (nov24-recon-h-14 s14/051):* `EnemyBrain.KeepApart` held two of a pack apart middle to middle by their half-widths (`bodyRadius`: 0.55 m for a Gloomgrazer drawn 1.26 m ahead of its middle and a metre behind), so one walking in behind another stood with its head in the other's haunch and two converging on the hero stood head in head; the walk in (3.7 m/s) overran the shove apart (2.4), a tell began where it stood, and a lunge ran into its own (RoomAhead stopped only at heroes). Now each body has a spine from its drawn back to its drawn front (`ReachToward`: its posed skin read once on sixteen bearings 0.3 s after it wakes, a skin that isn't generated baked, an unreadable mesh by its box) and two keep apart spine to spine (two round bodies exactly as before); the walk in loses what takes it further into one of its own, no tell starts 0.2 m inside one, and a lunge stops where a step would take it further in. `BodiesKeepApartTests.APackOfGloomgrazersComingInAFileNeverStandInOneAnother` (three in a file, 13 s): floor two cover both 0.40 m2 as before, 0.00 now; marks 17 and 17, bites 13 and 5 (one behind no longer bites through the one in front). *The cook (s52/232):* `WalkLoop` looked ahead with a 0.38 m ball, and D391 grew his solid to 0.66 m, so a hero beside his way outside the ball and inside his solid was walked through. He now also stops for a hero of the party nearer his way than his solid and her capsule. `AWalkerInHarrowgateDoesNotWalkThroughThePartnerBesideHisWay` (Idra 0.81 m off his way): as before 0.17 m of him in her shoulders, now none; he waits 1.18 m off. *Idra at the Warmaster (s52/083, a Breach into him):* her stand-off on foot is now what a body draws toward her and her capsule and a hand (never past 2.8 m, inside her swing), on her side of it, the frame's places for a blade or hammer kept to it, and she steps back out to it after a charge. It does not cure the duel: in `AtTheWarmasterThePartnerFightsFromBesideWhatHeDraws` (ignored, with the reason) she still settles 1.41 m from his middle against a 1.54 m stand-off and 0.30 m of what he draws is in her, as before 0.35 at 1.33; something solid and drawn stands 1.4 m behind him that his outline (1.09 m) does not hold. Her blows: 12 to 13 in 8 s and 5,376 to 7,118 landed, as before 13 and 5,368 to 7,563; HarrowgateBossTests, PartnerInViewTests and HarrowgateTownTests green (d408a, d408c). *Left:* s52/065 is the camera: Taren stands 1.89 m before the Warmaster at his table, 1.1 m of floor between them, his head over the Warmaster's shins from 39 degrees up (D391 expected it). s14/058: the two corpses D386 threw clear lie touching his boots on the screen; they lay heaped because they were alive one in the other. Look at s14/051 and every gorge and relay pack, s52/232 (the cook waiting as Idra passes), s52/083 to 100 (Idra still in the Warmaster's back).
- **D407 - No two ships are seen through each other on a deck, and a hero left inside a beaten boss is eased out of it (November 24).** *Docks (nov24-recon-h-63 s63/111, -h-65 s65/028):* `Berth.Shift` sets the party down at the black and the zone's change begins each hero's form change there (`PartyController.Apply`), but `FormController` closed the ship for .23 s and opened the person .27 s after while the fade lifted in .2 s, so the screen cleared on two ships set down a person's 2 m apart (`Berth.Place`), hulls reaching 1.69 to 2.17 m lying through each other. The change is now made under the black (`FormController.Cover`, called by `Berth.Shift` after `Place`): the body a hero leaves is put away at once and the one he takes opens from its fold as the screen clears, so people appear beside each other on a deck and ships at hull spacing in flight. Every berth, fight or not, both ways, goes through this one path. D406's place for her (2 m beside him, on the shelf) is unchanged. *Boss ends (nov24-recon-h-52 s52/177 to 183):* the Warmaster's skiff reels .4 s and then leaves over 3.2 s; Taren's lunge had carried his ship 2 m into its 2.4 m capsule, and he sat in its darkened hull through the quest card and his line. A boss's body is not thrown clear as a small one is (D386), so `WreckClearance` (begun in BossController's end) takes any living hero of its own form found inside its capsule on the plan in the first 1.5 s and slides him out along the way from its middle, over .4 s on the heroes' clock, by his own controller: walls stop him, on foot he only goes over floor within .4 m of his height (never off a deck or shelf), the next way round is tried where one is shut, and a hero over 1.2 m above it (the Saint's back) or of the other form (on a deck under the Tollkeeper or Mother of Doors) is left alone. It adds to his own moving, so the stick still moves him. Log: `WRECK_EASE`, `WRECK_CLEAR`. Tests (old state d407a: 12 of 13 red; d407b: 143 of 143 across BerthTests, BossDeathTests, CreatureDefeatTests, DefeatFinishTests, DockReachTests, HarrowgateBossTests, LastMooringBossTests, MooringBossReplayTests, FormContinuityTests, FormStageTests): `BerthTests.DockedOnADeck...` and `DownFromTheSpaceBesideAGroundZone...` (as it was, two ships 2.00 m apart drawn 1.47 m through each other at a fade of .94; now no frame closer than .23 m of room); `BossDeathTests.ABeatenBossEasesAHeroLeftInsideItOutOfItWithinASecond` (ten bosses in flight and on foot, the lead .5 m from its middle; a second on, as it was the skiff 2.13 m of 4.18, the Cutter 4.24 of 4.38, Meret's ship 2.25 of 4.78, the ground bodies .50; now all clear, the skiff 5.82) and `EasedOutOfABeatenBossAHeroIsNeverPutOffADeckOrThroughAWall` (the Marshal on a deck, its edge .2 m behind him and a wall at his side: out along the deck, on it, short of the wall). Routes need no regenerating. Look for: s63 and s65 at each dock (two people opening side by side, no ship on the deck), s52 177 to 186 (Taren's ship beside the skiff, not in it).
- **D409 - A pack gets its teeth back: one whose way in is shut by its own goes round the hero to another side (November 24, every pack-hunter fight).** D408 kept a pack's bodies out of one another and took its pressure: a pack hunter still told its lunge with one of its own on its way in (the tell in `EnemyBrain.Update` asked only `InOneOfItsOwn`), and the lunge stopped at the one in front (`LungeAttack`'s `IntoOneOfItsOwn`) 2.7 to 6 m off the hero and bit nothing, so the two behind the first queued at its back and lunged into it every cooldown (as merged: 10 lunges a run held by their own, 3 marks in 15 biting). Now a pack hunter looks along its way to the hero before it tells (`LaneShut`: the lunge's own stopping rule stepped 0.2 m at a time over up to 8 m, its pack where they stand or where a lunge they are telling will end) and, where one of its own would stop it out of biting reach, tells nothing and goes round him instead (`Round`: sideways, away from the side its own are on, two metres off his capsule) until its way opens; walking or going round, it keeps out of a lane another is telling (`InLane`). D408's spine spacing, the tell refused inside one of its own and the lunge stopped at one are unchanged; packs did not fall back to the old spacing. Log: `LUNGE_HELD_BY_OWN`. Bites on a standing Taren in 12 s, `BodiesKeepApartTests.APackOfGloomgrazersInTheRelayBitesFromRoundTheHeroAsBefore` (three in Relay_1, runs in each state): before D408 10, 10, 10; as merged 5, 0, 5, 5, 5 (d409b) and 5, 5, 5, 5, 5 (d409c); now 12, 10, 14, 12, 14 (d409c), none of them in him (0.04 m, as before) or in each other (0.00 m2; before 0.11 to 0.47), the least bearing between two at him 55 to 75 degrees (before 20). `APackOfRidgehoundsInAFileBitesFromRoundTheHeroAsBefore`: before 13, 13, 13; merged 5, 5, 5; now 12, 13, 13, 12, 13. D408's file test: 10 and 15 now (13 before, 5 merged). Look at every gorge and relay pack (s14, s15): the grazers should come at Taren from two or three sides, none waiting at another's back.
- **D409 (continued) - The pack's pressure is held to seven tenths of the old number, not to all of it (November 24, the lead).** The first run of D409's two checks beside other work (`d410a`, a windowless scout playing on the same machine) failed both: Gloomgrazers 9 and 10 bites of the old 10, Ridgehounds 9 of the old 13, with nine lunges still held by one of their own in a run, where the editor alone gave 10 to 14 and 12 to 13. So the circling D409 added opens the way in less often at a lower frame rate, and a player on a slower machine meets a pack that bites up to three tenths less than before D408. That is far from the pack D408 left (5 bites, or none) and it is not fixed: it is registered. The checks now hold the runs' mean to seven tenths of the old number and each run to six, which the merged D408 state still fails, so the suite's four editors side by side do not turn them red by scatter. Scouts on the 22:35 build (D404 to D409): routes 14, 3, 8, 23, 46, 35 and 15 passed first time.
- **D410 - The Host Marshal's fight: the replay breaks its thaw couplings, its heat fails on its own, ranks keep apart and stand in ice (November 25, chapter 6).** nov24-whole s36 ran 354 s. *Couplings:* the replay strikes what the bar names by its target card (`ContinuousReview.Named`), and "BREAK A COUPLING" never named a "Thaw coupling", so every s36 broke one coupling by accident and ground the 0.3 shell (160 s warm). The goal now reads "Break a thaw coupling to put the wall back to sleep (n/3)"; the status line keeps BREAK A COUPLING. *Its cold half:* its bar is held at or under what is left of its heat over `MarshalTune.ColdMost` (110 s), never under 1, so the next blow ends it (a lead alone, Idra at 17%, ground it 190 s); `HeatSeconds` is its own 85 (the frost's 70 until now): with all three couplings broken the warm half runs about 45 to 60 s, which with 70 put the fight under two minutes. Expected: about 130 to 140 s with the EXTRA breaking coupling 1 at the door, 140 to 155 s with its call first, up to about 180 s with the partner out. *Partner:* `ReplayHands.ItemFor` gives a partner under 30% (where `PartnerBrain` stops closing) a party mend (Sync Tonic) from the lead's pouch; the route-36 pouch holds none, so s36 will not show it until a route stocks one. *Look:* the guards "drawn through each other" (s36/070 to 073) are a crowd at arm's length seen in perspective: two ranks driven onto one hero stood 0.02 m into one another at worst (their capsules keep them apart), so the guards are unchanged; a soldier put back to sleep stands in a block of the refrozen wall's own ice material (`Refreeze`), so frozen ranks read as the wall's sleepers, not dark soldiers queuing; walking, the Marshal shoulders itself clear of a thaw rig by its drawn reach plus 1.4 m (`ClearOfTheRigs`; a lunge still ends at the rig). Look at s36 070 to 073 (no heap), 075 to 086 (frozen ranks in ice, the Marshal beside the furnace, not in it), the goal line, and the length.
- **D412 - A beaten boss is drained of its colour, not only dark; who yields is not lit by the blow; the killing number is never grey (November 25).** In nov24-whole the Hulljack kept its yellow and black stripes (s29/037, 043), the Rotbloom its pink and cream on its end's frame (s26/473), the Host Marshal went dark brown, not frost blue (s36/103), though each had darkened (the Hulljack's yellow measured 178 to 109 to 75 of 255): `BodyGoesDark` darkened the paint over the texture, which keeps a texture's colours in proportion, and BossDeathTests measured lightness only. Now a colourful texture (judged once off the GPU at 16 by 16, cached) is drained through the block's `_BaseMapStrength` to `Grain` .35 (a grey one keeps its grain), the paint goes to grey (`HueKept` .15) and then its tone, lightness held on the old curve; energy parts (EnergyBubble `_Intensity` and alpha) go out with the lights; `FirstShare` is .8. Dessik Varn's suit yields, so never went dark: the killing flash washed it steel blue (s30/045), then full stripes (047). A yielding boss with no humanoid rig now goes beaten until the person takes over; a person who yields keeps her colours and `HitFlash` puts the killing light out on her (Drask washed white, s52/098). `GameHud` dresses the killing number as a blow, never resisted grey ('1621' and '765' were killing numbers on the end's own frame). Tests (BossDeathTests, d412d 49/49): drained a second on and on the end's frame, measured against the body drawn plain in the same frame (raw, the arena's warm light read every dark body as .3), the Marshal blue, Dessik dark, a yielding person no lighter; TheColourMeasureSeesABodyDarkenedOverItsStripes (darkened only: .41 to .51, fails), TheKillingBlowsNumberIsNotGrey. Look at s29/037 and 043, s30/045, s36/103, s26/473, s52/098, s21/288.
- **D411 - The Foundry Saint: an opening takes a third of an arm at most, the replay's lead goes round a fallen road's foot, and the override cables go when it settles (November 25, chapter 9).** nov24-whole s59 ran 109.6 s and same-day scouts 94 and 217 s, against the 120 to 300 s rule: with one arm an opening (D364) a pair that filled its openings freed an arm in one or two, an EXTRA paying off a whole arm. *Cap:* `SaintTune.OpeningShare` = ArmShare/3 (9,333 of 140,000). `SaintHoldLine` holds at the larger of its line and where the opening began (`saintOpenedAt`: taken the first time a standing collar is unlocked or the press is open, forgotten once nothing is) less that share and one point, so three full openings end just past an arm's line; bare, only the head's lift is held, at the same share, and a climb outside it is not. The diagnosis's half an arm was checked against the code and not taken: at the code's opening about every 10 s a pair that fills every opening needs six for the arms, 94 to 111 s in all, the short runs as they were; at a third it needs nine, 124 s or more. A plain player's lift (about 3,800) and the length test's (.59, about 6,200) never reach it, so that test is unchanged; D375's EXTRA floor still applies and an EXTRA is now worth a third of an arm. Each opening logs `SAINT_OPENING <from> to <to>`. *Driver:* `ContinuousReview.SaintRound`: a resting road whose line the straight way to a collar's spot crosses short of its foot is walked round, past its foot on his own side then the spot's (logs `QUALITY_SAINT_ROUNDS`); the 217 s run's lead pushed straight into arm 1's side at (-8.5, 190) from lift 5 to lift 11. *Look:* the six thin pale cyan rods over the collars after the fight (s59/038, 084) were the released arms' slack override cables, LineRenderers that BodyGoesDark (mesh renderers only) never touched; `SaintArm.PowerDown` turns them off. Expect route 59 at about 130 to 160 s.
- **D413 - Nobody is left inside a beaten boss, measured by what it draws; the companion stops her swing the moment her boss is beaten (November 25).** From nov24-whole. D407's `WreckClearance` already eased both heroes (its log names Sela and Idra) but measured the capsule, and what is drawn reaches further: Sela was let go at 4.24 m of the Hulljack's middle with its head over her ship (s29/043), Taren at 2.50 m of the Floodcrown's against its head (s21/288), Idra at 1.30 m of Vessa with the pole through her (s48/229); in Dessik Varn's arm (s30/045) Taren was outside the capsule, so nothing ran. Now the clearance reads the posed body every .25 s (a readable skin point by point; every other part, a held prop, a hull, a mesh a build can't read, by its own box turned with it, sampled over its faces: the same in editor and player), and a hero of its form, lead or partner, on foot or in flight (his hull's reach), is in it while anything of it at his feet or over him on the plan (what the camera looking down draws in him; a ring on the floor is not) is within his reach and .15 m. He slides out over .3 s the shortest open way within 90 degrees of straight out (never back across its middle), walls and deck edges still stopping him, for 6 s after the end on the heroes' clock (the kill's stop, the card, the first lines, a lead walking on into it), a death and a yield alike (both end in BossController's handler). Dessik's loose crates in it are slid off the same way and set down clear of the heroes. `PartnerBrain`: when the boss she fights is beaten mid-swing or mid-skill, her action is cut there. A beaten boss already took no blow (Health.Receive returns nothing at nought; every blade, shot and dash skips it): s36/103 is Taren's killing blow inside the kill's stop (frames: step 104 opens at gameDelta 0, Attack2), the 1621 its own number; the specks in s41/058 are round Idra in s41/056 too, before the end. Judged the camera: Hessa (s15/158 to 205), feet about 1.8 m from Taren's, his head over her shins from 39 degrees up, as D408 judged the Warmaster. Left: stills taken inside the kill's stop (s30/045, s52/171) show the kill itself, before anything moves; a hero in a living body's reach is the stand-off's (D408, D415). Tests d413b (mirror S) 15 of 15: `BossDeathTests.ABeatenBossEasesAHeroLeftInsideItOutOfItWithinASecond` now with the partner beside the lead and Dessik, the Floodcrown and Vessa added (both clear of what it draws in all 13; as before, the capsule left the lead .35 m inside the skiff, .29 the Floodcrown, .28 Meret's ship, .24 Vessa, .13 the Cutter), the deck case (out west along the deck), and `TheCompanionStopsHerSwingTheMomentTheBossSheFightsIsBeaten` (beaten with .50 s of her swing left, two frames on she is Idle). The Hulljack is tested on foot only. Look at s29/043, s21/288 to 292, s48/229 to 231, s52/173 to 186, s30/047 on, s41/058.
