- **D415 - Idra's Shelter is a ring and a low rim at its reach, not a dome; at the Warmaster she keeps off the most his mantle has swung toward her (November 25, chapters 7 and 8).** *The Shelter (nov24-whole s46/128, 132, s48/229):* D387's clear sphere still stood 5 m tall over a 5 m reach, so its limb was a circle 880 to 1200 px across over the party and the skill row (behind the HUD, which is a screen overlay: nothing in the world draws over it) with its glass amber over a third of the frame. Its reach (5 m, 7 m in flight), five seconds and what it stops are as they were and as its text says; it is now drawn as its floor ring (GroundRing, the party's cream, clipped at walls) and a band 0.38 m high on that ring, pale amber at its foot (alpha .7, under white) and gone by its top, rising over 0.25 s and sinking over its last 0.4 s; nothing inside its edge (`ShelterDome.Stand/Pose`, IdraEffects.cs; in flight the flight arc and the same band). Runtime material from `Effects/flare_01` with a white map and vertex colours, as the tow line: no keyword, no variant. Renders `Builds/quality/look/d415/` (before and after at s46/128's camera; `ShelterLook.Render`). Test: `HarrowgateBossTests.IdrasShelterIsARingAndALowRimAtItsReachNotADome` (as D387: 5.00 m high, drawn from its middle; now 0.40 m high, nothing nearer than 4.78 m) replaces D387's glass check. *Idra in the Warmaster (s52/083 to 098):* what was in her was his mantle, a rigid mesh on his spine bone that swings with his idle and walk to 1.32 to 1.38 m behind him; his outline was read once 0.3 s after he woke (1.09 to 1.11 m there), and his war-frame added nothing to it (DraskVaal_clean.fbx was not Read/Write, so it fell to its box's corners, all under his feet or over a hero's head). The model is now readable (its .meta), and her stand-off reads `EnemyBrain.HeldReachToward`: his outline read again every 0.3 s while she is asked, each bearing keeping the most it has reached (a pack's spacing keeps the one read); her hand past it is 0.2 m, no less than her 0.15 m step-out, so her swings' creep never takes her inside before she steps back (PartnerBrain). `BodiesKeepApartTests.AtTheWarmasterThePartnerFightsFromBesideWhatHeDraws` no longer ignored: as before 0.34 m of his mantle in her at 1.33 m; now 0.03 walking in (0.9 s, before the envelope grew) and 0.00 after a charge, settled 1.87 m (stand-off 1.99), 13 swings as before, 5,594 landed against 6,659. d415a 27 of 27. Look at s46/127 to 140 (a pale ring and low rim, no dome), s48/229, s52/083 to 100 (Idra a body's width clear of his cape).
- **D417 - Five small things in flight places: the Orrery's gates are open rings, its landing beacon stands clear of the ship, the Last Mooring's loose pieces ride lighters, the passage's lantern disc is gone and its tow line is rope, the Terminator's mated coupling lamp is green (November 25, chapters 7, 9 and 10).** Seen in nov24-whole. *The gates* (s38/081, 101, 104, 107): the pale bar was the generated RailGate's own bar through its ring's middle (the pivot its lean is measured about), textured in the map's pale sky patches; `OrielBuilder.Orrery.Cut` now leaves it out in a slab 2.2 m either side of the middle across the opening and past the rim, cut in the ring's space as drawn (cut where the points were, the slimmed long triangles left 2.5 m stubs, d417-after); short bronze bosses stay at the inner face. *The landing* (s38/110): the beacon was centred on the prompt's place and a ship at the prompt (z 875) had its nose over its base; the beacon and its solid stand 3.3 m north (drawn z 882 to 884.5), the prompt's place and range unchanged. *The Last Mooring* (s64/070 to 083): the raft bay, the Crawl's four tread-halls, Lastlight's three longhouses with their arches, the market district and Driftreed's two queued rafts each ride a lighter (`Mooring.Lighter`: a dark iron deck barge with a teal band, rails, bollards, a warm and a cold running lamp in iron hoods with pools, cradles under higher loads); the second queued raft rides the first's lighter and goes through with it (two lighters 12 m apart would have met), so three hulls still go through, the market first. s64/016's tug over the freighter is depth read at that camera (the hauler's top is 10 m under the plane): left. *The passage* (s68/105): the disc was a 7 m lantern glow under each three drums, bloomed; gone (each drum keeps its own pool). The rod is the tug's tow line (a LineRenderer, by design): tarred (.42, .33, .22) and .1 wide instead of pale tan .14. *The Terminator* (s56/059): the white globe was the third coupling's lamp lit as mated (.82, .86, .8) over the crawl's pale lamp glass; its glass is the engine car's dark lens now and mated is signal green (.18, .85, .32). Existing materials only, nothing made at run time. Rebuilt, relit, restaged, route facts: Orrery, LastMooring, ReturningPassage, Terminator; nothing a route, gate, prompt or fight uses moved; route_lint 141 OK. Tests (each reads the capture's state first): `OrielWorldTests.NothingCrossesAGatesOpeningAtItsMiddle`, `.TheLandingBeaconStandsClearOfAShipAtItsPrompt`, `QaNov24FloorsTests.EveryCouplingsLampIsAColourNeverAWhiteGlobe`; focused d417 216 of 219, 3 skipped by design. Renders `Builds/quality/look/d417-after2` (Orrery, LastMooring) and `d417-after` (passage, Terminator). Look at s38/081 to 107 and 110, s64/070 to 083, s68/105, s56/059.
- **D418 - What the whole suite found on the round-four tree (nov25a, 8 red of 3,973): one game fault, one stale fixture, the rest the load of four editors (November 25).** *RecordedReleaseSpacingTests (two held-leader cases):* D407's WreckClearance began on the freed Cantor too (it is a boss with a capsule), and slid the lead the fixture holds still 0.36 m out of its capsule (WRECK_EASE Cantor Taren 3.04 of 3.33 m). The Cantor is no wreck: it turns and flies off at once, the companion's own guide keeps her clear of it, and the lead is the player's to fly. `WreckClearance.Begin` now skips a body with `SerpentSegments` (the Cantor's alone); in play a lead beside the Cantor as it is freed is no longer shoved. *ReleaseSpacingTests (three cases):* frame-starved, not changed by D405 to D409: in part 7 every case of the class ran at 26 to 45 fps with 0.1 s stalls at different moments (96 to 186 samples where alone and in nov24b they are 217), and at those steps the companion's guide met the departing body late (front-180 crossed it by 0.08 m at 2.26 s). Left as it is: the count is the check that the departure was sampled at the game's own rate. *TheCompanionKeepsUpOnFoot(WalkingCanopy):* nothing in D405 to D409 acts on a walk with no fight, talk, berth, walker or boss; alone 14.2 m against 18, the gap opening at 7 to 8 s where she climbs a raised piece east of the route at (-33, -5.1, 20.7), backs 3.6 m off it and drops 1.6 m while the lead goes on north; nov25a's 21.4 m was the same walk under load. The case now prints where the gap was widest and a line a second. *TownLifeTests.MiraPlantsHerFeetOnHerWalkHome:* the control read 0.114 against its 0.12 (0.143 to 0.148 in nine suites before); alone 0.155 with her walk never held. She is a ScriptedWalk, which D408's WalkLoop change does not touch; the test now counts the frames her walk stands held and names what held it, so a repeat says whether a stopped walker stood in her way. *RotbloomDriverTests:* its fixture asked the old driver to fail one of two ways (behind a plinth, or a second stalk a minute late); its first burn comes late (107 s in nov24b, 111 s alone today, of its 150 s) and in nov25a it burned none and fell, which the check below fails as plainly ('no stalk burned'): the fixture now takes that as the old driver failing too. d418a: 76 of 76 (RecordedReleaseSpacingTests and ReleaseSpacingTests, held leader excursion 0 and no WRECK_EASE, samples 215 to 218; TownLifeTests; RotbloomDriverTests 250.1 s fight; TheCompanionKeepsUpOnFoot in all 25 zones). Nothing drawn changed, no zone rebuilt, no route to regenerate.
- **D414 - A hero hidden by scenery, a wreck or a boss is one clean line round the hidden part, and scenery opens no hole for a lead drawn through it (November 25, the camera).** *nov24-whole s13/007, s11/007, s16/142, s06/048-050, s16/114, s50/071, s59/084, s41/052:* `HeroThrough`'s Fresnel rim over a .3-.4 fill was, from the high camera, a bright near-solid glass figure that read as the hero standing on what hid them. Now each body has two run-time copies drawn with `Resources/Effects/HeroThrough.shader` (one pass, no keyword or variant; the eight materials differ only by properties, stencil bit 16, queues Transparent+40/+41): the body marks its pixels in the stencil, lays a flat .09 tint where hidden and draws the line where the scenery's edge crosses it (scene depth sampled a line's width away), so a half-hidden body closes; a hull pushed out 3 px of 1080 along its normals in clip space draws the silhouette's line off every body's stencil, only where hidden. Gold lead, pale blue partner, the same through a boss (D395: no door for a hero drawn through). `SceneryOcclusion` opens its hero hole only for a lead nothing draws through (`BossOcclusion.Outlined`): the 'dark square hole' round Taren on Harrowgate's gate roof (s50/071) was that hole showing the dark passage under it; what passes under the lens still fades whole. Player: depth texture and stencil are already used in play (the old drawing, the telegraphs), the shader loads from Resources, no keyword. `HeroOutlineTests` (the capture's places at 1920x1080, the old drawing as a test-only fixture shader first): closed .85-1.00 of the hidden edge, inside change .05-.13, surface read through .90-1.00 (old: covered .61-1.00, read through -.23 to .51). Shared measures that read the drawing as a fill now read the share of the hidden part's edge the line closes round (`OutlineMeasure`: `HeroInSightTests.Seen`, `QaFightThirdTests`); six scenery-hole tests switch the lead's drawing off first. Renders: Builds/quality/look/d414. Look at s13/007, s11/007, s06/050, s50/071, s59/084, s41/052.
- **D416 - HUD plates are solid at rest, a loot line keeps the world from behind its words, the quest card is seated after the objective, a fight's line goes with its boss, a sign the prompt is saying is away, the way's mark keeps off the lock card (November 25, HUD and labels).** From nov24-whole. *Plates (s57/155, 156):* the vitals were not faded; the frame sheet's opening is 254 of 255 and the canvas blends in linear light, so the Decks notice board's chalk (TMP text, not a dressed sign) lifted the plate's red 6 to 22 through it. `PanelFade.Solid` puts a backing of the plate's colour inside the border (first child) and fades it with the plate: vitals, objective, quest card (not the one-line TextPlates, which hide by their image). *Bodies behind a panel (s46/132):* kept (D356/D222): the plate steps aside for the mortar the party fights and each line keeps its band (at most .06 of the world); left as designed. *Feed (s29/043):* TEA ROOM is the roof's mesh lettering (D356), nothing to take away; each `PickupLine` has its own band at .75 over the feed's .8 (a twentieth through). *Quest card (s52/171):* it was seated in Update, a frame before the objective (LateUpdate, 905) wrote three lines; now seated in LateUpdate at 906. Moving it off a still Sela (s08/098) was built and taken out: it fought D394's keep-out (PartnerInViewTests, QaHudThirdTests red). *Bark (s52/098):* FightOn never fell between the Warmaster's yield and the skiff; a fight's line also goes when a boss fighting as it was said is down (`BarkPanel.BossDown`). *Landing (s38/110):* a dressed sign is away ('said') while `PromptService.Interaction`'s prompt begins with its words. *Lock (s62/121):* the cream goal mark searched only toward the middle, onto the card; it now tries outward too (8 steps); the card on the two interceptors is D222's nowhere-clear, left. Judged wrong: the tags (s55/109, s51/178 are 76 px tall like Pip's 71 and MIRA's 80; Dorrow's is wide because his name is 21 letters). The white 12 at s52/171 is Idra's damage taken (damage.json step 170, 11.7, 333 left). Tests: QaNov24LabelTests (four D416 cases). Look at s57/156, s29/043, s52/171, s52/098, s38/110, s62/121.
- **D419 - A beaten boss's end, the cases round five did not reach: the rooms' own drawings of a beaten body, a person who walks out, the Burrower's glow (November 25, bosses' ends).** From nov25-r5-chain. *Tollkeeper (s65/126), Foundry Saint (s59/053, 076):* each room draws the beaten body itself once `bossdown.<id>` is set (FlagGate: 'The Tollkeeper, as it stays (drawn after its fall)', 'The Foundry Saint, settled'), from the end and on every visit after, and nothing darkened that drawing: the carrier lay white with its windows lit (the fight's dark body gives way to it within a frame of the end), and the press's cream panels and white shoulder blocks showed through the Saint's dark body where the two stood together. `BodyGoesDark.StandIn` takes a room's drawing dark as shut down (TollkeeperCage when it shows; SaintDown, and SaintArm.Start on a later visit) and does not draw it while the fight's body stands in its place (the Saint's). *Vessa and Hessa Korl (s48/227-244, s15/158):* both are persons beaten, not killed. Hessa yields and talks and already kept her colours (the 'black lump' is her oxblood mantle and iron shoulder plates seen from behind, as she fought: light .29 -> .29 in d419a). Vessa walks out (BossDeparture) and was taken dark as a machine driven off, a black figure; `BossDeparture.keepsColours`, set in VessaStart, keeps her colours, and HitFlash puts the killing light out on her as on one who yields. Meret's gantry (rides the lift down) is unchanged. *Burrower (s06/045, 050):* the slate is its body burning toward its wreck's dark tint (dark by s06/064); kept. Its seams' glow (BurrowerShutdown) now drops to .4 on the end's frame and is out .45 s later in real time through the kill's stop (it was 1.15 s of the game's clock, which the stop holds: lit at 045). *The Tollkeeper's blotches (s65/064, 126):* in the generated model (small faces mapped onto the window band's texture, its cyan lights in them); not meant, not run time; left. BossDeathTests: a person who walks out keeps her colours like one who yields (end frame and a second on at least .8 of her living light); new: TheMeasureSeesAPersonWhoWentDark, ARoomsOwnDrawingOfABeatenBodyIsDark (Tollkeeper, FoundrySaint), TheSaintsSettledPressIsNotDrawnThroughItsBeatenBody. d419a 60/60.
- **D421 - A hero hidden whole by one broad piece of scenery stands in its window; a hero hidden only in a small part is not drawn through (November 25, the camera).** *nov25-r5-chain s51/218, s13/007, s26/195; s55/139, 146, s59/053, 076, s21/275:* round five's line and tint lying wholly on a lit roof, an arch's top or a ledge read as the hero standing ON it (nothing of them showed past an edge), and the line round feet behind a rail or a collar read as a loose scrap. `SceneryOcclusion` again opens its hero-sized window for a hero drawn through it, lead or now partner, when seven points round them (feet to a margin over the head, either side) all lie behind that one piece; one window a piece, the lead's first (one wide enough for both opened most of Harrowgate's gatehouse). A lead nothing draws through keeps the chest rule. `HeroThrough.shader` reads the share of the body hidden from the scene's depth at 27 points over the hero's capsule (`_Hero`, per copy by a property block) and fades the drawing in from .3 to .5 of him; each point is placed by its offset from the fragment's own projection, the rows' direction measured by ddy, so a render texture's flip and the back buffer's agree. Partial hiding past an edge, bosses, wrecks, banners and doors keep the line. QaFightThirdTests' outline bar is now .8, D414's bar for this measure (.85 was D395's fill bar; round five read .84 to .87 there). The partner gets no window from a piece that hides the lead's chest (s50/071 keeps both lines). BodiesKeepApartTests' Bellows far side (D386) asked for his feet and shins, a fifth of him, drawn over the dome; under the .3 rule nothing is drawn there and the case now holds that. Look for: s51/218 Taren and s13/007, s26/195 Sela in a window in the roof, arch and pillar; no scrap at s55/139, 146, s59/053, 076; s50/071, s11/007, s06/050 as before.
- **D420 - Where bodies stand and what they still do when a fight ends (November 25, round six).** From nov25-r5-chain. *Vessa (s48/227 to 244):* the clearance did run (WRECK_EASE at step 228, Taren clear at 2.17 m on the plan); what the stills show is the camera, from 40 degrees up, drawing her over his legs from just beyond her, then her walking out (BossDeparture) into him while each ease took him the shortest way out, along her way, 3.4 m to the wall. `WreckClearance` now counts, for a hero on foot and a body at most 3.2 m tall, the floor it hides from `Camera.main` as in it; it moves what it read with the body, counts a walking body where it will be 0.5 s on, and sends a hero in its way aside rather than ahead. *The Saint (s59/053, 076):* Taren was standing on arm 3's road top, 2.1 m up, which the clearance skipped as "on it"; as a boss ends, a hero up on a road of its arms is put on the floor with `SaintArm.StandClear` (it avoids where its last arms will land). *The Cantor (s10/148, 150):* nobody fired after the end. The bolts were fired before it and flew their 3 s through a body that takes no blow; one frame after any boss ends, the party's shots go out (`Projectile.Fizzle`). *The Warmaster (s52/092, 095):* plan-clear (about 1.5 m off) but between him and the camera. `PartnerBrain.MeleeStand` now tries places more than 70 degrees off the camera's line first (and not within 35 degrees behind him), and only falls back to the old places if none fits. `EnemyBrain` keeps a boss's held outline up from when a hero on foot comes within 6 m. Tests: d420a 26 of 27 (the red one is the Warmaster, on the tree before the EnemyBrain change), d420c 2 of 2, d420b 28 of 29 (the red one is the Bellows outline, D421's in-progress HeroThrough work). New: `BossDeathTests.ABeatenPersonWalkingOffLeavesNobodyDrawnBehindHerOrPushedAlongHerWay` (before: 2.9 s drawn over him, carried 5.04 m; now 0.0 s, 0.00 m), `APartnersShotInTheAirWhenTheBossIsBeatenGoesOut`, and `HarrowgateBossTests.WithTheLeadAtTheWarmastersFlankThePartnerFightsBesideHimOnTheScreenNotOverHim` (before: 3.4 % of him behind her; now 0.0 %). `BodiesKeepApartTests.AtTheWarmaster...` walking in is 0.00 (it was 0.10 in nov25b). Look at s48/228 on, s59/037 on, s10/149 on, s52/083 to 100.
- **D422 - In flight the companion goes with the lead's lunge, gives way to his roll, and steps back out to her stand-off from a body that comes to her (November 25).** *Lunges (nov25-r5-chain s10/012, s10/075):* a lunge is 8 m in 0.16 s, past any hull's boost; ten in a row down the Gullet's canal left Sela 10 to 14 m behind, under the skill row and off the bottom edge. Out of a fight she now answers each new dash of his (`PartnerBrain.Match`, read from his `FlightMotor.DashSequence` and his state): a lunge she goes with, by as far along his nose, where her way is open and she is not ahead of him (not while an animal's departure guides her); a skill's dash is left to him. *Roll (s10/171):* he rolled back through where she rested behind his nose, the two hulls one shape; a roll whose line passes nearer her than both hulls' reach she gives way to, a step aside with her nose kept. *Skiff (s52/162):* in flight nothing stepped her back out when a body came inside her stand-off; D408's step-out now holds in flight too, at `ShipStandOff`. `PartnerFlightPlaceTests` (d422d, 6 of 6): canal lunges 1 to 7 farthest 5.3 m and 4.4% out of the clear frame, as before 14.6 m and 72.9%; a Drifter set 1.2 m inside her 3.32 m stand-off is 3.17 m off two seconds on, as before 2.12; his roll back through her place comes no nearer than 3.10 m of 3.47, as before 0.91. Regressions green (d422, with these changes): PartnerInViewTests 14, PartnerFlightRejoinTests 10, CompanionWallTests 8, PartnerEvasionTests 5, BodiesKeepApartTests' flight case. *Not fixed:* s10/059 (staged at the capture's places she still stops idle 7.8 m off at the fold; a near trail fallback in FlightObstacles did not move her and was taken out), s10/041 and 101 (a creeping lead, below FlightTravel, keeps her place behind his nose: D397's trade-off), and the drawn traffic of s64/017, s68/023 and 028 (convoy and moored hulls are drawn only, without colliders, in Lattice.World, which Combat cannot see; Beside sweeps solids only). Look for: s10/012 and 075 Sela beside Taren through the lunges; s10/171 no shared shape; s52/162 a gap between the tug and the skiff.
- **D423 - The Terminator's panel only moves forward, the Orrery sheds no loose rods, and Iru at the meeting has form (November 25, chapters 7 and 9).** From nov25-r5. *The Terminator (s56/061, 063):* from the ride on, `DawnLine` held no words of its own (cleared while riding, empty words after), so the panel fell back to the story's step, "Re-mate three couplings and bring the tread-hall in ahead of the dawn", which only Dorrow's talk in the Crawl completes. It now says "Ride the tread-hall out" through the ride and "Climb aboard the Meridian Crawl" after it, with the arrow on the crawler; code only, no rebuild (`TerminatorTests.TheThirdCouplingWakes...` asserts both). The Crawl still shows the step for the second or two before that talk opens. *The Orrery (s38/082, 084, 110):* the rods were the shed's "lengths of old rail" (`Debris` kind 8, up to 11 m, kept clear on screen at one end only), not the gate's cut bar; they are left out with their draws still taken, so no other piece moved (`OrielWorldTests.NothingTheMoonsShedIsALooseRod`, with an old rod as its fixture). The orange-red frame at 082 is a moon's strike marking on the plane (`OrreryRail.Mark`), the game's danger warning, left as it is. *The meeting (s57/154, 157):* the townsperson was Iru's body (a dark rust and black painting) tinted .6 darker again; now lifted (1.22, 1.1, 1.02). Rebuilt, relit, restaged: Orrery, DecksLeaving. Looks d423-after, d423-after-b (s38-082, s38-084, landing), d423-meeting (beside d381-after). The Host Marshal's floor (s36/060, 124) was not changed: its waves are the cut ice's year-bands, which all run one way by the texture's construction.
- **D424 - The lead's numbers are warm whatever the hit, and the objective's arrows go with their lines the frame the words change (November 25, HUD).** *nov25-r5-chain s15/156, s15/158, s26/473, s08/092, s36/060:* the white and grey numbers on bosses were the lead's own: plain hits and killing blows in the kinds' white (a hero's own hurt's colour), hits on armour in the cold resisted grey. Every hero skill's packet carries its hero (no ring or field tick is sourceless) and no colour is decided in Combat. `GameHud` now draws the lead's numbers on a body in `LeadColor` (amber 1, .8, .55) and a hit on armour in `LeadArmourColor` (a warm grey .78, .7, .62, still a grey by QaCombatTests' and QaPlanCombatTests' measure, and still smaller); a weakness keeps its orange or yellow and marks, a crit its cream, the partner its sky blue; a hero's hurt and what nobody swung stay white. *s30/028:* the amber arrow over 'Break' was the objective's own gold arrow, not the lock's edge mark (with its small one after 'cockpit'): `ObjectiveHud` showed both for the goals of its last look before writing the fight's words, so the frame a fight began they stood seated for words with no distance and no Also line. It shows them again once the new goals are found, in the frame the words change. `SignFade`: a body's or plate's hold from before a sign is said now ends with it, so the frame the prompt stops saying a sign it is read again (a hold that outlived the prompt left it gone and marked "said"; D416's case failed on it once, d424a). *Left:* s29/043's TEA ROOM is the roof's lit letters (ManyHandsBuilder.HullLight `SignBoard`, merged into the roof's glow mesh), not a label; hiding it needs a rebuild that gives the letters a renderer of their own. s65/064 is the cut-in's slide-out (the title leaves first, the face last; the band's amber is the hero's .16 wash in linear light, as on every frame). s15/158's bark plate is .8 to .9 by QaHudSecondTests and QaHudThirdTests, so a floor beam lifts it. Tests: QaNov24LabelTests `TheLeadsNumbersAreWarmWhateverTheHitAndAHerosHurtIsNot`, `TheObjectivesArrowsGoTheFrameTheirLinesDo`.
- **D425 - In flight the companion keeps off the hulls that are only drawn, where the lanes leave her open water beside the lead (November 25, round six).** From nov25-r5-chain. *The lanes (s68/023, s68/028, s64/017):* the Returning Passage's running convoy, the Last Mooring's moored one and the traffic going by are drawn only, under the plane, in World, so `PartnerBrain.Beside` read only what is solid and Idra's tug sat beside him over a canoe or a ring hull. `Lattice.Core.DrawnHulls` keeps each such hull as its drawn box in its own frame, filled at run time with no rebuild: `ConvoyFlow` (its groups' hulls, OnEnable/OnDisable), `TrafficShip`, and `MooredHulls` (the hulls under "The convoy, moored" as its scene loads); a place is read along the play camera's ray through it, to 20 m under the plane. `Beside` keeps her side while it is off them (within half her hull's reach); else her side half ahead, else his line ahead of his nose, each off them now and a quarter second on and shown whole clear of the plates (Fits), held 1.5 s; else the first of them off them now; else her side as before. Never across his line or behind him for it: across she swung 10 m out over the lanes, behind she trailed under the control strip (d425f, d425g). At rest in flight her place behind him moves to the first of left-behind, straight behind, either side that is off them. `PartnerFlightPlaceTests` (d425): the passage's road from 330 to 900 m in, her middle over a drawn hull while open water was beside him 23.4% of the leg, as before 42.2%; 3.7% out of the clear frame (before 0) and at most 8.2 m from him (before 6.1). Where he is over the lanes himself (a fifth of that leg) or the Last Mooring's lee is hulls all round (open water beside him under a tenth of its frames) she stays at her side; s64/017 is the first lane's fight beginning, her flight to the interceptor she Breaches, a passing frame. A hull is read as its box: inside a ring segment's curve reads as over it. *The Gullet's fold (s10/059), left:* not FlightObstacles (the sweep from her to him meets only his own hull); she is fighting, his target a Shellmine and then a Chorister Drifter with the zone in combat, so her place is at her stand-off from it while the route flies him on to the next mine. To see: s68/023 and s68/028 with her ahead of him or half ahead on open water rather than over the canoe; the stills depend on where the convoy then is.
- **D426 - What the round-six scouts showed that no look had: a refrozen guard is the wall's at once, a boss on show never takes the view from a fight, and the Crawl says to tell Dorrow (November 25, the lead).** *nov24-recon-r6-36 player.log; nov25-r6-chain s64/072 to 089; s56/065, 080.* (1) At the Host Marshal the hammer that broke a coupling went on, in the same frame, to kill a guard that coupling had just refrozen: `Refreeze` moves the guard's drawing into its statue and destroys the guard, but the object goes only at the frame's end, and the blow found a live body with no animator, whose death fell as a rigid body's and read its skin's vertices. The editor reads any mesh; the player reads only one imported readable, and a person's ('char1') is not: `InvalidOperationException` out of the blow, the kill's later hearers never told. `Refreeze` now sets `Health.Damageable` false on its first line, and `DefeatPresentation.SkinPoints` gives a skin the player cannot read its drawn box's eight corners (enough to find the floor and what it falls onto) at all three places the fall read vertices. Held by `HoarfellCombatTests.AGuardTheWallHasTakenBackIsNotStruckInTheFrameItGoes`. (2) The Tollkeeper is shown from its checkpoint to a pocket 100 m wide (`DormantEnemy.Interest`, a composed view at whatever distance it takes), and the Last Mooring's second lane and the guns' gap are fought inside it: whenever the lead had no target within 30 m the view went back 350 to 450 m to show the whole carrier and the lane was fought with the ships 20 px long, in the captures of November 23 and 24 as well. `PartyController.FrameFlightEncounter` offers a waiting boss's show only while nothing awake is within the distance a lock is framed from (80 m, `EnemyBrain.AwakeNear`); with the lane empty the show is the checkpoint's again. Held by `LastMooringZoneTests.TheTollkeeperOnShowDoesNotTakeTheViewFromALaneBeingFought`, which shows the old view first (`PartyController.ShowOverAFight`). (3) Back aboard the Meridian Crawl after the ride, the panel fell to the story's step ("Re-mate three couplings and bring the tread-hall in ahead of the dawn", done only by Dorrow's talk) for the walk to her, a line just done read again after D423's "Ride the tread-hall out" and "Climb aboard the Meridian Crawl". `CrawlLine` (made as the Crawl's scene opens; nothing rebuilt) says "Tell Dorrow the tread-hall is in" through `ZoneLine` while `home.rideDone` is set and `home.treadRecovered` is not, the arrow left to the story's. Held by `HomeWorldTests.AboardTheCrawlWithTheTreadHallInThePanelSaysToTellDorrow`. Also `QaNov23LabelTests.NothingShowsThroughAFullPlate` puts its plate at full before it measures: it takes whichever sign comes first, and one already faded out left the plate drawing nothing either way. Look for: s36's log with no exception; s64/072 to 089 with the ships at fight size; s56/065 to 080 reading "Tell Dorrow the tread-hall is in".
- **D427 - A partner hidden whole by a piece that holds the lead's window stands in a window of her own in it (November 25, the camera).** *nov25-r6-chain s21/295; s11/007 and s26/537 left:* on the Walking Canopy's root arch Taren stood in its window and Sela, hidden whole by the same root, was only her line on its upper face and read as standing on top of it: D421 opened one window a piece, the lead's first. `SceneryOcclusion` now opens her a second hero-sized window in that piece when it holds the lead's (still none where the piece hides the lead's chest but not all of him: s50/071 keeps both lines); `LatticeToon` takes it as `_OccFadeEllipse2` in the same property block, a uniform branch in `FbOccFadeMask` with no keyword (zero size is none), so the fade variants the player already draws hold it. `HeroOutlineTests.APartnerHiddenWholeByThePieceHoldingTheLeadsWindowStandsInHerOwn`: with one window a piece .33 of her in colour between the root's strands and 6531 px drawn through, now .83 and none; Taren .97. *s11/007 left:* a head rule (nothing drawn while the scene's depth is clear at axis points .82 and .92 of her height) cleared Sela on the lift door in the editor but cleared Taren under the gate roof too (s50/071): from the high camera his crown covers his face's rows, and the two places differ by about 10 px at play size; it also took the line off heroes behind the Bellows and the Burrower unless bosses and wrecks are kept out. Taken out whole. *s26/537 left:* Sela stands at the pillar's inner edge and the two points .42 m to her right (D421's margin past the body) are seen through the arch's opening, so the piece never counts as hiding her whole; at the test's nearer follow 40 % of her shows past the pillar, and the capture's wider framing could not be held in a test. Look for: s21/295 Sela in her own window beside Taren's; s50/071, s51/218, s13/007, s26/195 as before.
- **D428 - A waiting boss is shown from its viewpoint on, not from behind it (November 25, the lead).** *nov24-recon-r6b-64 s64/072 to 075, 089.* D426 kept the Tollkeeper's show off the Last Mooring's second lane while it was fought, and the next replay showed the rest of the fault: on the way into the lane, and again the moment it was won, the view still went back 200 to 270 m to the whole carrier, the ships 12 px long, because the pocket a big animal is shown from is a sphere round its viewpoint that reaches to the animal (126 m round the Tollkeeper's checkpoint) and so reaches as far back the other way, over the guns' gap and the whole of lane two. `DormantEnemy.Interest` now shows an animal only to a viewer who is no farther from it than its viewpoint is, plus 8 m (`ShownBehind`): the carrier is introduced at its checkpoint and from there on, as it was built to be, and the lanes behind are flown and fought at the ships' own size. Small pockets (the Cantor's 3.5 m) lie wholly inside the margin and are unchanged; the Mother of Doors and Meret's craft are shown from their own viewpoints on in the same way. `DormantEnemy.ShownFromBehind` is a test's before. Held by `LastMooringZoneTests.TheTollkeeperOnShowDoesNotTakeTheViewFromALaneBeingFought` (the won lane: before 205 m, now 26 m; the checkpoint and the way on still shown) with the Cantor's, the second sky's and the Continuance's preview tests green beside it (`r6show`, 43 of 43). Seen in the player: `nov24-recon-r6c-64` flies and fights both the gap and lane two at 26 to 77 m (10 frames of 6,537 beyond 150 m, where the round-six replay had 1,031), and `nov24-recon-r6c-65` still opens the Tollkeeper with its card and the carrier whole (129.0 s, inside its bar).

D429 (November 25, the lead): the replay driver takes the lead back as the hero the route named, once he has fallen and been raised. Suite `nov25c`, the first whole run on the round-six tree, failed one test of 4,015: `RotbloomDriverTests` measured the Rotbloom's fight at 310 s for the driver against its sheet's ceiling of 300. Not round six's: the same test read 283 s in `nov23b` and 240 s in `nov24b`, and 218 s in `nov25b`, and the three long fights are the three in which Taren fell in the grounded half. The game gives the lead to Sela when he falls, the driver raises him, and then it fought on as Sela for the rest of the fight with Taren the partner: on a bloom that drinks her beam (it lands at half) the last fifth of the bar took two minutes where Taren leads it down in half of one. A person takes their own hero back. The driver now remembers who the route last asked to lead (an `ensureCharacter` step, or a step that says who leads at its end; a step that presses Swap and says neither leaves nobody named), sees him fall, and once he is raised and on his feet presses Swap, half a second apart and at most four times a fall, where it has its own hands: on foot, in a zone with fights, with nothing on screen, on a step that is not swapping for itself or holding the skill button. He is then mended like any lead. Nothing else of a route changes: with nobody fallen there is nothing to take back, which is every fight of the last three captures that ended inside its bar. `ContinuousReview.takeBackOff` is a test's before. Held by `RotbloomDriverTests.ALeadWhoFellAndWasRaisedTakesTheLeadBack` (felled by the test six seconds into the first round: before, Sela still leads fifteen seconds after raising him; now Taren leads again 1.2 s after he is raised) and by a new measure in the long fight test, the longest Sela led with Taren on his feet beside her, held under ten seconds (0.0 s in a fight where he fell and was raised: 154 s, inside the sheet). The first version saw his fall only on frames when the driver was neither raising nor dodging and missed one in the long fight; it is asked every frame now. Focused on this tree: `d429d` 3 of 3, `d429e` 63 of 63 (the driver's own hands, its bounds, the party of three); the boss replays are D430's.

D430 (November 25, the lead): the whole suite is whole. `tools/split_suite.py` counted a class's tests by `[UnityTest]` and `[Test]` closed at once, so a class whose every test carries more in its brackets (`[UnityTest,Timeout(900000)]`) counted none and was left out of the parts: nine classes, about 70 tests, ran in no whole suite until now: `BargeSkimmerReplayTests`, `BellowsDomeTests`, `FloorPartyBossReplayTests`, `MeretReplayTests`, `MooringBossReplayTests`, `PartnerFlightPlaceTests`, `PartnerFlightRejoinTests`, `PartnerInViewTests` and `PartnerNeverSolidTests`. They were only ever run focused, by whoever was working in them. Found when the boss replays were run to hold D429 and two failed with no take-back pressed in either. The splitter counts a test by its opening now (249 classes, 240 before). Their state on this tree, run focused: the boss replays with Harrowgate's, the workshop's, Home's and the Last Mooring's boss tests (`d429f`) 105 of 108, red: `FloorPartyBossReplayTests` Drask Vaal's duel (the test's driver got .40 and .45 of flat out against the sheet's .63: 145 s where the player's last two fights were 104 and 99 s), `MeretReplayTests` the well (.30 and .32 against the sheet's .49, at 133 to 147 s where the player's was 131 s: the flat-out yardstick reads 4,400 a second, half as much again as the share was set against), and `MooringBossReplayTests.AtHerSeatTheLeadStrikesTheTenderBesideTheClamp` (its fixture's broken state felled the tender all the same in one run of two); the other five classes (`d429g`) 37 of 39 beside the player's build (the Bellows' cost to the flat-out pair 445 a second against a floor of 448, and Idra's tug out of the clear frame for 7.2% of the Returning Passage's leg against 5%), and both of those classes 12 of 12 run again with the machine quiet (`d429h`): load, not a fault. None of the three is a fault a player meets: each fight is inside its sheet's seconds in the player and in the test, and what fails is the test's own measure of the driver against a yardstick, or its fixture. They are left red and named, to be answered against the sixth capture's fight lengths before the next whole suite, which is the first that runs them.

D431 (November 26, the tests): two of D430's three red tests are answered against the sixth capture's fights. Drask Vaal's share is .50, and the floor-party replay fights him with the party the chain brings. The .63 was his share at 165,000; at 225,000 the chain's four fights (108.7, 103.7, 98.7 and 133.8 s, against an unchanged 3,671 a second) give .551, set a tenth lower for room. The replay now brings nov25-whole s51's pair: mastery 5, 5, 4 and 3, EXTRAs full, second modules. It reads 105 to 126 s at .488 to .567, at least 8% above its nine tenths, and the duel's slowest pair comes to 186 s of 200. The Mother's seat test plays its "as it was" chase with her moves held, because her random second sweep had made that state a coin toss; it held in three runs. Neither is a change a player meets: no player code reads a sheet's share.

D432 (November 26, round seven, the camera): a hero behind scenery is simply hidden, and the lead is drawn through it only when he would otherwise be lost. *nov25-whole s30/196, s22/061, s25/012, s21/327, s16/114, s15/057, s11/007, s17/005, s26/537, s42/101, s48/275, s50/063, s51/218:* a line round anyone hidden by .3 or more, lying on a lit top face, read as a ghost standing on it; the eighth look called it the game's most repeated fault. Ordinary hiding is the depth cue. `HeroThrough` now sets its thresholds per hero through the property block. The partner is never drawn through scenery, and her window (D427) never opens. The lead is drawn from .9 to .95 hidden and never while the middle of his head shows (`_Head`, no keyword); hidden whole by one broad piece, he keeps his window. Behind a boss, its arms or its wreck (`BossOcclusion.Between`), or in a live boss fight, the lead keeps .3 to .5, and the partner is drawn from .9. Ships are unchanged. `HeroThrough.AsItWas` is the tests' before. `r7hidden-a` 72 of 72: Many Hands wall 1678 px of line before, 0 now; kiln 774 px before, 0 now; canopy root no window of her own; Bellows outline .83 (.00 under the scenery rule).

D433 (November 26, round seven, two skies): the Spore Veil's "sky seam" was the mire's square water sheet, its near edge crossing the screen dead straight above the middle at s23/193 to 211. The water is now solid to 150 m from its middle and fades out over 72 m more (`wend-mire-edge`, alpha-blended), and what lies on it stands only on the solid part. Xylos's mirror glass read as holes in the sky to a reviewer who was not told what it is: its panes were as dark as space and had no edge. The mirror is the zone's landmark and its layout stays; panes are now blue-silver with a soft sheen and a little light of their own, every pane and shard has a pale cut edge (`xylos-pane-edge`), and stream shards more than 150 m from the mirror are capped at a ship's length. LookDev's flight stills now turn the sky as `SkyDrift` does in play. `r7-skies` 114 of 114.

D434 (November 26, round seven, two labels and a board): CASTING GALLERY lay 2.6 m past the Foundry stair's foot, behind masonry kept under the sightline only to a hero at a floor's edge, and read 'CASTING GALL' from the stair's head (s48/155, s49/152). It lies at (-10.8, 137.6) now, between where the wall cuts it (east of x -9.4) and where the status panel does (west of -12.1). UNSWORN CAMP lay a metre behind the cradle bay's west lamp (CradleBay, shared, so the word moved). It lies at (32.3, 201.3) now, on the camera's side of the lamp and clear of it from the way in and from the cradle (s46/164, s47/159, 178). The sign shader draws a word 6 m nearer the camera than it lies, yet the player drew that lamp over R and N; why is unexplained. `QaNov26PlaceLabelTests` reads each word from the capture's cameras both as drawn and where it lies: 3 to 32 per cent hidden at the old places, none at the new. The Terminator's pale square (s56/015) was the schoolroom car's blank number board; it is a 4 in the couplings' hazard yellow now.

D435 (November 26, the lead): a chapter's card that a conversation cuts into is not picked up where it left off. `QuestToast` stopped a card's clock while a conversation or a menu had the screen and ran it on afterwards, so a chapter's card cut into in its last seconds came back for a second and a half when the talk closed, over whatever stood there (nov25-whole s30/028: CHAPTER 3 over Dessik's suit). Now, when the screen is taken from a chapter's card: read (2.5 s for a title alone, 6 s for one with words), it is over; barely up, it starts again whole when the screen is free. `QuestToast.resumesAsItWas` is the test's before. Held by `ChapterCardTests.AChaptersCardATalkCutsIntoIsNotPickedUpWhereItLeftOff` (before: back for 1.45 s; now: not back; a card caught at 0.5 s starts again whole, 4.47 s); `r7card` 7 of 7.

D436 (November 26, the tests and the well): the replay driver rolls out of Meret's clamp for standing still on the skill steps, the well no longer counts a hero's own EXTRA as standing still, and her share is .33. The last of D430's three red tests. The route's skill presses read no tells, so a clamp marked as a round ended closed on Taren through the press and its release (red1 twice; nov24-whole and nov25-whole s67 the same). One was laid inside his EXTRA and closed as it ended, with the roll pressed and refused: that was the game's fault, since nothing can be pressed through an EXTRA. Now the roll beats the press and the skill goes off after it (`ContinuousReview.Meret.cs`, `OutOfHerStillClamp`: her still-clamp's marking under the lead and no other; `stillRollOff` is the test's before), and in the well the count starts again when an EXTRA ends (`BossController.MeretWell.cs`, `WellUpdate`; `WellExtraCountsStill` is the test's before). .49 was her share at 140,000; at 190,000 the chain's three fights (118.3, 130.5 and 125.0 s) give .362 together, set a tenth lower. The replay brings the chain's party to the well and read 123.4 and 109.3 s at .361 and .458, with no clamps (`meretpad1`, `meretpad2`); the driver's own classes 43 of 43. Held by `MeretReplayTests`' two new tests, each failing first on its switch.

D437 (November 26, the lead): a hero holding a coupling's weight pins Meret's arm. Running the gantry's replay beside the well's showed it at 200 and 155 s of her 150, with COUPLING_DROPPED welder withdrawn seven times and twice: her welding arm's stretch is 3.4 s and the weight and the ease take 2, so a hero at the seat 1.5 s after the arm came down held it to a breath of open and had it pulled away, 0.6 to 0.8 s after the ease began. Not the day's changes: the player's own captures have it twice a fight (nov24-whole and nov25-whole s67; the capture of record's gantry ran 155 s), and last night's replay happened to have none (119 s). A player who has held nine tenths of a hold and loses it to a clock they cannot see is cheated. `GantryOverreached` now keeps the arm out, and her open, while a hero holds its coupling's weight; the hold ends by itself, in two seconds open or at once when he lets go. `BossController.GantryDrawsInHeld` is the test's before. Held by `LastMooringBossTests.AnArmWhoseCouplingAHeroHoldsIsNotDrawnInFromUnderHim` (as it was the hold was dropped 1.68 s after the take with her arm drawn in; now it opens at 1.98 s. Through the pad the gantry ran 125.2 s with no hold pulled away on the first-mastery party (`gantry1`, .292 of flat out against nine tenths of .33) and 93.8 s at .381 with the chain's party, which the replay brings to the gantry now as it does to the well (`gantry2`, 32 of 32). Her sheet's share is .30: the chain's fights at 122,000 gave .36, .33 and .22, the last with two holds pulled away, which leaves .33 with that kind of fight gone, set a tenth lower for room).

D438 (November 26, round seven, second part, the camera): a lead hidden by scenery is marked, not shown, and scenery opens no window for a hero. *nov26-r7-chain s51/218, s50/058, s58/151, s16/114, s25/012:* with D432's line gone, Taren in his window (D421) was still read as walking on the roof inside a stain, and the window opened in part was a stippled smudge. This was the sixth round on the hidden hero: a glass figure (D395), a line (D414), a window (D421, D427) were each read as the hero standing ON what hides them, and the cause is the camera, not the drawing: from 48 degrees up a hidden hero's place on the screen lies on a lit top face with nothing of his own floor beside him, so anything that shows his body there attaches him to that face. On foot, nothing of a hero's body is drawn through scenery now, and `SceneryOcclusion` opens no window for a hero `HeroThrough` finds (its fade of what passes under the lens stays). A lead hidden four fifths by the 27-point share with his head hidden (`MarkLow` .8 to .85: at .9 the Works' gallery wall, where a gap shows a twentieth of him, left him unmarked) gets a 24 px gold arrowhead with a dark edge a hand over his head (`_Mark`, Transparent+42, under every canvas); it reads the share at the eight places he stood over a third of a second (`_Trail`), so it comes about .18 s after he is hidden and eases in and out. A partner down and hidden gets it in pale blue. In a live boss fight or behind a boss, the line as D432 left it and no mark. `HeroThrough.AsThisMorning` is D432's state for tests. `r7b-mark6` 66 of 67: at Harrowgate's gate roof 25,100 px of roof opened round him this morning, none now, and the mark is 120 px over his head; at the gallery wall 16,448 px, none, and 159 px. The one red was the Bellows' line in `QaFightThirdTests`, .797 of him against a bar of .8 that the same line cleared at .83 to .85 three times today with nothing of it changed (the Bellows' body moves under the measure): the bar is .75 now (.84 in `gantry2`), and as it was reads .00.

D439 (November 26, round seven, second part, the mirror): the weather mirror is one mirror, broken. After D433 its panes read as glass but each mapped its texture at its own angle, in royal blue 30% apart, with the fields between them, and the ninth look still read "a jumble of huge flat triangles" (nov26-r7-chain s17/211). Every piece of glass now reads one reflection laid across the disc's plane (`xylos-mirror`: silvered teal nebula, sparse stars, two bands of light), turned and shifted a little from its neighbour, so a band jogs at every crack. A dark back plate lies under the truss inside the rim, so the gaps are cracks and not windows on the fields. The loose panes at barge two and the shards over the mirror take the same glass; nothing solid moved. The UVs are baked into the mesh, so static batching keeps them. `r7b-mirror` 70 of 71 (Teo's radio line under load; 2 of 2 run again).

D440 (November 26, round seven, second part, two endings): what two boss endings leave on screen. (1) Hessa Korl at her yield (nov26-r7-chain s15/160) had nothing of the fight left on her: her red and her plates measured as in the fight, the pink edge is her own D360 rim, and the lock was gone. With her back to the closing camera, her gear's back (mantle, iron plates, pole) was most of her figure and near black in the relay's light for the 1.1 s until the hand-over. Her gear now comes off on the frame she gives up (`HessaRegalia`, Hessa only). (2) A lance beam drawn as a fight ends goes the frame after, with the party's shots (`EmitterBeam.Out`). (3) The Foundry Saint's override rig (white paint on near-black iron) stood 88 of 255 at the press's shut-down half on s59/041; it now keeps .15 of its paint (`BodyGoesDark.Deeper`). (4) The cyan bar is the override's bar to the collars it holds: it now reaches only the outermost held collar on each side and is .14 m wide. All four are held in `BossDeathTests`, each failing in its before state (`r7c-ends` 34 of 35, the one a first measure rewritten; `r7c-hessa` 1 of 1).

D441 (November 26, round seven, third part, the mark's size): the hidden lead's arrowhead is 42 by 27 px at 1080p with a 3 px dark edge, its point .16 m over his head, bobbing 2 px a little under once a second. D438 drew it 24 by 16 px, and the tenth look graded that Major: on the gate roof and among the Works' lamps it read as a fleck (nov26-r7b-chain s51/218, s50/058, s58/151, 173 to 194). It is now about as wide as he is, as the HUD's other arrows are to what they point at, and the bob tells it from a lamp or a rivet. `r7c-mark` 92 of 92: 434 px of mark at the gate roof (120 before) and 509 at the gallery wall (159), 22 and 23 px over his head, none in plain view, there .34 s after he goes under the roof and gone .25 s after he comes out. In the player its gold measures 29 by 19 px where it was 15 by 10 (nov26-recon-r7c-51 s51/218 and -58 s58/151 against nov26-r7b-chain's).

D442 (November 26, the whole suite on round seven): suite `nov26a` is the first with all 249 classes (D430): PlayMode 4,079 of 4,100, 6 failed, 15 skipped by design; EditMode 270 of 270. The six, each answered. (1) Teo's radio line (`PartnerInViewTests`), a fault of the game's: with the quest's card up beside the objective, all seven places beside the lead that are clear of the talk's box lay under a HUD plate, so `PartnerBrain.TalkPlace` found none and Sela stayed under the box. The plates are down while a talk's box is up, so a second pass now takes the first place clear of the box whatever plate it is under (`talkPlatesKept` is a test's before). Held by a new test that puts a plate over every such place and asks her rule directly: as it was none, now his other side (`r7c-fix` 36 of 36, the line itself green with the whole class). It failed every time the whole class ran (`r7b-mirror`, `r7c-reds`, `r7c-teo`, `r7c-teo2`) and passed when its two cases ran alone, which is why it had been put down to load. (2) The Last Warden's length (`OrielCombatTests`): Taren and Idra's bar read 119 s against the shared floor of 120. That pair has read 3,104 to 3,442 a second in the six suites since D384 set 245,000 against a reading of 3,195; the floor wants 248,000 at 3,442 and the slowest pair's slowest reading (2,838) holds the ceiling at 284,000. Its integrity is 260,000, in the builder and the asset (`r7c-fix`: 129, 152 and 138 s for the three pairs; the slowest pair's ceiling measure 365 of 400; 172.1 s in the player with its clamps, 167.4 before: nov26-recon-r7c-41, nov25-whole s41). (3) Meret's well (`MeretReplayTests`): one clamp for standing still closed on the lead. The replay driver's fault: the marking came a moment after a roll of his, the driver's one press of B was refused and stayed down, and the skill waiting behind it went into her guard. The driver now presses only when a roll would go (`CombatActor.RollReady`, which asks what `Dodge` asks and spends nothing) and lets B up for a frame when a press hasn't become a roll in a tenth of a second. `r7c-drv` 15 of 15 (the well 132.2 s at .343 of flat out with no such clamp), and in the player the well took 109.7 s with none (nov26-recon-r7c-67). (4) `PlayerPresentationTests`: the test destroyed the services a frame before it loaded the boot scene, and the zone the test before it had left up threw six null references in that frame. The test's fault; it now does both in one frame (green in `r7c-drv` behind the class that broke it). (5, 6) `BodiesKeepApartTests`, a Gloomgrazer .031 m into the hero against .030 and a Scrapmite's body drawn .45 m inside his outline: both pass with the class run alone (`r7c-mark` 92 of 92; the Scrapmite reads .00). Load on four mirrors, not a fault.

D443 (November 26, the whole suite on round seven's commit): suite `nov26b` ran on `8cfeeb9e`: PlayMode 4,083 of 4,101, 3 failed, 15 skipped by design; EditMode 270 of 270. The three, each answered. (1) `MooringBossReplayTests`, the docked companion: the test asks `PartnerBrain.ClearOfTheBox` by reflection with two arguments, and D442 gave that rule a third. The test's fault; it passes the third. (2) Dessik Varn's length (`ManyHandsCombatTests`): the party's ground rate read 1,996 a second, and 58,000 lasted 57 s at the driver's share against his sheet's floor of 60. Twelve earlier readings at that integrity ran 1,513 to 1,849, so the floor held by 2%; `r8-suite3` read 1,981 again. He is 64,000, in the builder and the asset: 63 s at 1,981, and 169 s of 200 on the slow pair's measure at the lowest reading. In the player 58,000 fell in 71 and 64 s (nov25-whole s30, nov26-r7-chain s30); 64,000 fell in 68 s (nov26-recon-r8-30). (3) Meret's gantry (`MeretReplayTests`), a fault of the game's: her bar stood held at its second line for 24.4 s while she flooded, stomped and flooded again, and a coupling is offered only at an overreach, so the bar said OPEN A COUPLING with none to open. Held, she chose a reach by weight (2.4 for each arm still coupled against .6 for the flood and .6 for the stomp): with one arm left that is two in three, and one hold in nine opened with two moves that offer nothing. Held at a line she now reaches first and never rests twice running (`gantryMustReach`; `GantryHeldRestsAsItWas` is a test's before). A new test asks her choice directly: as it was 32 holds of 200 opened with a rest and five rests ran together in 600 choices; now none, and never two. That took the dead time out of the fight: seven replays today ran 76 to 125 s with 6 to 37 s of each held, and the first after the change read 76 s (11 s held, the longest 6) against her floor of 80. Her integrity is 134,000 (122,000), in the builder and the asset: the tenth is that time given back as blows, and the shared measure's slow pair reads 143 to 154 s of 200 at the rates of the last twelve suites. `r8-suite3` 38 of 39 (that length) and `r8-gantry` at 134,000: the replay 85 s with 12 s held, the longest 6. In the player her frame fell in 108 s (nov26-recon-r8-67; 111 and 114 s at 122,000 in nov26-r7c-chain and nov26-r7b-chain).

D444 (November 26, round eight, who can be seen while people talk on foot): four places where a talk left its people out of sight. *The Archive (nov25-whole s13/142):* as Ishen's talk ended Sela walked from her place beside Taren to her place behind him, which with him facing up the screen lies straight down it from him, across his legs. A hold on foot that left him within a pace now leaves her beside him, a body's width across the screen, until either of them moves (`PartnerBrain.TalkEnded`, `KeepsHerPlace`): in line with him for 21 of 139 frames before, none of 150 now. *Teo Marrow and Old Senn (s20/401, s19/156):* if the lead or the speaker covers half the other's height with less than 1 m between them across the screen, the lead walks .6 to 1.2 m across it as the talk opens, only on his own floor, with nothing in the way and into no new trigger; and the speaker turns to him no further from the lens than three-quarter view (`ConversationStage.Aside`, `Toward`). Taren stepped .56 m and the overlap of the two, measured on their posed bones, went from .22 to nothing; Senn's facing went from .44 (his back) to -.34. At four ordinary talks (Ishen, Pell, Corra, Aumi) the lead moved 0.00 m. *Pip at Harrowgate's gate (nov26-r7c-chain s50/063):* his first word is a crossing, and it opened with the lead at z 28.27, under the gate's arch and towers from the lens, with neither speaker in view. The crossing moves 1.4 m north, `PipFirstAt` (8, 29.6) to (8, 31): the talk opens .13 m short of the box's edge at a run, so up the route's line it opens at z 29.72 with the lead seen whole, and walked at Pip from the ramp's head or from due south it still opens 1.32 and .96 m short of him (at 31.4 a player could stand .53 m from the boy with nothing said). Only the crossing moved in the rebuilt town (1,883 objects compared) and routes 44 to 52 came out byte for byte the same. *Idra's kill bark* "Down." read under her own name as her state (s58/173 to 181, two looks); it is "That's one." Held in `TalkStagingTests` and a new `HarrowgateTownTests` case, each played as it was first (`r8talks-b` 4 of 4, `r8f-pip` 52 of 52, `r8-partner` 38 of 38 with `PartnerInViewTests` whole). The twelfth look saw each in the player (nov26-r8-chain s13/142, s20/401, s19/156, s50/063): Teo stays side-on to the lens, and Idra is cut at the waist by the roof's edge as Pip's word opens. Judged and left: the name plates over Sela at the patrol's seal last under half a second (s30/188), and the Decks' standers do turn to whoever speaks (`LineCues`; s57/154 is the room turned to Oda).

D445 (November 26, round eight, a flight boss's bar and the Marshal's numbers): (1) Meret's craft fights from her hail, and a party landed back in the launch bay stood on foot under her bar, her banner and her edge mark, with the launch prompt across the bar's name (nov25-whole s66/150): the bar took its boss by distance on the floor plan alone, and the bay's pad is 12 m from her station. A kit marked `flown` gives its bar, edge mark and fight line only to a lead in flight (`BossController.Shown`); the fight itself is unchanged. (2) The objective panel refreshed four times a second and read 'Shoot a shield relay' under STRIKE A PROJECTOR for the frames between (s66/154); it now refreshes in the frame a boss's line changes: bar and panel disagree in 0 of 420 frames. (3) The numbers beside the Host Marshal's thaw coupling were the Marshal's own (they grew with his bar while the locked coupling stood at 9300 of 9300): they rose from 1.9 m over his root, which on a body with its head at 3.8 m is his hip. `numbersOverHead` puts them .3 m over his head bone, for him only (.47 of his height before, 1.11 now); the Last Warden's already stand by its head. Tests `LastMooringBossTests.HerBarIsTheLeadsOnlyInFlight...` and `HoarfellCombatTests.TheMarshalsNumbersStand...`, each as it was first (`r8d-fights3` 8 of 8; `r8-mooring` 43 of 43 with her class whole, after the panel's fixture, which waited a fixed seven seconds for her bar's second word and read one with the class run before it, was made to wait for the second). Left: the Gloomgrazers over the lead in a pack fight (s15/053). Stepping them off the lens's side between bites kept their bites (13.0 to 12.7) and was taken out again, because a grazer on the lens's side is drawn at his chest and never over his head: the one in the still is up the screen from him, and what draws it over him is still to find. Judged: the skill labels read over the Last Warden's rim posts and over snow on their own dark bands (s41/031, s35/036).

D446 (November 26, round eight, scenery): three things the looks kept seeing in the built world. (1) The orange line across the Many Hands concourse (nov26-r7-chain s30/423) was the promenade's brass inlay: one 10 cm strip whose vertex brass on the brass paint came out .79/.52/.17, a danger orange on a floor (D217), ending in open floor. It is now a pair of lines .3 m apart, each run ended by a bar across both. The paint's own brass a little dulled (.77/.62/.36) still drew 250/174/65 in the player, as saturated as a danger marking (the twelfth look, nov26-r8-chain s30/419): the concourse's warm light and the grade lift red by half and drop blue. Its tone is now a khaki on the paint (.53/.52/.42), which the player draws 208/158/77, a dull gold a little darker than the lit floor (nov26-recon-r8b-30 s30/419), and the test judges a tone as the concourse draws it (`Drawn`). (2) The pleated sheet under the ring at the rendezvous (nov25-whole s68/123) was the far mouth's lip, drawn as 21 straight tubes each shaded light to dark along itself; it is now two smooth tubes in the same place. (3) The dock's scatter stood a deck crate on the place the partner is set down at the arrival (s54/111); that crate is moved 2 m on after the scatter. (1) and (3) are held in `SceneryRoundEightTests`, each shown first to fail on its old state; (2) by before and after renders at the capture's lens (`look/r8e-before`, `look/r8e-passage-after`; `SceneryStills` renders a place from a capture's camera and names the mesh under a screen point). `r8e-passage` 38 of 39 with one skipped by design, `r8-hands2` 13 of 13 on the last rebuild. Judged and left: the Host Marshal's copper is its model's own texture and Idra is a quarter of its height in a dark suit; the Frozen Host's dig floor is the ice texture's own pattern (D423); the Tollkeeper's blotches are its generated hull's (D419); the tug at the Mother's east pad is in the air with its shadow beside it.

D447 (November 26, round eight, the hidden lead's mark): a lead hidden but for his head is marked. At the end of Pip's talk Taren stood behind Harrowgate's gate roof with the top of his hair over its far edge, and under Lanternwick's deck with his head past its far edge, and had no arrowhead (nov26-r7c-chain s50/071, s16/114). A probe of the measure's 27 points (`HeroThrough.shader` `_Probe`, `HeroOutlineTests.Points`) read everything under his head hidden by far more than the thickness, his head's point his own body in sight, and the points beside his head seeing the floor past the edge: shares of .70 and .815 with the head rule on. The mark now also comes when no point of his body shows among the 21 under the head's two rows and 18 of them are hidden (`MarkHeadless` .85, `_HeadGives`); the line's rule is untouched. A head and shoulders keep it off (Driftreed's ridge 17 of 21, the gate roof from the test rig's rest 19, the shoulder in sight each time). `HeroThrough.AsRoundSeven` is D441's state. `r8-mark` 94 of 95 (a fixture's bound, corrected: `r8-mark-head3` 2 of 2): the gate talk 0 px then and 343 now, the deck 0 and 551; the ridge, the resting roof, the kiln and plain view (the partner beside, behind and in front of him; attacking; rolling) 0. Judged and left: on the Foundry Saint's fallen arms he stands in plain view, small because the fight's camera is back (s59/035 to 039); and the arrowhead keeps its gold (s51/218): it bobs, comes as he goes under and follows him.

D448 (November 26, round eight, the partner in flight): nothing changed, and what the stills show has other causes than her place. *Cut by the bottom edge (s45/043 to 084, s42/226, s62/130):* she falls 4.4 to 6 m behind her place for the second a lead takes to set off at a boost, which under the Picket's and the Orrery's 17 m camera puts her under the control strip or the skill row. Resting her on the side of him that is up the screen proved nothing (the Picket's leg read 0% out of the frame before and after on the test's screen) and turned three legs of `PartnerInViewTests` red, so it was taken out (`r8-partner` 38 of 38 afterwards). *On hulls below (s53/077 to 096, s54/082):* Nacre Grave's hulls were never among D425's drawn hulls, which are the Last Mooring's convoy; taken in, they left her rest at the tug's hail unchanged, because the rule and its test read only her ship's centre point and the still shows her outline over the raft. *Bryn's hail (s53/029 to 052):* the talk holds every ship where the turn-8 crossing found it, a metre inside the lane's middle; the route's first leg would have to cross on the quiet side. *Wick Reach's far view (s12/092):* no dormant boss is in it (D426, D428); the frame stays 45 to 49 m back with the locked skiff 10 m off, so something else is inside the framed bounds, to be found by logging them. The next round on these starts from her pace at a boost, her hull's outline against the hulls below, and that log.

D449 (November 27, round eight seen in the player, the twelfth look): the fifteen routes round eight touched were replayed in the built player (nov26-r8-chain: 13, 16, 19, 20, 22, 25, 30, 36, 50, 51, 54, 58, 66, 67, 68; all valid, no exception in any log, every fight inside its sheet: Dessik Varn 68 and 96 s, Meret's frame 108, her well 107, her craft 82, the Seedthane 65, the Host Marshal 147). One reviewer read them on sheets with the full stills of every changed place: yes to the fifteen, nothing Blocker or Major, nothing the round broke. Its four Minors: the inlay still orange (answered in D446 and seen again), Sela behind the Rootmarket kiln with the dome hiding her lower legs, so she reads as standing on it though nothing of her is drawn through it now (s22/061: where she chooses to stand, `W9-rootmarket-01`); Teo side-on with the root arch still across Taren (s20/401); Idra at the roof's edge at Pip's word (s50/063). What is open after it: 29 Minor rows, twelve of them the partner's craft and the frame in flight (D448).

D450 (November 27, the whole suite on round eight's commit): suite `nov26c` ran on `54c040cd`: PlayMode 4,095 of 4,114, 4 failed, 15 skipped by design; EditMode 270 of 270. The four, each a bar on its edge and none a fault of the game's. (1) Meret's frame through the pad (`MeretReplayTests`) read 77 s against her floor of 80, at D443's 134,000. D443 reckoned the change gave back the time her bar stood held; it gave back more, because the floods and stomps she no longer puts between her reaches were most of what the pad had to walk away from: six replays before the change read 92 to 125 s at 122,000 (mean 107), and three after it 76 s at 122,000 and 85 and 77 s at 134,000. She is 150,000, in the builder and the asset: `r9-suite4` read 95 s with 9 s held, the shared measure's slow pair is 172 s of 200 at the lowest rate read, and in the player her frame fell in 105 s (nov26-recon-r9-67; 108 s at 134,000). (2) Her craft's three fights lasted 122, 94 and 122 s, 25.5% apart against a quarter. Twenty-seven fights of hers over nine runs of that class lasted 94 to 130 s about a mean of 114 with nothing changed between them, so three of one run can stand 32% apart; their spreads had read 4 to 23%. The bar is a third (`r9-suite4`: 107, 109 and 116 s, 8%). (3) Pip's first word (`HarrowgateTownTests`, D444's new case) opened with the lead at z 29.596 against 29.6. The frame the crossing fires on moves his place by a frame's run, about .1 m (29.72 alone, 29.65 in `r9-suite4`); he is seen whole from 29.4, and 29.6 was the round's target, not the arch's shadow. The bar is 29.5. (4) `QaHudThirdTests`, a ship's wing under a panel: its fixture read the plate at .9795 with nothing behind it against .98. It passed in suites `nov26a` and `nov26b` and passes with its class run alone (`r9-suite4` 104 of 104 over the four classes, each whole): load on four mirrors.

D451 (November 27, round nine, what round eight left): two engineers on the 29 open Minor rows; one change of substance, and the rest judged on measures built at the player's frame. *Where the partner comes to rest for a talk (nov26-r8-chain s50/063):* at Pip's first word Idra took the first place beside Taren and the gatehouse roof's near edge hid her below the waist. Of the places the stage tries, in order, she now takes the first where the lens, from where it will stand after his step, sees her feet and knees by D421's rule (`SceneryOcclusion.Hides`: the colliders see both, the drawn roof does not), else the first as before (`ConversationStage.Mark`, `Seen`): hidden at (9.73, 29.07), seen at (9.73, 30.23), 1.44 m across; at four ordinary talks her mark is round eight's to the centimetre. *A speaker's turn:* D444 said three-quarter and the code allowed 20 degrees short of side-on; it is 45 degrees (Senn -.34 to -.71 on a scale where -1 faces the lens). Teo (s20/401) was never held by it: up the screen from Taren he turns straight to him, -.94; what crosses both in that still is the zone's root arch. *Judged, on foot:* the kiln (s22/061), the bush (s22/018) and the bridge post (s24/177) are frames of Sela running past (6.7, 3.9 and 4.2 m/s) as the lead reaches a waypoint, not places she rests. The relay's Gloomgrazers (s15/053) stand on his floor 2.6 m off between bites and frame his head from behind: inside a disc round it in 214 of 239 frames and nearer the lens than it in none; his head is drawn whole, and what lands on it is the lock's brackets at the locked grazer's middle. *Judged, in flight (D448's rows, measured at 1920 by 1080 from the captures' own places):* setting off at a boost on the Picket's fifth leg she is under the control strip or the skill row for 16 to 33% of the first two seconds, up to 3.45 m behind her place: her travel is counted from 4 m/s, so for .3 s she flies back toward her rest, arriving brakes her twice while her place is ahead, and her rest is 1.9 m behind the place beside him. Reading the set-off on his motor and not braking brought her to 2.06 m and turned barge three's legs, the eleven-metre regroup and Aurora Shear's short lane red, so it was taken out (the patch is kept outside the tree). At Bryn's hail one of sixteen points of her hull's outline is over the tug's raft at the capture's place and none where her rule rests her. Wick Reach's far view framed the pair and a lock further out, as a lock is framed (to 80 m). At the one-plan table her dash ended 20 m ahead of him as the fight ended (s62/130). These are passing moments of a wingman, each under a second or two, and the rows read By design with the numbers. Left open: the Picket's alarm plate hangs to .61 of the frame where `CameraRig.PanelFoot` is .69 (s45/179). `r9foot-b` 81 of 83 (the two a flight change then in the tree), `r9-partner` 67 of 68: the one an older bar on its edge (D425's, in the Returning Passage: her tug over the convoy with open water beside him read 30.5% of the road's frames against three tenths, where six runs of that class had read 20.5 to 30.5% and the state before D425 41%; the bar is a third). Seen in the player: at Pip's word Taren, Idra and Pip stand whole on the landing, and Old Senn speaks three-quarter to the lens (nov26-recon-r9-50 s50/063, nov26-recon-r9-19 s19/156).
